P·L COMMENTARIA IN CANTICA 112

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 112 ·

COMMENTARIA IN CANTICA

PL 112, coll. 1089–1166  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  First English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Rabanus Maurus

c. 780 – 856

Pupil of Alcuin, abbot of Fulda and archbishop of Mainz, and the most prolific compiler of the Carolingian renewal. His biblical commentaries and the encyclopaedic De rerum naturis transmitted patristic learning to the medieval schools; later ages called him praeceptor Germaniae.

Commentaria in Cantica

  • 1089

1089ADomino reverendissimo et in cultu verae religionis strenuosissimo LUDOVICO regi RABANUS devotus servulus in Christo aeternam optat salutem.

Nuper quando ad vos in cellula monasterii nostri, quae vocatur Ratestorph, vocatus veni, et sermo fuit inter nos de Scripturis sacris, persuadere mihi dignati estis ut cantica quae in matutinis laudibus sancta psallit Ecclesia vobis allegorico sensu exponerem; ad quod tunc respondi id me jam factum habere in opusculis nostris, quae in tractatu divinorum librorum, quantum potui, confeci, nec modo necesse esse id denuo repetere; sed cum ad monasterium reversus essem et quaesissem utrum in omnia illa cantica expositionem jam factam haberem, comperi quod in illa quae de prophetis sunt, Isaia videlicet et Habacuc, 1089Bnecdum manum miserim. Unde cogitavi quod haec simul cum caeteris in unum volumen coaptarem, et vobis ad legendum maxime in istis diebus Quadragesimae transmitterem. Feci siquidem haec et utramque editionem simul posui; illam videlicet juxta quam Romana psallit Ecclesia, et hanc quam ex Hebraica veritate translatam caeterae Occidentis ex maxima parte tenent Ecclesiae, utriusque editionis, ubi inter se discrepare videbantur, sensum exponens, et juxta id quod in sanctorum Patrum dictis jam expositum inveni, vel per me divina gratia opitulante investigare potui, opus consummavi, petens lectorem ut si quid sibi in eo displiceat, non cito reprehendat, sed prius consideret utrum majorum sensui concordet et veritati contrarium non sit: sicque devotionem mentis 1089Cmeae magis quam eloquentiam verborum perpendens aequo judicio de illo judicet, sciens scriptum: In qua mensura mensi fueritis remetietur vobis (Matth. VII). Si quem autem movet quod ipsa cantica non eo modo quo ab auctoribus suis condita sunt ordinem teneant, sed ea quae postea edita sunt priore loco ponantur, quia canticum Exodi et canticum Deuteronomii ante caetera cantica per legislatorem edita sunt, agnoscat hoc non frustra factum, sed decenti ordine et secundum rationem actum esse, ut mysteriis et institutionibus per omnia convenirent divinis. Nam in Dominica die, quae per omnia honorabilis et digna laudibus exstat, laudes trium puerorum in Ecclesia canuntur. In quibus omnes creaturae ad laudandum et benedicendum Creatorem suum provocantur. In secunda vero feria canticum Isaiae, in quo adventus Salvatoris et 1090Abaptismi sacramentum praedicatur, cantari decretum est, quia haec sunt initia salutis nostrae. In tertia quoque feria canticum sive oratio Ezechiae psallitur, in quo de infirmitate sua convalescens gratiarum actiones pro salute sua Deo referebat, admonens nos ut ipsi post perceptam gratiam Dei et ablutionem sacri baptismatis a mortis vinculis absoluti, per omne tempus vitae nostrae Deo gratias agere studeamus. In quarta autem feria canticum Annae prophetissae canitur, in quo Judaicae perfidiae expulsio denotatur, et electio Ecclesiae de gentibus demonstratur. Nam in quinta feria merito canticum Israelitarum canitur, quod post paschae celebrationem educti ex Egypto, et per mare Rubrum sicco vestigio translati cecinerunt. Nam et in ipsa feria Salvator noster pascha 1090Btypicum cum discipulis suis celebrans, pascha mysticum continuatim in sacramento corporis et sanguinis sui et in immolatione illius agni qui tollit peccata mundi, consecravit. Ideo consequenter in sexta feria canticum Habacuc prophetae, in quo sacramenta Dominicae passionis continentur et resurrectionis atque ascensionis ejus in coelos mysteria declarantur, congrue in Ecclesia psallitur, ut omnibus innotescat, quomodo in sexta feria, in qua primus homo conditus denuo est, humanum genus ad vitam aeternam per Christum restauratum sit. Sabbato quoque bene et congrue canticum Deuteronomii canendum constitutum est, in quo beneficia Dei erga priorem populum demonstrantur, et quam ingrate idem populus ea susceperit, hisque abusus sit, certissime exprimitur; 1090Cquia ea die antiquitus constitutum est Deuteronomium legis in synagoga recitari, ne oblivio praeceptorum Dei in populo fieret, sed magis incitarentur ad observationem praeceptorum illius. Unde in Actibus apostolorum Jacobo dicente ita scriptum legimus: Moyses enim a temporibus antiquis habet in singulis civitatibus, qui eum praedicent in synagogis, ubi per omne sabbatum legitur (Act. XV); nos ergo similiter eodem carmine spiritualiter instruimur, ut non simus ingrati beneficiorum Dei, sed magis per omne tempus vitae nostrae laudibus ejus insistamus, donec veniat illud sabbatum, in quo perpetualiter cum Christo requiem possideamus aeternam. Cantica vero de Evangelio sumpta apte et convenienter quotidie in Ecclesia decantari a magistris Ecclesiae decretum est, hoc est, ut canticum Zachariae prophetae, ubi exortus 1091Alucis in tenebris memoratur, in matutinis laudibus decantetur, et canticum Mariae Dei genitricis, in quo per brachium Domini dispersio superborum et potentium depositio humiliumque exaltatio praedicatur, recte in vespertino officio novissime canitur. Quia in vespere mundi, hoc est, in fine saeculi per adventum Salvatoris, superbi in ignem projicientur inferni, et humiles in gloriam exaltabuntur aeternam. Haec ergo juxta id quod sensimus et a magistris traditum accepimus, stylo protulimus, non praejudicantes his qui melius sapiunt ac melius sermonibus proferre norunt. Tu autem, sapientissime rex, in omnibus bene eruditus, ea quae tibi a devoto famulo collata sunt, benigna mente suscipe, illisque utere secundum quod perspexeris tibi tuisque prodesse. Ego autem 1091Bquidquid servitii possum tibi libenter impendam, insuper et ipse impendar, Deum orando atque obsecrando pro salute tua, et pro stabilitate regni tui, ut hic diu sospes vivas, et post terminum praesentis vitae in coelesti regno vita fruaris aeterna. Divinitas Domini nostri Jesu Christi hic et ubique, nunc et semper sanum et incolumem te conservare dignetur.

Commentaries on the Canticles

  • 1089

1089ATo the most reverend lord, and most vigorous in the cultivation of true religion, King LOUIS, RABANUS, his devoted little servant in Christ, wishes eternal salvation.

Lately, when I came to you, having been summoned to the cell of our monastery which is called Ratestorph, and there was talk between us concerning the sacred Scriptures, you deigned to urge upon me that I should expound for you, in the allegorical sense, the canticles which holy Church sings at the morning lauds. To this I then replied that I had already done this in our little works, which, as far as I was able, I composed in a treatment of the divine books, and that there was no need now to repeat it afresh. But when I had returned to the monastery and had inquired whether I had already made an exposition upon all those canticles, I found that upon those which are from the prophets — namely Isaiah and Habakkuk — 1089BI had not yet set my hand. Whence I thought that I would fit these together with the rest into one volume, and would send them to you to read, especially in these days of Lent. And so I have done this, and have set both editions down together: that one, namely, according to which the Roman Church sings, and this one, translated from the Hebrew verity, which the other Churches of the West for the most part hold; expounding the sense of both editions where they seemed to differ between themselves; and, according to what I found already expounded in the sayings of the holy Fathers, or was able to investigate by myself with divine grace assisting, I have completed the work, asking the reader that, if anything in it displease him, he should not be quick to find fault, but should first consider whether it accords with the sense of our elders and is not contrary to the truth; and that so, weighing the devotion of my mind 1089Crather than the eloquence of my words, he may judge concerning it with an even judgment, knowing what is written: With what measure ye shall have measured, it shall be measured back to you (Matth. VII). But if it moves anyone that these canticles do not keep the order in which they were composed by their own authors, but that those which were published later are set in the earlier place — since the canticle of Exodus and the canticle of Deuteronomy were published by the lawgiver before the other canticles — let him recognize that this was not done to no purpose, but was carried out in a fitting order and according to reason, so that they might agree in all things with the divine mysteries and institutions. For on the Lord's day, which stands forth honorable in all things and worthy of praises, the praises of the three boys are sung in the Church. In them all creatures are called forth to praise and bless their Creator. But on the second feria it has been decreed that the canticle of Isaiah be sung, in which the coming of the Savior and 1090Athe sacrament of baptism are proclaimed, since these are the beginnings of our salvation. On the third feria too the canticle, or prayer, of Hezekiah is sung, in which, recovering from his infirmity, he rendered thanksgivings to God for his own health, admonishing us that we ourselves, after we have received the grace of God and the washing of holy baptism and have been loosed from the bonds of death, should be zealous to give thanks to God through the whole time of our life. But on the fourth feria the canticle of Anna the prophetess is sung, in which the casting out of Jewish perfidy is denoted, and the election of the Church from among the Gentiles is shown. For on the fifth feria the canticle of the Israelites is deservedly sung, which, after the celebration of the Pasch, they sang when they had been led out of Egypt and carried over the Red Sea with dry foot. For on that same feria our Savior also, celebrating the typical Pasch 1090Bwith his disciples, consecrated without interruption the mystical Pasch in the sacrament of his body and blood, and in the immolation of that Lamb who taketh away the sins of the world. Therefore, consequently, on the sixth feria the canticle of Habakkuk the prophet — in which the sacraments of the Lord's passion are contained, and the mysteries of his resurrection and of his ascension into the heavens are declared — is fittingly sung in the Church, so that it may be made known to all how, on the sixth feria, on which the first man was formed anew, the human race was restored to eternal life through Christ. On the Sabbath too it has been well and fittingly appointed that the canticle of Deuteronomy be sung, in which the benefits of God toward the former people are shown, and how ungratefully that same people received them and abused them is most certainly expressed; 1090Cbecause on that day it was anciently appointed that the Deuteronomy of the law be recited in the synagogue, lest forgetfulness of God's precepts should arise among the people, but that they should rather be stirred up to the observance of his precepts. Whence in the Acts of the apostles we read it thus written, as James says: For Moses from ancient times hath in every city them that preach him in the synagogues, where he is read every sabbath (Act. XV); we therefore likewise are instructed spiritually by the same song, that we be not ungrateful for the benefits of God, but rather press on in his praises through the whole time of our life, until that sabbath come in which we may perpetually possess eternal rest with Christ. But the canticles taken from the Gospel have been decreed by the masters of the Church to be sung aptly and suitably every day in the Church — that is, that the canticle of Zachary the prophet, where the rising 1091Aof light in the darkness is recorded, be sung at the morning lauds, and that the canticle of Mary the mother of God, in which the scattering of the proud and the putting down of the mighty and the exaltation of the humble is proclaimed through the arm of the Lord, be rightly sung last in the evening office. Because in the evening of the world — that is, at the end of the age, through the coming of the Savior — the proud will be cast into the fire of hell, and the humble will be exalted into eternal glory. These things, therefore, according to what we have perceived and have received as handed down from the masters, we have set forth with the pen, without prejudging those who know better and know how to set things forth better in words. But do thou, most wise king, well instructed in all things, receive with kindly mind those things which have been gathered for thee by a devoted servant, and use them according as thou shalt see it to profit thee and thine. And I 1091Bwill gladly spend whatever service I can upon thee, and moreover will myself be spent, praying and beseeching God for thy salvation and for the stability of thy kingdom, that here thou mayest long live in safety, and that after the end of the present life thou mayest enjoy eternal life in the heavenly kingdom. May the divinity of our Lord Jesus Christ, here and everywhere, now and always, deign to preserve thee sound and unharmed.

INCIPIT CANTICUM ISALAE PROPHETAE.

Isaias propheta postquam de incarnatione Domini et de convocatione Ecclesiae atque destructione idololatriae mysticis disputaret eloquiis, ita ut de flore virgae, de radice Jesse ascendente, super quem septemplicis 1091CSpiritus gratia requiesceret primum narraret, postea de concordia et cohabitatione bestiarum cum pecudibus, hoc est, divitum cum pauperibus et ferocium atque superborum cum mansuetis et humilibus concordiam in Ecclesia praedicaret, et signum crucis undique gentes convocare ad unitatem fidei testaretur, ad extremum desolationem fluminis Aegypti, hoc est, errorem gentium, qui in tenebris infidelitatis habitabant, dissolvendum esse manifestavit, sicque Ecclesiae plebem, sive animam fidelem ad laudandum Deum provocat, quatenus ejus gratiae hoc deputet, quod ab inimicis liberata, et a filiis irae separata, filia reconciliationis et misericordiae sit effecta. Unde dicit:

« Confitebor tibi, Domine, quoniam iratus es 1091Dmihi. Conversus est furor tuus et consolatus es me. » Confessio ergo, sicut saepe diximus, aequivocum nomen est, et pro locorum diversitate suscipitur. Confitentur enim et qui peccata deplorant, id est, qui pro ipsis dignam poenitentiam agunt, et qui Domino gratias referunt confitentur: sed illud in lacrymis, istud in gaudio est: illud in afflictione, istud in sancta mentis alacritate noscitur constitutum. Unde hic profusa laetitia exsultat populus fidelis quamdam imaginem futuri saeculi designans, ubi voces istae continuae sunt et laudes Domini devota mente concelebrant. Grandis enim laetitia est peccatricis quondam animae, cum se recordatur a vinculis peccatorum et laqueis errorum per gratiam 1092Asacri baptismatis erutam et per renovationem Spiritus sancti in electorum sortem translatam spem habens vitae futurae et gaudii sempiterni. Sed unde haec gratia sibi provenerit ostendit dicens:

« Ecce Deus Salvator mens: fiducialiter agam, et non timebo. » Omnis enim nostrae redemptionis et salutis causa in incarnatione Jesu Christi Domini nostri consistit, quia non est aliud nomen super terram datum hominibus, testante Petro apostolo, in quo oporteat salvos fieri, de quo et Paulus ait: « Gratia enim sumus salvati (Ephes. II). » Et item: « Quis me, inquit, liberabit de corpore mortis hujus? Gratia Dei per Jesum Christum Dominum nostrum (Rom VII). » De quo rursum ait: « Gratias agimus Deo, qui dignos nos fecit in parte sortis 1092Bsanctorum in lumine, qui eripuit nos de potestate tenebrarum, et transtulit in regnum filii dilectionis suae, in quo habemus redemptionem et remissionem peccatorum, qui est imago Dei invisibilis, primogenitus omnis creaturae, quia in ipso condita sunt universa in coelis et in terra (Col. I). » In ipso quoque fiducialiter agit omnis qui recte credit in Domino et sperat in eo, sciens quia ipse potens est liberare et defendere eos qui in ejus protectione veraciter confidunt, et ob hoc non timet adversarium, quia nocere illi non potest, nisi permissus. Unde Psalmista ait: « Dominus mihi adjutor est, et ego videbo inimicos meos (Psal. CXVII). » Subauditur infirmos fieri, et impotentes, spiritales enim nequitias vult intelligi, quas in illo judicio dicit esse 1092Cvidendas, ubi jam diabolus cum caeteris ministris suis destructus atque damnatus humanis conspectibus apparebit.

« Quia fortitudo mea et laus mea Dominus, et factus est mihi in salutem. » Non potest impulsus cadere, cui auxilium Domini videtur fortitudo praestare; sed ne putaretur sola pericula declinare quem Domini dona servabant, adjecit, « factus est mihi in salutem, » quia per ipsum aeternam vitam suscipiunt, per quem et saeculi hujus ruinas evadere promerentur. Ipse enim est et fortitudo et laudatio aeterna beatorum. Audiat sceleratissima haeresis factum Dominum his qui salvantur, et quorum prius Dominus non erat, ut creationem in Scripturis atque facturam non semper conditionem eorum quae 1092Dnon erant sed interdum gratiam in eos qui meruerint sibi Deum fieri, intelligamus. Post haec Propheta sub figura exhortatur eos qui credunt in Deum, ut ejus doctrinam intente discere studeant, qui ejus sortiti sunt et fidem, et dicit:

« Haurietis aquas in gaudio de fontibus Salvatoris. » Quem supra Emmanuel, et Accelera spolia detrahere, Festina praedari, et reliquis appellaret nominibus, ne videretur alter esse praeter eum quem Gabriel Virgini nuntiavit dicens: « Vocabis nomen ejus Jesum. Ipse enim salvum faciet populum suum (Matth. I), » nunc vocat Salvatorem, et de ejus fontibus aquas praedicat hauriendas, nequaquam de aquis fluminis Aegypti, quae percussae sunt, neque de aquis fluminis 1093ARasni, sed de fontibus Jesu. Hoc enim Hebraeorum lingua Salvator exprimitur. Unde et ipse clamabat in Evangelio: « Qui sitit, veniat ad me, et bibat. Qui credit in me, sicut dicit Scriptura, flumina de ventre ejus fluent aquae vivae. Hoc autem, » inquit Evangelista, « dicebat de Spiritu sancto, quem credentes in eum accepturi erant (Joan. VII). » Et in alio ipse loquitur Evangelio: « Qui biberit de aqua quam ego dedero ei, non sitiet in aeternum, sed fiet in eo fons aquae salientis in vitam aeternam (Joan. IV). » Fontes Salvatoris doctrinam intelligamus evangelicam, de qua in septuagesimo septimo psalmo legimus: « In Ecclesiis benedicite Deo, Deum de fontibus Israel, » quam videlicet apostoli gentibus infuderunt, fons enim religionis inde per caeteras 1093Bnationes emanavit. Unde et sequitur:

« Dicetis in illa die. Confitemini Domino et invocate nomen ejus. Notas facite in populis adinventiones ejus, mementote quoniam excelsum est nomen ejus. Cantate Domini quoniam magnifice fecit. Annuntiate hoc in universa terra. » Adinventiones enim Domini praecepta sunt divina, quae ex consilio summae sapientiae solummodo sunt prolata, et salutifera eis omnino fiunt, qui ea factis adimplere student. Haec praecipiuntur ab apostolis et reliquis doctoribus de Israel his, qui de gentibus crediderunt, ut soli confiteantur Domino et, idolis derelictis, invocent nomen ejus, et cuncta ejus opera praedicent infidelibus, ut sciant quoniam ipse solus excelsus est, et « in nomine ejus omne genu flectitur 1093Ccoelestium, terrestrium et infernorum (Phil. II), » cui cantandum est, quod magnifice fecerit, et in omni orbe illius misericordia praedicetur. Huic etiam loco concordat initium centesimi quarti psalmi, ubi ita scriptum est, « Confitemini Domino et invocate nonem ejus, annuntiate inter gentes opera ejus. » In hoc ergo tribus constat partibus ordo dictorum. Primo dicit: « Confitemini Domino, » id est, laudate eum et bonis factis gratiam vobis divinitatis acquirite, quia id ipsum est laudare Dominum, et ore illi honorem deferre, et operibus ejus jussiones efficere. Illa enim confessio hic intelligenda est, quae Domini praeconia celebrare non desinit, quando et subsequentia tale votum actionemque declarant. Deinde Dominum commonet invocari, 1093Dut post ministerium sanctissimae laudis grates ei dignas solvamus. Ipse enim rite invocat Deum, qui magis actu quam voce clamat ad Deum. Unde Salvator in Evangelio ad superbos et inobedientes sibi ait: « Quid enim vocatis me, Domine, Domine, et non facitis quae dico? » (Luc. VI.) Et alibi: « Non omnis, inquit, qui dicit mihi, Domine, Domine, intrabit in regnum coelorum; sed qui facit voluntatem Patris mei, ipse intrabit in regnum coelorum (Matth. VII). » Tertio dicit: « Annuntiate inter gentes opera ejus, » quod bene potest gloriosis evangelistis et sanctissimis apostolis, et nunc sacerdotibus convenire, qui per gentes universas magnalia ejus annuntiare noscuntur.

1094A« Exsulta et lauda, habitatio Sion, quia magnus est in medio tui sanctus Israel. » Primum dicendum juxta litteram: O habitatio Sion, exsulta et lauda Deum tuum, quod qui tuus ante videbatur Deus et modicis Judaeae terrae claudebatur angustiis, nunc repleverit omnem terram scientia sua, et de morte consurgens regnet in gentibus, et ipsum nationes deprecentur atque adorent, ita duntaxat, ut adjiciat secundo manum tuam ad possidendum, quod reliquum est populi sui, et congregantes profugos Israel et dispersos Juda colligendos a quatuor plagis terrae, quia seminarium Evangelii per apostolos, qui ex Judaeis erant, processit a fontibus Israel, melius autem est, ut Sion, id est speculam in sublimibus collocatam interpretemur Ecclesiam, de qua 1094Bet quinquagesimus psalmus canit: « Benigne fac in bona voluntate tua, Sion, ut aedificentur muri Jerusalem. » Ut acceptabile fiat in ea Domino sacrificium justitiae, oblationes et holocausta, et vitulus, quem filio poenitenti pater clementissimus immolavit.

HERE BEGINS THE CANTICLE OF ISALAE THE PROPHET.

Isaiah the prophet, after he had disputed in mystical utterances concerning the incarnation of the Lord and concerning the calling together of the Church and the destruction of idolatry — so that first he told of the flower of the rod, ascending from the root of Jesse, upon which 1091Cthe grace of the sevenfold Spirit should rest; and afterward proclaimed the concord and the dwelling together of the beasts with the cattle, that is, of the rich with the poor, and the concord in the Church of the fierce and the proud with the meek and the humble; and testified that the sign of the cross calls the nations together from every side to the unity of the faith — at last made manifest that the desolation of the river of Egypt, that is, the error of the Gentiles who dwelt in the darkness of unbelief, was to be dissolved; and so he calls forth the people of the Church, or the faithful soul, to praise God, so far as she may reckon it to his grace that, freed from her enemies and separated from the children of wrath, she has been made a daughter of reconciliation and of mercy. Whence he says:

« I will confess to thee, O Lord, because thou wast angry 1091Dwith me. Thy fury is turned away, and thou hast comforted me. » Confession, therefore, as we have often said, is an equivocal word, and is taken according to the diversity of places. For both those confess who bewail their sins — that is, who do worthy penance for them — and those who render thanks to the Lord confess; but the one is in tears, the other in joy; the one is known to be set in affliction, the other in the holy eagerness of the mind. Whence here the faithful people exults with abundant gladness, marking out a certain image of the age to come, where these voices are unceasing and celebrate together the praises of the Lord with devoted mind. For great is the gladness of a soul once sinful, when it remembers that it has been snatched from the bonds of sins and the snares of errors through the grace 1092Aof holy baptism, and translated by the renewal of the Holy Spirit into the lot of the elect, having hope of the life to come and of everlasting joy. But whence this grace came to it he shows, saying:

« Behold, God the Saviour, mind: I will deal confidently, and I will not fear. » For the whole cause of our redemption and salvation consists in the incarnation of Jesus Christ our Lord, since there is no other name given to men upon the earth — Peter the apostle bearing witness — in which it behooves us to be saved; of whom Paul also says: « For by grace we are saved (Ephes. II). » And again: « Who, he says, shall deliver me from the body of this death? The grace of God through Jesus Christ our Lord (Rom VII). » Of whom again he says: « We give thanks to God, who hath made us worthy in the portion of the lot 1092Bof the saints in light, who hath delivered us from the power of darkness, and hath translated us into the kingdom of the Son of his love, in whom we have redemption and the remission of sins; who is the image of the invisible God, the firstborn of every creature, because in him all things were created in the heavens and on the earth (Col. I). » In him also everyone deals confidently who rightly believes in the Lord and hopes in him, knowing that he himself is able to deliver and defend those who truly trust in his protection; and on this account he does not fear the adversary, because he cannot harm him unless permitted. Whence the Psalmist says: « The Lord is a helper to me, and I shall look upon my enemies (Psal. CXVII). » It is to be understood that they are made weak and powerless; for he wishes spiritual wickednesses to be understood, which he says are to be 1092Clooked upon in that judgment, where the devil, with the rest of his ministers, already destroyed and damned, will appear to human sight.

« For the Lord is my strength and my praise, and he is become salvation to me. » He who is struck cannot fall, to whom the help of the Lord is seen to furnish strength; but lest he whom the Lord's gifts preserved should be thought only to escape dangers, he added, « he is become salvation to me, » because through him they receive eternal life, through whom they also merit to escape the ruins of this age. For he himself is both the strength and the eternal praise of the blessed. Let that most wicked heresy hear that the Lord was made so for those who are saved, and whose Lord he was not before — that we may understand that creation and making in the Scriptures is not always the founding of those things which 1092Dwere not, but sometimes grace toward those who have deserved that God become theirs. After these things the Prophet under a figure exhorts those who believe in God, that they should study to learn his doctrine attentively, who have obtained also his faith; and he says:

« You shall draw waters with joy out of the Saviour's fountains. » Whom above he had called Emmanuel, and Hasten to take away the spoils, Make speed to take prey, and by the remaining names, lest he should seem to be another beside him whom Gabriel announced to the Virgin, saying: « Thou shalt call his name Jesus. For he shall save his people (Matth. I), » him he now calls Saviour, and proclaims that waters are to be drawn from his fountains — by no means from the waters of the river of Egypt, which were smitten, nor from the waters of the river 1093Aof Rasni, but from the fountains of Jesus. For in the tongue of the Hebrews this expresses "Saviour." Whence he himself also cried out in the Gospel: « He that thirsteth, let him come to me and drink. He that believeth in me, as the Scripture saith, out of his belly shall flow rivers of living water. Now this, » says the Evangelist, « he said of the Holy Spirit, whom they that believed in him should receive (Joan. VII). » And in another Gospel he himself speaks: « He that shall drink of the water that I will give him shall not thirst for ever; but it shall become in him a fountain of water springing up into life everlasting (Joan. IV). » Let us understand the fountains of the Saviour to be the evangelical doctrine, of which we read in the seventy-seventh psalm: « In the churches bless ye God, God from the fountains of Israel » — that doctrine, namely, which the apostles poured into the Gentiles; for from thence the fountain of religion flowed forth through the remaining 1093Bnations. Whence there also follows:

« You shall say in that day. Confess ye to the Lord and call upon his name. Make his devices known among the peoples; remember that his name is exalted. Sing ye of the Lord, for he hath done magnificently. Declare this in the whole earth. » For the devices of the Lord are the divine precepts, which have been brought forth solely from the counsel of the highest wisdom, and become altogether salutary to those who are zealous to fulfil them in deeds. These things are enjoined by the apostles and the remaining teachers from Israel upon those who have believed from among the Gentiles: that they should confess to the Lord alone and, with idols forsaken, call upon his name, and proclaim all his works to the unbelieving, that they may know that he alone is exalted, and that « in his name every knee is bent, 1093Cof things in heaven, on earth, and under the earth (Phil. II), » to whom it must be sung that he hath done magnificently, and in the whole world his mercy is to be proclaimed. With this place also agrees the beginning of the hundred and fourth psalm, where it is thus written: « Confess ye to the Lord and call upon his nonem, declare his works among the nations. » In this, therefore, the order of the sayings consists of three parts. First he says: « Confess ye to the Lord, » that is, praise him and by good deeds acquire for yourselves the grace of the Godhead; because this very thing is to praise the Lord: both to render him honour with the mouth, and to carry out his commands by works. For that confession is here to be understood which does not cease to celebrate the Lord's praises, since the things that follow also declare such a vow and action. Then he admonishes that the Lord be called upon, 1093Dso that after the ministry of most holy praise we may pay him worthy thanks. For he duly calls upon God who cries to God more by deed than by voice. Whence the Saviour says in the Gospel to the proud and to those disobedient to him: « For why do you call me, Lord, Lord, and do not the things which I say? » (Luc. VI.) And elsewhere: « Not everyone, he says, that saith to me, Lord, Lord, shall enter into the kingdom of heaven; but he that doth the will of my Father, he shall enter into the kingdom of heaven (Matth. VII). » Thirdly he says: « Declare his works among the nations, » which can well suit the glorious evangelists and the most holy apostles, and now the priests, who are known to declare his mighty works throughout all the nations.

1094A« Rejoice and praise, O habitation of Sion, for great is the Holy One of Israel in the midst of thee. » First it must be said according to the letter: O habitation of Sion, rejoice and praise thy God, because he who before seemed to be thine own God, and was shut within the narrow bounds of the land of Judea, has now filled the whole earth with his knowledge, and rising from death reigns among the nations, and the peoples entreat and adore him — provided at least that he set thy hand a second time to possess what is left of his people, and gathering the fugitives of Israel and the dispersed of Juda, to be collected from the four quarters of the earth; because the seed-plot of the Gospel proceeded, through the apostles who were of the Jews, from the fountains of Israel. But it is better that we interpret Sion, that is, a watchtower placed on high, as the Church, of which 1094Bthe fiftieth psalm also sings: « Deal kindly, O Sion, in thy good will, that the walls of Jerusalem may be built up. » So that there may be made acceptable in her to the Lord the sacrifice of justice, oblations and holocausts, and the calf which the most merciful father immolated for the penitent son.

INCIPIT CANTICUM EZECHIAE Regis Judae.

Canticum ergo, quod sub Ezechiae nomine praenotatur, quod aliqui interpretum scripturam, alii orationem nominant, videtur mihi melius gratiarum actionis vocabulo praenotari. Sic enim scriptum 1094Cest in propheta (Isa. XXXVIII): « Scriptura Ezechiae regis Judae, cum aegrotasset et convaluisset de infirmitate sua. » Septuaginta vero ita posuerunt: « Oratio Ezechiae regis Judae, quando languit et surrexit de infirmitate sua. » Manifestum est enim quod post redditam sanitatem de infirmitate consurgens ista conscripserit, ex quo non oratio est, sed gratiarum actio pro beneficio quod acceperat. Potest enim hoc canticum bene convenire his qui post longam aegritudinem animarum resipiscunt a diaboli laqueis, et de peccatorum gravitudine sanantur, et student se virtutibus sacris recuperare, et laudes Dei non solum voce, sed etiam corde et actu pronuntiare.

« Ego dixi: In dimidio dierum meorum vadam 1094Dad portas inferi. » Narrat quid tempore prementis angustiae imminentisque languoris tacitus cogitarit. « Dixi, inquit, in corde meo, In dimidio dierum meorum, » sive, ut alii interpretati sunt, in infirmitate et silentio dierum meorum, sive in sanguine dierum meorum, quando meus cruor meusque spectabatur interitus. Itaque in desperatione dixi: « Vadam ad portas inferi. Vel communi lege naturae, vel illas portas, de quibus, quod liberatus sit Psalmista decantat: Qui exaltas me de portis mortis, ut annuntiem omnes laudationes tuas in portis filiae Sion (Psal. IX). Has portas inferni reor quae adversum Petrum non praevalent, quia dormivit in plenitudine dierum suorum. Sancti implent dies suos, qualis 1095Afuit Abraham qui mortuus est plenus dierum in senectute bona: peccatores vero et impii in medio dierum suorum moriuntur, de quibus et psalmus loquitur: Viri sanguinum et dolosi non dimidiabunt dies suos (Psal. LIV), » non enim implent opera virtutum nec student poenitentia emendare delicta. Unde in medio vitae cursu et in errorum tenebris ducentur ad tartarum.

« Quaesivi residuum annorum meorum. » Non me putans ultra esse victurum, dixi, « Non videbo Dominum Deum in terra viventium. » Pro Domino et Deo hic in Hebraico ponitur, Ia, quod in extrema syllaba alleluia sonat, pro quo Septuaginta transtulerunt, « nequaquam ultra videbo salutare Dei in terra viventium; » de qua et in alio loco scriptum 1095Best. « Placebo Domino in regione viventium (Psal. CXIV); » et rursum: « Placebo Domino in lumine viventium (Psal. LV). » Regio ergo sanctorum ipsa est, quae appellatur lux viventium. « Non est enim Deus mortuorum, sed vivorum (Matth. XXII), » hoc autem est omne quod metuit, ne salutare Dei, ductus ad inferos, nequaquam mereatur aspicere. Sequitur:

« Non aspiciam hominem ultra et habitatorem, quievit generatio mea, ablata est et convoluta est a me, quasi tabernaculum pastorum, praecisa est veluti a texente vita mea, dum adhuc ordirer, succidit me. » Timet ergo, ne cum sanctis et hominibus Dei non habitet in quiete, ne non videat Deum in terra viventium, ne generatio illius instar 1095Ctabernaculi dissolvatur, ne in telae similitudinem in ipso lucis exordio praecidatur, et nequaquam de semine ejus Christus oriatur. Quod autem corpus nostrum appelletur tabernaculum, et Apostolus instruit dicens: « Nos qui sumus in hoc tabernaculo ingemiscimus aggravati (II Cor. V). » Vita ergo humana non Parcarum filis secundum gentilium fabulas orditur, sed dispositione Dei ordinatur. Unde Job ad Dominum ait: « Breves dies hominis sunt, numerus mensium ejus apud te est, constituisti terminos ejus, qui praeteriri non poterunt (Job. XIV). » Sed quaeritur, quomodo numerus certus sit apud Dominum vitae humanae, si Ezechias se dixit in dimidio dierum moriturum. Sed sciendum in hoc quod propheta sibi praedixit mortem proximam illi esse venturam, 1095Dostenderit quid ipse meruerit: attamen praedestinatio Dei id quod voluit fecit, quia orationi ejus annuens, et poenitentiam considerans, addidit numerum dierum, quem ante saecula praescivit et praedestinavit futurum. Ne ergo elevaretur cor Ezechiae post incredibiles triumphos, et de media captivitate victoriam, infirmitate corporis visitatur, et audit se esse moriturum, et conversus ad Dominum flectat sententiam ejus, quod quidem et in Jona propheta legimus, et in comminationibus contra David, quae dicuntur futurae, nec factae sunt, non Deo mutante sententiam, sed provocante humanum genus ad notitiam sui. Dominus enim poenitens est uper malitiis: nec propheta igitur fallax, quia tempus 1096Amortis innotuit, quo vir ille mori merebatur, nec Dominica statuta convulsa sunt, quia ut ex largitate Dei anni vitae crescerent, hoc quoque ante saecula praefixum fuit, atque spatium vitae, quod inopinate foris est: additum sine augmento praescientiae fuit intus statutum

« De mane usque ad vesperum finies me, a vespere usque ad mane, quasi leo, sic contrivit omnia ossa mea. » Quod sic juxta aliam editionem legitur: « De mane usque ad vesperum finies me. » Sperabam usque ad mane, quod et Job in angustia sua atque in tormentis corporis sustinuisse se dicit, quando in die exspectabat noctem, et lucem praestolabatur in tenebris, mutatione temporum putans mutari posse supplicia. Hoc verum esse novit, qui 1096Bmagnis febribus aestuat, cujus ignis internus in similitudine leonis omnia ossa consumit, nec se putat prae doloris magnitudine esse ultra victurum.

« De mane usque ad vesperam finies me, sicut pullus hirundinis, sic clamabo, meditabor, ut columba. » Hae duae aves gemitum pro cantu edere solent. Unde harum similitudinem Ezechias in doloris sui querelis et gemitibus, cum oratione profusa ad Deum assumit, quatenus lugubris et fletu mixta deprecatio protinus ad pii judicis aures ascendat, et sic ipsius opem sibi citius collatam sentiat. Unde et sequitur.

« Attenuati sunt oculi mei suspicientes in excelsum. Domine, vim patior, responde pro me, quid dicam, aut quid respondebit mihi, cum ipse fecerim. » 1096CDefecerunt, inquit, oculi mei aspiciendo excelsum coeli ad Dominum, qui eruit me et abstulit dolorem animae meae, et ipse fecit; nunc quoque mors imminet et languores atque incumbens dolor, quasi leo, ita corporis mei ossa frangebat, sed ego in similitudinem hirundinis et columbae fletibus et gemitibus dies noctesque jungebam, et a Deo solo, qui poterat subvenire, elevatis in altum oculis, auxilium praestolabar, dicebamque ad eum: Plus patior, quam mea poscunt merita; sed et si quid erravi, convertar ad melius, tu sponde pro me. « Non est enim volentis, neque currentis, sed miserentis Dei (Rom. IX); » rursumque in se revertitur: Quid dicam, inquit, quidve causabor contra factorem meum, aut quid respondebit mihi, quia fecit ipse quod voluit. 1096DSustinenda ergo sunt quaecunque decreverit.

« Recogitabo omnes annos meos in amaritudine animae meae. Domine, si sic vivitur et in talibus vita spiritus mei. Corripies me et vivificabis me, ecce in pace amaritudo mea amarissima, tu autem eruisti animam meam, ut non periret. » Nulla res longa mortalium est omnisque felicitas saeculi, dum tenetur amittitur. Cum enim tribulationis tempus advenerit, omne quod praeteritum est, nihil adjuvat sustinentem. Unde stulta Epicuri sententia est qui asserit per recordationem praeteritorum bonorum mala praesentia mitigari. Ergo Ezechias reputare se dicit omnes annos regni sui, et praeteritae, ut putabat, beatitudinis in praesenti amaritudine: et quia 1097Ajam securus est, nec patitur quae refert de humano statu, philosophatur et dicit: « Domine, sic vivitur, et tali sumus conditione generati, » corripuisti me, sed vivificasti me pacemque tribuisti fugato Asyro; sed pax mea omni mihi amaritudine fuit amarior, quia tranquillitate populi reddita et urbe secura, ego solus limina mortis intravi, sed tu eruisti animam meam, ut non periret, vel praesenti vitae, vel futurae.

« Projecisti post tergum tuum omnia peccata mea, quia non infernus confitebitur tibi neque mors laudabit te. » Projecisti, inquit, post tergum omnia peccata mea, ne illa tristis aspicerem, sed tuam misericordiam contemplarer: infernus enim et mors non confitebuntur neque laudabunt te, juxta illud 1097Bquod scriptum est: « In inferno autem quis confitebitur tibi (Psal. XXX)? » Confessioque quae in hoc loco non pro poenitentia, sed pro gloria et laude accipitur, sicut et in Evangelio legimus: « Confiteor tibi, Pater, Domine coeli et terrae (Matth. XI). »

« Non exspectabunt qui descendunt in lacum veritatem tuam; vivens vivens ipse confitebitur tibi, sicut et ego hodie. » Non exspectabunt, inquit, qui descendunt in lacum veritatem tuam; melius enim hoc ad intellectum pertinet, quam illud, quod in LXX legimus, non exspectabunt, qui descendunt in lacum, misericordiam tuam; maxime, cum Salvator ad infernum descenderit, ut vinctos de inferis liberaret. Pro lacu manifestius iidem inferos transtulerunt. « Vivens vivens ipse confitebitur, sicut et ego 1097Chodie. » Et hic confessio pro laudatione ponitur: neque enim sua scelera confitetur, sed gratias agit Deo, et non est pulchra laudatio in ore peccatoris. Cumque infernus et mors non confiteantur, nec laudent Deum, econtrario vita atque viventes Dominum glorificant, quodque sequitur:

« Pater filiis notam faciet veritatem tuam. Domine, salvum me fac et psalmos nostros cantabimus cunctis diebus vitae nostrae in domo Domini. » Pater filiis notam faciet veritatem tuam. Hoc significat, quod et in Deuteronomio dicitur: « Interroga patrem tuum et annuntiabit tibi; seniores tuos, et dicent tibi (Deut. XXXII), » ut per successiones et singulas generationes Dei in posteros clementia praedicetur, pro quo LXX posuerunt. Ab hodie enim 1097Dfilios faciam, qui annuntiabunt justitiam tuam, causalis, quae conjunctio sequentia cum superioribus copulat, quod videlicet idcirco viventes et ipse vivens benedicant Deo, quia ab hac die facturus sit filios, qui annuntient veritatem ejus: quod certe suae non erat potestatis, neque enim per Prophetam illi liberi repromissi sunt, sed praesens vita concessa, praesertim cum impiissimum filium genuerit Manassen, qui repleverit Jerusalem sanguine a porta usque ad portam, et non benedixit, sed maledixit Deo, persequens sanctos ejus. Possumus ergo, juxta LXX, hoc dicere, quod non dixerit filios faciam, sed PIDIA, quod vel parvulos, vel pueros sive infantulos et posteros intelligimus, ut ex eo quod ipse misericordiam 1098Aconsecutus est, omnis ventura posteritas hoc cognoscens laudet ejus incredibilem clementiam. Salvum ergo me fac, Domine, ut omnes qui in te credimus tuoque sumus auxilio liberati, cunctis diebus vitae nostrae te cantemus in templo.

HERE BEGINS THE CANTICLE OF HEZEKIAH King of Judah.

The canticle, therefore, which is entitled under the name of Hezekiah, which some of the interpreters call a writing and others a prayer, seems to me better entitled by the word "thanksgiving." For thus it is written 1094Cin the prophet (Isa. XXXVIII): « The writing of Hezekiah king of Judah, when he had been sick and had recovered from his infirmity. » But the Seventy set it down thus: « The prayer of Hezekiah king of Judah, when he was sick and rose up from his infirmity. » For it is manifest that he wrote these things after his health had been restored, rising up from his infirmity; from which it is not a prayer, but a thanksgiving for the benefit which he had received. And this canticle can well suit those who, after a long sickness of their souls, come to themselves out of the devil's snares, and are healed of the heaviness of their sins, and are zealous to recover themselves in the holy virtues, and to utter the praises of God not only with the voice, but also with the heart and with deeds.

« I said: In the midst of my days I shall go 1094Dto the gates of hell. » He tells what he had silently thought in the time of the pressing anguish and the impending sickness. « I said, he says, in my heart, In the midst of my days, » — or, as others have translated, in the infirmity and silence of my days, or in the blood of my days, when my own gore and my own destruction were being looked upon. And so in despair I said: « I shall go to the gates of hell. Either by the common law of nature, or those gates concerning which the Psalmist sings that he has been delivered: Thou who liftest me up from the gates of death, that I may declare all thy praises in the gates of the daughter of Sion (Psal. IX). These I take to be the gates of hell which do not prevail against Peter, because he slept in the fulness of his days. The saints fill up their days, such as 1095AAbraham was, who died full of days in a good old age; but sinners and the impious die in the midst of their days, of whom also the psalm speaks: Bloody and deceitful men shall not live out half their days (Psal. LIV), » for they do not fill up the works of the virtues, nor are they zealous to amend their offences by penance. Whence in the mid-course of life and in the darkness of errors they will be led down to Tartarus.

« I sought the residue of my years. » Not thinking that I should live any longer, I said, « I shall not see the Lord God in the land of the living. » For "Lord" and "God" there is here set in the Hebrew Ia, which sounds in the last syllable of alleluia; for which the Seventy translated, « by no means shall I any more see the salvation of God in the land of the living »; concerning which it is also written in another place: 1095B« I will please the Lord in the region of the living (Psal. CXIV); » and again: « I will please the Lord in the light of the living (Psal. LV). » That, therefore, is the region of the saints which is called the light of the living. « For he is not the God of the dead, but of the living (Matth. XXII). » Now this is the whole of what he fears: that, led down to the powers below, he may by no means deserve to behold the salvation of God. There follows:

« I shall not look upon man any more, and upon the inhabitant; my generation is at rest, it is taken away and folded up from me, as a shepherds' tent; my life is cut off as by a weaver, while I was yet beginning, he cut me down. » He fears, therefore, lest he should not dwell in rest with the saints and the men of God, lest he should not see God in the land of the living, lest his generation should be dissolved after the manner 1095Cof a tent, lest in the likeness of a web it should be cut off at the very beginning of the light, and lest Christ should by no means arise from his seed. But that our body is called a tent, the Apostle too teaches, saying: « We who are in this tabernacle groan, being burdened (II Cor. V). » Human life, therefore, is not begun upon the threads of the Fates, according to the fables of the heathen, but is ordered by the disposition of God. Whence Job says to the Lord: « The days of man are short; the number of his months is with thee; thou hast appointed his bounds, which cannot be passed (Job. XIV). » But it is asked how the number of human life can be fixed with the Lord, if Hezekiah said that he would die in the midst of his days. But it must be known that in this — that the prophet foretold to him that death would come near to him — 1095Dhe showed what the man himself deserved; yet the predestination of God did what it willed, because, assenting to his prayer and considering his penance, it added a number of days which before the ages it foreknew and predestined to be. Lest, therefore, the heart of Hezekiah should be lifted up after his incredible triumphs and his victory out of the midst of captivity, he is visited with infirmity of body, and hears that he is to die, and, turning to the Lord, he bends his sentence — which indeed we read also in the prophet Jonah, and in the threatenings against David, which are said to be coming and were not done: God not changing his sentence, but provoking the human race to the knowledge of himself. For the Lord is one who repents uper evils; nor therefore is the prophet false, because the time 1096Aof death was made known at which that man deserved to die; nor were the Lord's decrees overthrown, because that the years of life should grow out of God's bounty — this too was fixed beforehand before the ages; and the span of life, which unexpectedly is outside: it was added without increase of foreknowledge, having been appointed within

« From morning even to evening thou wilt make an end of me; from evening even to morning, like a lion, so hath he broken all my bones. » Which according to the other edition is read thus: « From morning even to evening thou wilt make an end of me. » I hoped until morning — which Job too says he endured in his anguish and in the torments of his body, when in the day he looked for night, and awaited light in the darkness, thinking that by the change of the times his sufferings could be changed. He knows this to be true who 1096Bburns with great fevers, whose inward fire, in the likeness of a lion, consumes all his bones, and who does not think, because of the greatness of his pain, that he will live any longer.

« From morning even to evening thou wilt make an end of me; like a young swallow, so shall I cry; I shall meditate like a dove. » These two birds are wont to utter a groan instead of a song. Whence Hezekiah takes on their likeness in the complaints and groanings of his sorrow, together with prayer poured out to God, so that a mournful deprecation, mingled with weeping, may straightway ascend to the ears of the merciful judge, and so he may feel his aid the more swiftly bestowed upon him. Whence there also follows:

« My eyes are weakened, looking upward. Lord, I suffer violence; answer thou for me. What shall I say, or what shall he answer me, since I myself have done it? » 1096CMy eyes have failed, he says, in looking upon the height of heaven to the Lord, who rescued me and took away the sorrow of my soul, and he himself did it; now too death is at hand, and sicknesses and pressing sorrow, like a lion, so was it breaking the bones of my body; but I, in the likeness of the swallow and the dove, joined days and nights with weepings and groanings, and with eyes lifted on high I awaited help from God alone, who was able to succour, and I said to him: I suffer more than my deserts demand; but even if I have erred in anything, I will turn to what is better; be thou surety for me. « For it is not of him that willeth, nor of him that runneth, but of God that sheweth mercy (Rom. IX); » and again he returns into himself: What shall I say, he says, or what plea shall I make against my maker, or what shall he answer me, since he himself did what he willed? 1096DWhatsoever therefore he shall have decreed must be borne.

« I will recount all my years in the bitterness of my soul. Lord, if so one lives, and the life of my spirit is in such things. Thou wilt rebuke me and wilt quicken me; behold, in peace is my bitterness most bitter; but thou hast delivered my soul, that it should not perish. » No thing of mortals is long, and all the felicity of the world is lost while it is held. For when the time of tribulation has come, all that is past is no help to him who endures. Whence foolish is that opinion of Epicurus who asserts that present evils are mitigated by the recollection of past goods. Therefore Hezekiah says that he reckons up all the years of his reign, and of his past blessedness, as he thought it, in his present bitterness; and because 1097Ahe is now safe, and does not suffer the things which he relates concerning the human condition, he philosophizes and says: « Lord, so one lives, and we are begotten in such a condition, » thou hast rebuked me, but thou hast quickened me and hast granted peace, the Assyrian having been put to flight; but my peace was more bitter to me than all bitterness, because, the tranquillity of the people being restored and the city made secure, I alone entered the thresholds of death; but thou hast delivered my soul, that it should not perish, either to the present life or to the life to come.

« Thou hast cast behind thy back all my sins, because hell shall not confess to thee, neither shall death praise thee. » Thou hast cast, he says, behind thy back all my sins, that I might not look upon them in sadness, but might contemplate thy mercy; for hell and death shall not confess nor praise thee, according to that 1097Bwhich is written: « But in hell who shall confess to thee (Psal. XXX)? » And confession, which in this place is taken not for penance but for glory and praise, as we read also in the Gospel: « I confess to thee, O Father, Lord of heaven and earth (Matth. XI). »

« They that go down into the pit shall not look for thy truth; the living, the living, he shall confess to thee, as I also do this day. » They that go down into the pit, he says, shall not look for thy truth; for this pertains better to the understanding than that which we read in the Seventy, they that go down into the pit shall not look for thy mercy — especially since the Saviour descended to hell, that he might free the bound from the powers below. For "pit" the same translators rendered more plainly "the powers below." « The living, the living, he shall confess, as I also 1097Cthis day. » And here confession is put for praise; for he does not confess his own crimes, but gives thanks to God — and praise is not comely in the mouth of a sinner. And since hell and death do not confess nor praise God, on the contrary life and the living glorify the Lord; and as to what follows:

« The father shall make thy truth known to his children. Lord, save me, and we will sing our psalms all the days of our life in the house of the Lord. » The father shall make thy truth known to his children. This signifies what is also said in Deuteronomy: « Ask thy father, and he will declare to thee; thy elders, and they will tell thee (Deut. XXXII), » so that through successions and through the several generations the clemency of God may be proclaimed to those that come after; for which the Seventy set down: For from this day 1097DI will make sons, who shall declare thy justice — a causal [particle], which conjunction couples what follows with what goes before; namely, that the living and he himself living bless God for this reason, because from this day he is to make sons who shall declare his truth: which certainly was not in his own power, for neither were children promised him through the Prophet, but the present life granted — especially since he begot that most impious son Manasseh, who filled Jerusalem with blood from gate to gate, and did not bless but cursed God, persecuting his saints. We can therefore say this, according to the Seventy, that he did not say I will make sons, but PIDIA, by which we understand either little ones, or boys, or infants and those who come after; so that, from the fact that he himself obtained mercy, 1098Aall posterity to come, learning this, may praise his incredible clemency. Save me therefore, O Lord, that all we who believe in thee and have been delivered by thy help may sing to thee in the temple all the days of our life.

INCIPIT CANTICUM ANNAE, Matris Samuelis prophetae.

Igitur Canticum quod in libro Regum prophetico spiritu Anna mater beati Samuelis prophetae proferebat, et sub titulo orationis praenotatum est, multa sacramenta de Christo et Ecclesia ejus continet, quia 1098Bipsius munere haec prophetizando praesagabatur, cujus ope totius Ecclesiae subsistit fortitudo, et amplificatur multitudo, et confirmatur stabilitas, sed quaeritur cur Scriptura dicat Annam orando hoc carmen proferre, cum magis in ea praedicatio futurorum, quam postulatio alicujus rei inveniatur. Sic enim scriptum est, Et oravit Anna et ait:

« Exsultavit cor meum in Domino, et exaltatum est cornu meum in Deo meo. » Non enim invenitur orare in hoc cantico, nisi per duo verba tantum, ut dicit: « Laetata sum in salutari meo, » et aliud est, « quia non est Deus praeter te. » Apostolus autem dicit: « Orate sine intermissione (I Thess. V). » Illi enim qui in divino versatur officio, omnia gesta ejus, dictaque pro oratione reputantur; quia 1098Cjustus cum sine intermissione, quae justa sunt, agit, per hoc sine intermissione justus orabit. Et in Psalmis dicit: « Elevatio manuum mearum sacrificium vespertinum (Psal. CXL). » Elevat manus ille, qui elevat actus suos. Aliter, Moyse elevante manus, vincebat Israel, dimittente vero, vincebat Amalech; quia donec famulus Dei elevat actus suos ad Dominum, vincit plebs Dei, cum autem dimiserit actus suos, vincit Amalech, inimicus Dei. « Exsultavit, » inquit Anna, « cor meum » et necessarie addidit, « in Domino, » ut Paulus dixit: « Gaudete in Domino semper (Phil. IV), » quia potest quis gaudere in carnalibus. Cum me homines laudant, non in Domino gaudeo, sed gaudeo in vanitate: hinc compungar propter verbum Dei, cum dicit: « Exsultavit cor 1098Dmeum in Domino. » Dicat ergo Anna, quae interpretatur gratia, Ecclesia videlicet Christi, civitas regis magni, gratia plena, prole fecunda, dicat, quod tanto ante de se prophetatum per os hujus piae matris agnoscit. « Exsultavit cor meum in Domino, et exaltatum est cornu meum in Deo meo. » Vere exsultavit cor, quia in Domino, et vere exaltatum cornu, quia non ex se, sed in Domino Deo suo exaltatum est. Et in alio loco dicit: « Exaltabuntur cornua justi (Psal. LXX), » quia de crucis Christi apicibus, justis conferuntur haec cornua, in his ventilabimus atque destruemus adversarias potestates animae nostrae insidiantes.

« Dilatatum est os meum super inimicos meos. » 1099AQuia et in angustiis pressurarum sermo Dei non est alligatus, nec in praeconibus alligatis.

« Quia laetata sum in salutari tuo. » Salutaris iste Dominus est Christus Jesus, quem Simon, sicut in Evangelio legitur (Luc. II), senex amplectens parvum, agnoscens magnum: « Nunc dimittis, inquit, Domine, servum tuum in pace; quia viderunt oculi mei salutare tuum. » Si laetatus fuero in salutari Domini, tunc dilatatum est os meum, per meditationem utique verbi Dei ad elevationem cordis veniemus. Unde Apostolus dicit: « Os nostrum ad vos patet, o Corinthii, cor nostrum dilatatum est (II Cor. VI). » Ex latitudine enim cordis ori sapientia ministratur.

« Non est sanctus ut est Dominus. » Distinctione 1099Butitur, non dizit, nisi Dominus, sed nullus est, sicut Dominus sanctus et sanctificans, justus et justificans.

« Neque enim est alius extra te, et non est fortis sicut Deus noster. » Non est, inquit, sanctus praeter te, Domine, quia nemo fit, nisi abs te, etsi sunt, qui dicuntur dii multi, et domini multi, nullus tamen eorum naturaliter est id quod Deus est, quia ipse solus est, qui dixit ad Moysen servum suum: « Ego sum qui sum (Exod. III); » nam umbra ad comparationem corporis non est, et fumus ad comparationem ignis non est.

« Nolite multiplicare loqui sublimia gloriantes. » Et Salomon dicit: « Altiora te ne quaesieris (Eccli. III). » Et alibi: « Dominus in coelo et tu super terram, 1099Cideo sint pauci sermones tui (Eccle. V). »

« Recedant vetera de ore vestro. » Hoc est, magniloquium non exeat de ore vestro. « Omnis sermo malus, » dicit Apostolus (Ephes. IV), « ex ore vestro non procedat. »

« Quia Deus scientiarum Dominus est, et ipsi praeparantur cogitationes. » Ipse vos scit et ubi nemo scit: Spiritus enim omnia scrutatur, etiam profunda Dei. Unde qui putat se aliquid esse, cum nihil sit, seipsum seducit. Haec dicuntur adversariis civitatis Dei ad Babyloniam pertinentibus, de sua virtute praesumentibus, in se, non in Domino gloriantibus, ex quibus sunt etiam carnales Israelitae terrenae Jerusalem, cives terrigenae, qui, ut dicit Apostolus (Rom. X), « ignorantes Dei justitiam, » id est, 1099Dquam dat homini Deus, qui solus est justus atque justificans, « et suam volentes constituere, » id est, velut a se sibi paratam, non ab illo impartitam, « justitiae Dei non sunt subjecti. » Utique, quia superbi de suo putantes, non de Dei posse placere. Sed quia est Deus scientiarum, atque ideo et arbiter conscientiarum, ipse videt cogitationes hominum, quoniam vanae sunt. Unde vanae sunt, nisi quia proprie hominum sunt, a quo ipsi homines sunt.

« Arcus fortium superatus est et infirmi accincti sunt robore. » Infirmatus est arcus, id est, intentio eorum, qua tam potentes sibi videntur esse, ut sine Dei dono atque adjutorio humana sufficientia divina possint implere mandata, et infirmi accincti sunt 1100Arobore, qui tantum in Dei pietate confidunt, et ab illo solatia quaerunt, dicentes cum Propheta: « Miserere mei, Domine, quoniam infirmus sum (Psal. XXX). » Infirmus erat populus gentilis, quando alienus a testamento Dei fuerat. Nunc ergo praecinctus est robore, cum indutus est scuto fidei et galea salutis, et gladio spiritus hostis antiqui tela ignea proterit.

« Saturati prius pro pane se rocaverunt et famelici saturati sunt. » Qui sunt intelligendi saturati panibus, nisi iidem ipsi, quasi potentes, id est, Israelitae, quibus credita sunt eloquia Domini? sed in eo populo ancillae filii minorati sunt, cum se subtraxerunt, ne crederent in Salvatorem, et in ipsis panibus, id est, divinis eloquiis quae illi soli tunc ex omnibus 1100Bgentibus acceperunt, terrena tantum sapiunt. Gentes autem, quibus lex illa non erat data, posteaquam per Novum Testamentum ad eloquia illa venerunt, multum esuriendo terram transierunt; quia in eis non terrena, sed coelestia sapuerunt, et hoc velut quaereretur causa cur factum sit.

« Donec sterilis peperit plurimos et quae multos habebat filios infirmata est. » In Hebraeo et in Septuaginta translatione non plurimi, sed septem leguntur. Judaei hunc locum ita intelligunt, quod nato Samuele mortuus sit filius primogenitus Fenennae, et singulis Annae filiis nascentibus, singuli Fenennae mortui sint filii; sed quaerendum est quomodo hoc stare possit, cum Fenenna septem, Anna autem non plus quam quinque filios habuerit. Hanc quaestionem Hebraei 1100Csolventes duos filios Samuelis cum filiis Annae annumerant: Quia sterilis, inquit, peperit septem et multa in filiis infirmata est. Hic totum quod prophetabatur eluxit agnoscentibus numerum septenarium, quo est universa Ecclesiae significata protectio, propter quod Joannes apostolus ad septem scribit Ecclesias, eo modo se ostendens ad unius plenitudinem scribere: et in Proverbiis Salomonis hoc antea praefigurans. « Sapientia aedificavit sibi domum et suffulsit columnas septem (Prov. IX). » Sterilis enim erat in omnibus gentibus Dei civitas, antequam iste fetus, quem cernimus, oriretur. Cernimus etiam quae multa in filiis, nunc infirmatam Jerusalem terrenam: quoniam quicunque filii liberae in ea erant, virtus ejus erant. Nunc vero ibi, quoniam littera est, 1100Det spiritus non est, amissa virtute infirmata est.

« Dominus mortificat et vivificat. » Mortificat illam quae multa erat in filiis, et vivificat hanc sterilem quae peperit septem, quamvis commodius possit intelligi eosdem vivificare, quos mortificaverit, id enim vel repetivit addendo.

« Deducit ad inferos et reducit. » Quibus enim dicit Apostolus: « Si mortui estis cum Christo, quae sursum sunt quaerite, ubi Christus est in dextera Dei sedens (Rom. VI), » salubriter mortificantur utique a Domino: quibus adjungit, « quae sursum sunt sapite, non quae super terram, » ut ipsi sint illi qui esurientes transierunt terram. Mortui enim estis, inquit, quomodo salubriter mortificat Deus; deinde 1101Asequitur: Et vita vestra abscondita est cum Christo in Deo. Ecce quomodo eosdem ipsos vivificat Deus: sed nunquid eosdem deduxit ad inferos et reduxit? Hoc utrumque sine controversia fidelium in illo potius videmus impletum, capite scilicet nostro, cum quo vitam nostram in Deo Apostolus dixit absconditam. Nam qui proprio filio non pepercit, sed pro nobis omnibus tradidit eum, iste modo utique mortificat, et quia resuscitavit a mortuis, eumdem rursus vivificavit. Et quia in prophetia vox ejus agnoscitur: « Non derelinques animam meam in inferno (Psal. XV), » eumdem deduxit ad inferos et reduxit, hac ejus paupertate ditati sumus.

« Dominus enim pauperes facit et ditat. » Nam quid hoc sit ut sciamus, quod sequitur audiamus.

1101B« Humiliat et sublimat. » Utique superbos humiliat et humiles exaltat; quod enim alibi legitur: « Deus superbis resistit, humilibus autem dat gratiam (Jac. IV); » hoc totus habet sermo, cujus nomen interpretatur gratia ejus. Jam vero, quod adjungitur:

« Suscitat a terra pauperem, » de nullo melius quam de illo intelligo qui propter nos pauper factus est, cum dives esset, ut ejus paupertate, sicut paulo ante dictum est, ditaremur; ipsum enim de terra suscitavit tam cito, ut caro ejus non videret corruptionem, nec illud ab illo alienabo, quod dictum est:

« Et de stercore erigit inopem. » Inops quippe idem qui pauper; stercus vero, unde erectus est, 1101Crectissime intelliguntur persecutores Judaei: in quorum numero, cum se dixisset Apostolus Ecclesiam persecutum: « Quae mihi, inquit, fuerunt lucra, haec propter Christum damna esse duxi, nec solum detrimenta, verum etiam stercora existimavi esse, ut Christum lucrifacerem (Phil. III). » De terra ergo suscitatus est, ille supra omnes divites pauper erectus est, et de illo stercore super omnes opulentos ille inops.

« Ut sedeat cum principibus, et solium gloriae teneat. » Quibus ait: « Sedebitis super duodecim sedes (Matth. XIX); » et sedem gloriae haereditatem dans eis. « Domini enim sunt cardines terrae et posuit super eos orbem. » Quia ipse dominatur a mari usque ad mare, et a flumine usque ad terminos orbis 1101Dterrae (Psal. LXXI). « Domini, inquit, sunt cardines terrae: » hoc est, quatuor plagae mundi, in quibus dilatabit orbem Ecclesiae suae, quae a solis ortu usque ad occasum laudat nomen ejus.

« Pedes sanctorum suorum servabit. » Hoc est, electorum suorum opera in via justitiae dirigit.

« Et impii in tenebris conticescent. » Qui mittentur in tenebras exteriores, ubi erit fletus et stridor dentium. Ubi conticescent, id est, ab arrogantia superbiae suae cessabunt.

« Quia non in fortitudine roborabitur vir. » Hoc est, non in fortitudine propria, sed in virtute divina potens est vir, id est, populus credentium.

« Dominum formidabunt adversarii ejus. » Quia 1102ADominus infirmum faciet adversarium ejus. Potest ex ambiguo graeco intelligi et adversarium suum. Cum enim Dominus possidere nos coeperit, profecto adversarius, qui noster fuerat ipsius fit, et vincetur a nobis, non viribus nostris, quia non in virtute propria potens est vir. Dominus ergo infirmum faciet adversarium suum. Dominus sanctus, ut vincatur a sanctis, quos Dominus sanctus sanctorum efficit sanctos, ac per hoc non glorietur potens in sua potentia, et non glorietur prudens in sua prudentia, nec dives in divitiis suis; sed in hoc glorietur, qui gloriatur intelligere et scire Dominum et facere judicium et justitiam in medio terrae, non parva ex parte intelligit, et scit Dominum, qui intelligit, et scit etiam hoc sibi a Domino dari, ut intelligat et 1102Bsciat Dominum. Sed quia retributio justa in die judicii futura est a Domino super filios Adae; inde sequitur:

« Super ipsos in coelis tonabit. » Ipse judicabit extremum terrae, quia justus est, prorsus ordinem tenuit confessionis fidelium haec prophetissa, ascendit enim in coelum Dominus Christus, et inde venturus est ad judicandum vivos et mortuos. « Nam quis ascendit, » sicut dicit Apostolus (Ephes. IV), « nisi et qui descendit in inferiores partes terrae? Qui descendit, ipse est qui ascendit super omnes coelos, ut adimpleret omnia. » Per nubes ergo suos tonuit, quas sancto Spiritu cum ascendisset implevit. De quibus carnali Jerusalem, hoc est, ingratae vineae comminatus est apud Isaiam prophetam, ne pluant 1102Csuper eam imbrem. Sic autem dictum est:

« Dominus judicabit fines terrae. » Ac si diceretur Dominus judicabit extrema hominis: quoniam non judicabuntur, quae in melius, vel in deterius medio tempore commutantur, sed in quibus extremis inventus fuerat qui judicabitur. « Et dabit imperium regi suo, hoc est, populo christiano: dat eis virtutem, qua carnem sicut reges regant, et in illo, qui propter eos fudit sanguinem suum, mundum vincant. Sequitur:

Et sublimabit cornu Christi sui. » Quomodo Christus exaltabit cornu Christi sui? Omnes quippe unctos ejus chrismate, recte Christos possumus dicere, quod tamen totum cum suo capite corpus unus est Christus. Haec Anna prophetavit Samuel 1102Dmater sancti viri, multumque laudati, in quo quidem tunc figurata est mutatio veteris sacerdotii, et nunc impleta, quando infirmata est, quae multa erat in filiis, ut novum haberet in Christo sacerdotium, sterilis quae peperit septem.

HERE BEGINS THE CANTICLE OF ANNA, Mother of Samuel the prophet.

The Canticle, therefore, which in the book of Kings Anna, the mother of the blessed Samuel the prophet, uttered in the prophetic spirit, and which is entitled under the title of a prayer, contains many mysteries concerning Christ and his Church; because 1098Bby his gift she was foreshowing these things in prophesying, by whose help the strength of the whole Church subsists, and its multitude is enlarged, and its stability confirmed. But it is asked why Scripture says that Anna uttered this song in praying, since there is found in it rather a proclamation of things to come than a petition for anything. For thus it is written, And Anna prayed and said:

« My heart hath rejoiced in the Lord, and my horn is exalted in my God. » For she is not found to pray in this canticle except by two phrases only, when she says: « I have rejoiced in my salvation, » and the other, « because there is no God beside thee. » But the Apostle says: « Pray without ceasing (I Thess. V). » For as to him who is engaged in the divine office, all his deeds and sayings are reckoned for prayer; because 1098Cwhen the just man does without ceasing the things that are just, by this he will pray without ceasing as a just man. And in the Psalms he says: « The lifting up of my hands, an evening sacrifice (Psal. CXL). » He lifts up his hands who lifts up his acts. Otherwise: when Moses lifted his hands, Israel conquered; but when he let them down, Amalec conquered: because so long as the servant of God lifts up his acts to the Lord, the people of God conquers; but when he has let down his acts, Amalec, the enemy of God, conquers. « My heart, » says Anna, « hath rejoiced, » and she necessarily added, « in the Lord, » as Paul said: « Rejoice in the Lord always (Phil. IV), » because one can rejoice in carnal things. When men praise me, I do not rejoice in the Lord, but I rejoice in vanity: hence let me be pricked to the heart because of the word of God, when it says: « My heart hath rejoiced 1098Din the Lord. » Let Anna, therefore — who is interpreted "grace" — that is, the Church of Christ, the city of the great king, full of grace, fruitful in offspring, let her say that she recognizes what was prophesied of her so long before through the mouth of this holy mother. « My heart hath rejoiced in the Lord, and my horn is exalted in my God. » Truly the heart rejoiced, because in the Lord; and truly the horn is exalted, because not of itself, but in the Lord its God is it exalted. And in another place he says: « The horns of the just shall be exalted (Psal. LXX), » because from the summits of the cross of Christ these horns are conferred upon the just; with them we shall winnow and destroy the adverse powers that lie in wait for our soul.

« My mouth is enlarged over my enemies. » 1099ABecause even in the straits of afflictions the word of God is not bound, nor is it bound in bound heralds.

« Because I have rejoiced in thy salvation. » That salvation is the Lord Christ Jesus, whom Simon, as is read in the Gospel (Luc. II), an old man embracing the little one, recognizing the great one, [said]: « Now thou dost dismiss, he says, thy servant, O Lord, in peace; because my eyes have seen thy salvation. » If I shall have rejoiced in the salvation of the Lord, then is my mouth enlarged; by the meditation of the word of God we shall assuredly come to the lifting up of the heart. Whence the Apostle says: « Our mouth is open to you, O Corinthians, our heart is enlarged (II Cor. VI). » For out of the breadth of the heart is wisdom ministered to the mouth.

« There is none holy as the Lord is. » He uses a distinction: 1099Bhe does not say dizit, "unless the Lord," but there is none, as the Lord is holy and sanctifying, just and justifying.

« For neither is there any other beside thee, and there is none strong like our God. » There is none holy, he says, beside thee, O Lord, because no one becomes so save from thee; and although there are those who are called many gods and many lords, yet none of them is by nature that which God is, because he alone is who said to Moses his servant: « I am who am (Exod. III); » for a shadow, in comparison with a body, is not; and smoke, in comparison with fire, is not.

« Do not multiply to speak lofty things, boasting. » And Solomon says: « Seek not the things that are too high for thee (Eccli. III). » And elsewhere: « God is in heaven and thou upon earth; 1099Ctherefore let thy words be few (Eccle. V). »

« Let old things depart from your mouth. » That is, let no great-speaking go forth out of your mouth. « Let no evil speech, » says the Apostle (Ephes. IV), « proceed out of your mouth. »

« For the Lord is a God of knowledges, and thoughts are prepared for him. » He himself knows you even where no one knows: for the Spirit searcheth all things, even the deep things of God. Whence he that thinketh himself to be something, whereas he is nothing, deceiveth himself. These things are said against the adversaries of the city of God, those belonging to Babylon, who presume upon their own strength, glorying in themselves and not in the Lord; among whom are also the carnal Israelites of the earthly Jerusalem, earth-born citizens, who, as the Apostle says (Rom. X), « being ignorant of the justice of God, » that is, 1099Dthat which God gives to man, who alone is just and justifying, « and willing to establish their own, » that is, as though prepared for themselves by themselves, not imparted by him, « are not subject to the justice of God. » Assuredly, because being proud they think that they can please out of their own, not out of God's. But because he is the God of knowledges, and therefore also the arbiter of consciences, he himself sees the thoughts of men, that they are vain. And whence are they vain, save because they are properly men's own, from whom the men themselves are?

« The bow of the mighty is overcome, and the weak are girded with strength. » The bow is weakened, that is, that purpose of theirs by which they seem to themselves so powerful that without God's gift and help human sufficiency can fulfil the divine commandments; and the weak are girded 1100Awith strength, who trust only in the mercy of God and seek consolations from him, saying with the Prophet: « Have mercy on me, O Lord, for I am weak (Psal. XXX). » The Gentile people was weak when it was a stranger to the testament of God. Now therefore it is girded with strength, since it is clothed with the shield of faith and the helmet of salvation, and with the sword of the spirit tramples down the fiery darts of the ancient enemy.

« They that were filled before have hired themselves out rocaverunt for bread, and the hungry are filled. » Who are to be understood as filled with loaves, save these same men, as being the powerful, that is, the Israelites, to whom the oracles of the Lord were entrusted? But in that people the sons of the handmaid were made less, when they withdrew themselves that they might not believe in the Saviour; and in those very loaves — that is, in the divine oracles, which they alone at that time out of all 1100Bthe nations received — they savour only earthly things. But the Gentiles, to whom that law had not been given, after they came through the New Testament to those oracles, passed over the earth by hungering much; because in them they savoured not earthly but heavenly things: and this as though the cause were sought why it was done.

« Until the barren hath borne very many, and she that had many children is made weak. » In the Hebrew and in the translation of the Seventy it is read not "very many," but "seven." The Jews understand this place thus: that when Samuel was born the firstborn son of Phenenna died, and that at the birth of each of Anna's sons one of Phenenna's sons died. But it must be asked how this can stand, since Phenenna had seven sons and Anna not more than five. The Hebrews, 1100Cresolving this question, reckon the two sons of Samuel along with the sons of Anna: For the barren, he says, hath borne seven, and she that had many children is made weak. Here the whole of what was prophesied has shone out to those who recognize the number seven, by which the whole protection of the Church is signified — on account of which John the apostle writes to seven Churches, showing in that manner that he writes to the fulness of one: and prefiguring this beforehand in the Proverbs of Solomon: « Wisdom hath built herself a house, and hath propped up seven pillars (Prov. IX). » For the city of God was barren among all the nations, before that offspring which we behold arose. We also behold her who was many in sons, the earthly Jerusalem, now made weak: because whatever sons of the free woman were in her, they were her strength. But now, since there is the letter there 1100Dand the spirit is not, having lost her strength she is made weak.

« The Lord killeth and maketh alive. » He killeth her that was many in sons, and maketh alive this barren one who hath borne seven; although it can more conveniently be understood that he maketh alive the same whom he hath killed, for this he either repeated by adding.

« He bringeth down to hell and bringeth back. » For those to whom the Apostle says: « If you be dead with Christ, seek the things that are above, where Christ is sitting at the right hand of God (Rom. VI), » are assuredly killed wholesomely by the Lord; to whom he adds, « mind the things that are above, not the things that are upon the earth, » so that these may be the very ones who hungering passed over the earth. For you are dead, he says — how does God kill wholesomely? — and then 1101Athere follows: And your life is hid with Christ in God. Behold how God maketh these same men alive: but did he bring down the same to hell and bring them back? Both of these, without controversy among the faithful, we see rather fulfilled in him, namely in our head, with whom the Apostle said our life was hid. For he who spared not his own Son, but delivered him up for us all, he assuredly in this manner killeth; and because he raised him from the dead, he again made the same alive. And because in the prophecy his voice is recognized: « Thou wilt not leave my soul in hell (Psal. XV), » he brought down the same to hell and brought him back; by this poverty of his we have been enriched.

« For the Lord maketh poor and maketh rich. » Now that we may know what this is, let us hear what follows.

1101B« He humbleth and he exalteth. » Assuredly he humbleth the proud and exalteth the humble; for that which is read elsewhere: « God resisteth the proud, but to the humble he giveth grace (Jac. IV); » this the whole discourse holds, whose name is interpreted "his grace." But now, as to what is added:

« He raiseth up the needy from the earth, » I understand it of none better than of him who for our sakes was made poor, whereas he was rich, that by his poverty, as was said a little before, we might be enriched; for him he raised up from the earth so quickly that his flesh saw no corruption; nor will I alienate from him that which is said:
