LXXII), » not that he is a beast of burden absolutely, but that before God he is a beast of burden. « Raising up, therefore, the Lord raises up his bow, and bends his arrows over the sceptres, » that is, over the kingdoms of the devil, which are contrary to the Lord in divers vices and sins — that is, in avarice, luxury, envy, detraction, thefts and perjuries — against which the word of God, sitting upon his horses and 1122Ahis chariots, that is, in the souls of the saints, upon which the divine word mounts, raises up the bow of the holy Scriptures, out of which the darts of the divine testimonies are sent forth, and fulfils the oaths to the tribes of the Lord which he spoke to their fathers: to those tribes, namely, of which the Psalmist, discoursing of the mystical Jerusalem, says: « For thither did the tribes go up, the tribes of the Lord, the testimony of Israel, to praise his name (Psal. CXXI). » And in this which is read according to the edition of the LXX interpreters, « Was thy anger upon the rivers, O Lord? or thy fury upon the rivers, or thine onset upon the sea? » the hearts of unbelievers can be expressed by the name of rivers and of the sea: rivers, because they are rightly so called, since with the whole onset of their intention they flow down to lower things; a sea, because 1122Bwithin they are darkened by turbid and bitter thoughts, and lift themselves above the rest with the swelling whirlpools of boastfulness. Have these then, he says, sinned so grievously — who sink the mind from heavenly desires into the appetite of infirm things, and who by the haughtiness of an unstable mind raise themselves against their neighbours — that in such men the wrath which they deserve should abide, never to be loosed? or dost thou, appearing in the world, bestow the grace of thy mercy upon all who sin throughout the earth, whether more lightly or more grievously? For I do indeed perceive that thou wilt send apostles to preach thy glory to the nations; but who they are that shall believe belongs to thy thought, not to man's. For this is what follows: « Because ascending thou wilt ascend upon thy horses, and thy horsemen are health, » that is, ascending in the heart of thine elect 1122Cthrough the illumination of grace, by which, under thy governance, they may walk the way of the virtues, and through which, bearing thee through the whole world by their evangelizing, they may preach to the world the life of perpetual salvation. Of which horsemanship a figure was shown in the Lord even according to the letter, when, journeying to Jerusalem, he sat upon an ass, while the crowds that went before and that followed and that came to meet him sang together: « Hosanna, blessed is he that cometh in the name of the Lord (Matth. XXI). » In which riding health resounded, because assuredly that spiritual journey was signified by which, with the Lord as leader, men are led through the apostles to the kingdoms that are to be beheld of that Jerusalem which is above, which is the mother of us all.

« Drawing thou wilt stretch forth thy bow over the sceptres, saith the Lord. » By the bow he means the unforeseen coming 1122Dof the divine examination, by which he foresaw that the sceptres also, that is, the kingdoms of the world, were to be examined. The Prophet, therefore, intimating what our Lord does in them when he ascends upon his horses — that is, when he fills and rules with his grace the apostles and their successors — says: « bending, he says, thou wilt stretch forth thy bow over the sceptres, » that is, threatening through the doctors thou wilt threaten that thy judgment is suddenly to come, so that whosoever shall have been terrified at the threatening of wrath as at a stretched bow, and shall have taken care to entreat thy mercy, may not feel the sending forth of the arrows, that is, the threatening of everlasting punishments. But in that, having set down first « drawing thou wilt stretch forth thy bow over the sceptres, » he added « saith the Lord, » he signifies God the Father, of whom the 1123ASon himself says: « The Father, he says, judgeth not, but hath given all judgment to the Son (Joan. V). »

« Cleaving the rivers of the earth. » Let us first set down an example, that by this as by a step we may come to higher things. We read in those who have composed volumes concerning marvels, and who have carried down the Olympiads of Greece even unto our own memory, setting forth what new thing befell in the world in each several year, that among other things rivers burst forth by an earthquake which had not been before, and others were swallowed up and went to ruin: namely, that all the veins of the lands have in themselves hidden waters, as the human body has blood, which are burst open by the shaking of the earth and remain as rivers. If we have understood this, let us see that the human soul has in itself naturally waters and rivers, and that through our sloth 1123Bthey are hidden and do not flow; but that when it has been shaken at the preaching of the word of God, and moved from its former state, then that which lay hidden bursts forth and flows for the refreshment of those who drink. But as to what is read according to another edition, « The earth shall be cleft by rivers »: by rivers in this place he does not mean the same ones concerning which above he feared the wrath and the fury of the Lord, but rather those of which he himself says in the Gospel: « He that believeth in me, he says, as the Scripture saith, out of his belly shall flow rivers of living water. » And the evangelist expounding it: « Now this, he says, he said of the Spirit which they should receive who believed in him (Joan. VII). » By these rivers, therefore, the earth shall be cleft: when the hearts of carnal men, watered by the words of saving doctrine, grind down the hardness of their unbelief by humbling themselves, and lay open the bosom of inward thought, 1123Cwhich had been ill closed, to receive the words of saving rebuke or of exhortation.

« They saw thee, O God, and the mountains grieved; the whirlpool of waters passed away; the deep gave its voice; the height lifted up its hands; the sun and the moon stood in their habitation; in the light of thine arrows they shall go, in the brightness of thy glittering spear; in thy roaring thou wilt trample down the earth; in thy fury thou wilt astonish the nations; having gone forth for the salvation of thy people, for salvation with thy Christ; thou hast struck the head from the house of the impious; thou hast laid bare the foundation even to the neck for ever. » We have set down our own edition alone, so that, weaving the sequence of the passage according to it — that is, according to the Hebrew — we may afterwards discuss the LXX clause by clause, section 1123Dby section. They saw thee, O God, and the mountains grieved — the lofty kingdoms, namely, and the sublime powers of this world, and the four chariots in Zacharias which go forth from the brazen mountains. These saw thee and trembled; and the whirlpool of waters passed away, that is, all their onset and persecution, wherewith they harassed thy people, passed away after they saw thee. Then the deep, that is, those below, praised thee; then also those above, that is, the angels, clapped their hands together in applause, and showed thee as victor by a kind of gesture and dance of uplifted hands. Thy sun and moon, and all the splendour wherewith thou hadst before given light to thy people, and which afterwards, under the weight of evils, had all been covered over with the horror of darkness, 1124Areceived their light again and had their pristmum brightness. Thine arrows and thy glittering spear, that is, thy strokes and thy instruction, afforded light to thy people. Finally, in the light of thine arrows and in the brightness of thy spear, which chastised them to this end, that it might amend them, thy people walked in thy roaring. When therefore thou shalt avenge the wrong done to thy people, thou wilt tread down the earthly kingdoms and wilt make all the nations wonder, because thou hast gone forth for the salvation of thy people and hast come to them with thy Christ; for Jesus is interpreted "Saviour." And when Jesus Christ thy Son came, thou didst strike Antichrist from the house of the impious, that is, in this world which is set in wickedness; or thou didst strike the devil himself, who is the head of impiety, and thou hast laid bare his foundation even to the neck, that 1124Bis, thou hast made his hidden things open, not for a short time but for perpetuity; for this is what Sela signifies, that is, "always." But as to what is read according to another edition:

« The peoples shall see thee and shall grieve. » Such is the sense: the hearts of sinners, cleft to the acknowledgment and confession of the truth by the frequent address of those who teach, as by the washing of repeated floods, see the Lord meanwhile through faith, and grieve that they have withdrawn from him so long through fault; but when the grief of penance is ended, they see him more fully in the time to come through sight, and rejoice in the blessed vision of him for ever. There follows:

« Sprinkling waters in his journeys, the deep gave its voice from the height of its imagination. » 1124CThe same doctors who above are figured by the name of rivers are figured also by the name of the deep. But they are rivers because of the force of a stronger invective, wherewith they cut through the hardness of the earthly mind to do penance for its errors. But they are rightly called a deep from the height of the knowledge wherewith they themselves are filled within, Solomon bearing witness, who says: « The words of a man's mouth are deep water (Prov. XVIII). » Sprinkling waters, therefore, in the journeys, the deep gives its voice, when holy preachers, filled within in heart with the deep knowledge of truth, set forth the ministry of the word outwardly to their hearers, bringing it out little by little and in parts, according to the capacity of the weak, which they themselves inwardly take in both many and ample at once. And by "in the journeys" he means "in the works," either of the doctors themselves, namely, or of their hearers. For they sprinkle waters 1124Din their own journeys, when wherever they go they always show to those who look on examples of right living together with the voice of preaching. And they sprinkle waters also in the journeys of those who look on, when by teaching and by living alike they show them beforehand by what ways of action they ought to walk. And well, when he had said that the deep gave its voice, did he add "from the height of its imagination"; because that which the holy preachers utter marvellously without proceeds assuredly from that fountain of wisdom wherewith they themselves within are more marvellously enlightened by the contemplation of heavenly joys. Or did not the great deep give its voice from the height of its imagination, when Paul the apostle said: « Our mouth is open to you, O ye Corinthians, our heart is enlarged; you are not straitened in 1125Aus, but you are straitened in your own bowels; I speak as to my children: be you also enlarged. But whence, or in what order, it came to pass that the world should be sprinkled with saving waters, and that the deep of heavenly wisdom should thunder upon the lands, is shown in what follows, when it is said:

« The sun was lifted up, and the moon stood in its order. » For the Lord — the sun of justice, namely — was lifted up into heaven after his passion and resurrection, and, the Spirit having been sent by the Father, he enlightened the Church and spread her abroad through the whole world, that she in her own order — that is, after the secret of the Lord's incarnation had been fulfilled — rising from the long languor of unbelief, might stand in faith, act manfully, and be strengthened in his charity. And because he had compared Christ to the sun and the Church to the moon, 1125Bfitly, turning to that same sun, he straightway subjoined:

« In the light thy darts shall go, in the brightness of the flashing of thy arms. » Now the darts of Christ are his words, wherewith the hearts of men are pricked, so that, a saving wound being inflicted, the faithful soul may say, I am wounded with charity: which darts, namely, go in the light, because through the ministry of the doctors the words of truth became openly known to the world, according to that which the Truth itself commanded them, saying: « The things which I tell you in the dark, speak ye in the light; and that which you hear in the ear, preach ye upon the housetops (Luc. XII); » and since the brightness of miracles also followed the utterances of light, he added, in the brightness of the flashing of thy arms; for with darts men at war strike their adversaries, 1125Cwith arms, by fortifying themselves, they descend from a wound. Whence by darts are fittingly intimated the sayings of preachers, wherewith they overcome the depravity of unbelievers, and by arms the miracles wherewith they confirm the truth of their preaching. The darts of Christ, therefore, go in the light; they go in the brightness of the flashing of his arms: because the mighty works which he did, the secrets which he opened, the commandments which he set forth, the rewards which he promised, have now become known to the whole world through the evangelical writings more clearly than the sun. And the holy doctors also, because they are sons of light, whatsoever by his gift they do or speak shines out bright with light and brightness.

« In thy threatening thou wilt make the earth less, and in thy fury. » Thou wilt drag down the nations by threatening the strictness of the judgment whereby the impious are condemned; thou wilt wholesomely humble 1125Dthose who were wont to set earthly things before heavenly, so that, earthly desires being little by little made less, they may begin to relish and to seek the things that are above; and by bringing in fororem thou wilt condemn for ever those who, for the sake of their eternal exaltation, scorned to be humbled for a time.

« Thou wentest forth for the salvation of thy people, that thou mightest save thy christs. » The mediator of God and men went forth from the Father and came into the world, not that he might judge the world, but that the world might be saved by him. And by christs he means all the elect, because from the unction of spiritual grace they are most rightly named by this word. Whence is that word of the Psalmist concerning those who, wishing to hurt the saints, were restrained 1126Aby a divine prohibition. And he reproved kings for their sakes: « Touch ye not my christs (Psal. CIV). » And he saved his christs — not those whom he found christs, but those whom, by going forth from the Father and appearing in the flesh, he made christs, that is, his anointed ones, through the Spirit of adoption. Of which unction John the apostle, admonishing his hearers, says: « And as for you, let the unction which you have received from him abide in you (I Joan. II). »

« Thou hast sent death upon the heads of thine enemies; thou hast stirred up the bonds even unto the necks. » For the Lord slays sin, sending death upon the heads of his enemies, that he may give life unto justice. I will say something more boldly: Christ came into the world for this, that he might send death upon the heads of his enemies, and that, as he himself died once to sin, so they too might die to iniquity, and that they who 1126Bhad been made partakers of death might be made partakers also of this life. The Lord raised up the bonds of charity, that, the former burden being laid down and the heaviest yoke wherewith they were pressed being cast off, we might take up the light yoke of Christ, so that, set with them in the chariot, we might carry the best of charioteers. Which can also be understood otherwise: for he who offered the joy of salvation to his elect, whom he had called christs through the Prophet, himself sent eternal death upon those who neglected to be anointed with his grace; which is agreed to have been fulfilled even bodily in that very nation of the Jews, which persecuted the Lord appearing in the flesh even unto death: for after it had crucified him, not many years intervening, when the Roman army pressed on — only those excepted who had separated themselves unto the faith of the evangelical grace — 1126Cit was condemned with a monstrous slaughter, and was moreover itself deprived of kingdom and country. And this is what he says: « Thou hast stirred up the bonds even unto the neck » — the neck, namely, of the kingdom, wherewith they had been raised up against the Lord; of which the most blessed protomartyr Stephen says to those same men, raging even against himself: « Stiffnecked and uncircumcised in hearts and ears, you always resist the Holy Spirit (Act. V). » But even unto this neck did the Lord stir up the bonds, when he sent a hostile army for the overthrow of that proud nation. Whence it is written: « The just Lord shall cut the necks of sinners (Psal. CXXVIII). »

« Thou hast cursed his sceptres, the heads of his warriors, coming as a whirlwind to scatter me. Their exultation is as of him that devoureth the poor man in secret. Thou hast made a way in the sea for thy horses, in the 1126Dmire of many waters. I have heard, and my belly was troubled; at the voice my lips trembled. Let rottenness enter into my bones, and swarm beneath me, that I may rest in the day of my tribulation, that I may go up to our girded people. » And now I have set down the Hebrew only, that we may discuss the edition of the LXX separately; for the LXX disagrees much with the translation of all the rest. Thou hast cursed the sceptres, that is, his kingdoms — there is no doubt but that they are the impious one's, of whom he had said above, thou hast struck the head from the house of the impious, thou hast laid bare the foundation even to the neck. But we understand the impious one to be either Nebuchadnezzar or every adversary of the people of God; and not only his sceptres, but also the heads of the warriors 1127Awhom he had struck, who came, he says, as a whirlwind, that they might scatter me — that is, that they might overturn Israel and lead it captive into divers places — so that they exulted, devouring the poor man and Israel subjected to them, as though they did this in secret, and devoured us unawares. Thou camest therefore to battle for thy people, and, sending thy four-horse cars into the waters, that is, into many nations, thou madest them a way in the mire of many waters, that is, that thou mightest trample them down and grind them like mire with the hoof and the wheel of thy horses and thy chariot. But as to what follows, « I have heard, and my belly was troubled at the voice, » it is to be understood thus: my lips trembled; let rottenness enter into my bones and let them swarm beneath me, that I may rest in the day of tribulation, that I may go up to our girded 1127Bpeople. This is the sense: now we all willingly suffer straits, and at thy threatening we tremble with all our bowels; now my lips tremble, and the dread of a trembling mind is marked upon my mouth; and not only this, but of my own accord I seek and of my own accord I desire yet another thing: let rottenness enter into my bones and swarm above me — that is, I willingly suffer what Job suffered; I desire that not only my flesh but the marrow of my bones also should waste away, and that my bed should swarm with the rottenness of the body and with worms without number, so that, after I shall have borne these things here for my sins, I may rest in the bitter day, in the day of tribulation, in the day of need and of straitness; and that I may go up to our girded people, that is, to a strong warrior and a fighter. And beautifully, "I shall go up," 1127Cfor one always goes up to a girded people; and elegantly, "our," for he who has been afflicted and has willingly borne oppressions, and has weighed the rewards to come against present evils, boldly says "our", so that, like Abraham and Isaac and Jacob, he too may sleep in a good old age, full of days, and be laid unto his fathers. Another edition:

« Thou hast cut off in alienation the heads of the mighty; the nations shall be moved therein. » For "alienation" it is written in the Greek « in ecstasy, » which some have interpreted « in stupor, » others « in an excess of mind »: but whether it be called stupor, or alienation, or excess of mind, it signifies one and the same thing — when a man, troubled and stupefied by a sudden marvel, is rendered a stranger to the sense of his own mind. And that this befell the Jews 1127Dthe frequent history of the Gospel declares, saying that they were stupefied and wondered at the doctrine and the mighty works of Jesus, saying: « Whence hath this man all these things, whose father and mother we know (Joan. VI)? » And in the Acts of the apostles, when the lame man at the gate of the temple had been healed by Peter and John: « They were filled, he says, with stupor and ecstasy (Act. III); » in which ecstasy, that is, in wonder or alienation of mind, many of the people were provoked to believe in the Lord; but the heads of the mighty, that is, the chief priests and the elders, were cut off from the lot of the faithful by not believing. The nations also were moved therein, when, having heard or seen the mighty works of the Lord and of his apostles, they were so stupefied and amazed that, anathematizing 1128Aand casting away those whom they had worshipped as gods, they received with devout mind the new faith of Christ.

« They shall open wide their mouths as a poor man eating in secret. » For as a poor man who has remained fasting for some while, if perchance he finds food anywhere, busies himself to refresh himself with it at once in secret, and will not bring it forth in public, lest perhaps it be snatched away by another and he himself perish of hunger: so assuredly, so did the peoples of the nations, when the bread of the word was offered them through the apostles — from which they had continued fasting a longer while — straightway open the mouth of their heart, and begin to taste it with all avidity. And in what order the poverty of the nations came to receive the banquets of the word is intimated, when it is straightway added:

« Thou hast sent into the sea horses troubling many waters. » 1128BFor by the sea he means the world; by the horses of God, the holy preachers; by the many waters, the peoples of the nations. And when the horses of God were sent into the sea, many waters were troubled, because when the heralds of the word were scattered into the world the hearts of the nations were troubled — some to believe and to receive the sacrament of faith, but others to gainsay, or even to persecute the heralds of that same faith. And well is it said above of these horses, namely of the holy preachers, Because ascending thou wilt ascend upon thy horses; and now of the same it is added, Thou hast sent thy horses into the sea; so that from both sentences it may be gathered that the Lord so sent preachers into the world that he himself is never absent from those same preachers, but, as a charioteer over his horses, so is he always set over their minds to govern them.

1128C« My belly kept watch and was terrified at the voice of the prayer of their lips. » By his belly, after the manner customary to the prophets, he means his own mind; because as by the belly food is received whereby the strength and life of the body is restored, so pious thoughts are received in the holy mind whereby the life of the inner man, lest it should fail, is sustained and held together. I kept watch, he says, over myself the more carefully with trembling mind, lest perchance I should sin in heart, and lest, preaching to others, I myself should be made a reprobate. And it is to be noted that he says he was terrified at the voice of the prayer of his own lips, whereas he seems to have prayed nothing at all in this whole song, but only, fearing and quaking, to describe the sacraments of Christ and of the Church that were to come. Nor yet is he deceived who calls his song a prayer, who also set over it 1128Dsuch a title, The prayer of Habakkuk the prophet for ignorances; because whatsoever a holy man speaks, all this assuredly is a prayer to God: everything that you do, whose sincere intention is to please the Lord, this intercedes for him with the Lord, and commends him to the Lord.

« And trembling entered into my bones. » Just as Sicu by the name of flesh Scripture sometimes designates our carnal actions, so by the word of bones it is wont to designate strong and spiritual deeds. My heart therefore was terrified, he says, at these things which I foresee as coming into the world; and whatsoever I thought there was of spiritual strength in me, all this trembled as a frail thing, while I look upon the greater strengths and passions of Christ and of the blessed apostles — 1129Awhich very thing is more manifestly explained by the following word, when it is said:

« And beneath me my strength was troubled. » And well does he say that his strength was troubled not in himself but beneath himself, because the prophet, caught up to the contemplation of heavenly secrets, saw himself in a manner lifted above himself; and the loftier he is made by the light of contemplation, the more does he behold his own imperfection: for being raised up to gaze upon things above, he is rightly troubled concerning the things which he had done among the lowest. And the strength of the prophet was troubled, his bones trembled, his belly was terrified, not only because he knew himself less perfect in action, but also because he had learned that all who would live godly in Christ should suffer persecutions. And he saw that Christ himself, who should enter into the world without sin, 1129Bshould not go forth from the world without the punishment of sin.

« I shall rest in the day of my tribulation, that I may go up to the people of my transmigration. » For he rests in the day not only of retribution but also of tribulation, because he doubts not that through temporal afflictions he shall increase his eternal joys, according to that word of the Apostle: « For by hope we are saved (Rom. VIII). » And again: « Rejoicing in hope, patient in tribulation (Rom. X). » And this is the rest of the elect in this life: that, the appetite of the lowest things being left behind, with the whole intention of the mind and with the daily steps of good works they may strive to ascend and to pass over to the fellowship of those who had gone before them in Christ. But even before that last and general judgment the saints rest in the day of tribulation, ascending 1129Cto the people of their transmigration, when after good works, being translated from the world, they are joined in the heavens to the joys of the just who went before, while their persecutors, likewise taken away from this life, undergo the torments of gehenna, to be afflicted perpetually. And the day of tribulation can be understood also in this life, when, the penury of temporal goods growing heavy, they who had loved such things grieve exceedingly, as though surrounded with miseries; but every elect man, though bearing those same discomforts bodily, with the hope of his mind fixed in the Lord, has rest, knowing that the more heavily he is pressed down among the lowest things, the more loftily after his oppressions he ascends to the eternal fellowships of the citizens above. To which sense that which follows fitly agrees:

« For the fig tree shall not blossom, and there shall be no bud in 1129Dthe vines; the work of the olive shall lie, and the fields shall bring no food; the flock shall be cut off from the fold, and there shall be no herd in the stalls. » LXX: « Because the fig tree bringeth not forth fruit, and there shall be no generation in the vines; the work of the olive shall lie, and the fields make no meats; the sheep have failed from meat, and there shall be no oxen in the stall. » According to the Hebrew, in which we said above, let rottenness enter into my bones and swarm beneath me, that I may rest in the day of tribulation, that I may go up to my girded people, thus will the things that went before be joined to those that follow: therefore have I willed to bear tribulation for the present, and afterwards to go up to the strong people, because the day of tribulation and of need shall come; and while the rest are set in straitness, I shall rejoice in thy 1130Amajesty: the fig tree shall not blossom, and there shall be no bud in the vines; the work of the olive shall lie, and the fields shall bring no food, and the rest — which, because they differ not much from the LXX, let us discuss together with their interpretation. When the day of tribulation shall have come, and I shall have gone up to the people which once went on pilgrimage with me — or assuredly, when the day of the overthrow of the Jewish nation and of the former people shall have come, and the daughter of Sion shall have been left as a tabernacle in a vineyard, and as a lodge in a cucumber field, and as a city that is stormed — while that one indeed perishes I have been chosen for the people of which it is said: « And unless the Lord had left us seed, we had been as Sodom, and we should have been like unto Gomorrha (Rom. IX). » I will join myself to the disciples of Christ, whom, because he teaches them on the mountain, the crowds and the feeble 1130Bbeing left below, I too will climb up to the mountains. But these verses, if anyone ask what they figuratively signify, can be expounded thus: the fig tree, the vine and the olive were the synagogue of the Jews, when, devoted to God, it brought forth the sweetness of good working, the blazing of fervent love, the fatness of a merciful mind. Sheep and oxen were typical in that same people: sheep, namely, in those who humbly heard the voice of the chief shepherd; but oxen in those who, diligently bearing the yoke of the law to bring forth the fruits of good works, prepared the hearts of their hearers by sedulously teaching and chastising them, as by ploughing the land of the Lord; and to these, living spiritually, the most broad fields of the divine Scriptures made spiritual meats, in whose pasture he delighted who had been made as a beast of burden before God 1130Cand always clung to him, saying: « The Lord ruleth me, and I shall want nothing: in a place of pasture, there hath he set me (Psal. XXII). » But this fig tree, when the Lord came to it a third time — that is, in the giving of the law through Moses, in sedulous rebuke and exhortation through the prophets, in the offering of grace through himself — neglected to bear the fruit of virtue; for which cause, at his curse, it was condemned with everlasting dryness. The generation failed in the vines that were once the Lord's, that is, the fruit of charity in the multitudes of the Jews; for which cause they offered him vinegar for wine when he thirsted — that is, to him who sought in them the sweetness of the virtues they brought forth the sharpness of vices. The work of the olive lied, when that same people fattened the heads of wretched men with the oil of flattery, and re-echoed with a false mouth the truthful things drawn from the prophet, saying: « But I, 1130Das a fruitful olive tree in the house of God, have hoped in the mercy of God (Psal. LI). For which cause, at the time of the last retribution, being about to bring lamps extinguished, it is to be shut out with its own darkness from the entrance of the heavenly country. The fields make no meats, when that same people, though the pages of the divine letters lie open, is not able, by understanding rightly, to find the pastures of truth. The sheep fail from meat, because for those from whom the refreshment of inward sweetness is absent, there is nothing whence the innocence of a simple life may come forth. And thus it is said, the sheep have failed from meat, that is, because meat was lacking — as also the Prophet in the psalm: « And my flesh, he says, is changed for oil (Psal. CVIII), » that is, because there was no oil wherewith I might be refreshed or anointed. Finally certain codices 1131Ahave it thus, they failed, because the sheep did not eat: there are no oxen in the stall, since indeed among the Jews the stalls of the heavenly letters abound, but because they do not relish in them the fodder of heavenly understanding, those who would bear the sweet yoke of the Gospel are wanting. All which things the Prophet, considering that they should come upon the faithless part of his own nation, straightway shows what he himself would do together with the faithful of that same nation — nay rather, together with the fellowship of the whole Church, which was to be gathered throughout the world in Christ, or had been gathered — saying:

« But I will rejoice in the Lord, and I will exult in God my Jesus. » Another edition: « But I will glory in the Lord, I will rejoice in God my Jesus. » But I, he says, will glory not in my own justice, but in the faith of the divine protection; I will rejoice in God my Jesus, 1131BI who weigh that salvation is not mine in me, but is in him. And, as though we should ask of him why he gloried in the Lord and in God Jesus — whom by the grace of a great love he called his own — and why he rejoiced, straightway, intimating as it were the most just cause of that same joy, he thus ended his song.

« The Lord God is my strength, and he will set my feet as the feet of harts, and upon my high places he will lead me, singing in the Psalms, to the victor. » [Vulg. gives the nominative here, i.e. "he shall lead me, as conqueror, singing in psalms"] The Lord God, he says, is my strength: I shall have no other strength at all, save in Christ, and I shall reckon all the justices of the law as refuse; and he will set my feet as the feet of harts, that I may tread upon the asp and the basilisk, and as a little child may put [my hand] into the hole and draw out the serpent Judas from the snake. For my cousin 1131Cis like to a roe or to a young hart; and because he himself is a hart, he granted to me also that I should be a hart, lofty in horns, dividing the hoof, chewing the cud, and that at my scent the serpents should flee: of which it is said in the psalm: « Who hath made my feet perfect as a hart's, and will set me upon high places (Psal. XXVIII). » And again: « The voice of the Lord perfecting the harts. » He will therefore set my feet among the rest of his harts, and will lead me through to heavenly things, that among the angels I may sing the glory of the Lord and may announce upon earth peace to men of good will. And I shall sing his victory, and his triumph, and the trophy of the cross. But according to another edition this prophetic sentence is read thus.

« O Lord God, my strength, set my feet unto consummation; and he will establish me upon high places, 1131Dthat I may conquer in his brightness. » As though he said openly: beneath me indeed my strength was troubled — that is, while I look upon the condition of human frailty which is below; and while I lift up the eyes of my mind to the grace of divine help, I trust that in him I can do valiantly. He is able to lead the steps of my works through unto the consummation of a firm end; he is able to establish me upon high places: so that, namely, by the contemplation of everlasting goods I may despise as of no worth all the loftiness of worldly power, and may conquer in his brightness all the temptations which meet me whether from the adversities or from the blandishments of the world — that is, while in all that I do I seek not my own glory, but his, from whom I remember that I have received whatsoever good 1132AI do. For deservedly are they helped by the Lord, so that, temptations being conquered, they may come approved to the palm of the heavenly calling, who refer the whole cause of their victory to his praise. Certain codices have, « that I may conquer in his Canticle, » which looks to one and the same sense. For he conquers in the Canticle of the Lord whosoever, in all the tribulations which he suffers, knows how to give him thanks from the heart, knowing that « to them that love God all things work together unto good (Rom. VIII). » Yet in this sentence of the prophet there can be, according to the history, such a sense as if he himself said: I shall conquer in song, and my hands shall compose the cithara and the psaltery and every kind of instrument, and I shall write a panegyric to him that triumphs; and I who at the beginning said, « How long shall I cry, and thou wilt not 1132Bhear? shall I cry out to thee, suffering violence, and thou wilt not save? » and who pleaded concerning justice and judgment, shall afterwards praise equity and shall surpass the other singers by my song. And beautifully does it answer to the beginning of this prophetic song: for he who, having heard and considered the works of the Lord appearing in the flesh, faithfully fears and quakes — it comes to pass that, despising those things which in this life are borne along in changing state after the fashion of a fluctuating sea, he glories and rejoices in him alone.

INCIPIT CANTICUM LIBRI DEUTERONOMII.

Legitur in Deuteronomio, quod Moyses, postquam conscripsit verba legis in volumine atque complevit, 1132Clocutus sit, audiente universo coetu Israel, verba carminis hujus et ad finem usque compleverit. Unde conjici datur carmen istud, quod in testimonium filiis Israel Moyses decantavit, non simplicem tantummodo historiam sonare, sed spirituali intelligentia plenissimum esse. Partim enim secundum historiam Israelitarum pro beneficiis opulentissime sibi a Domino collatis exprobrat perfidiam: partim per allegoriam de mysteriis coelestibus sanctam Dei instruit Ecclesiam: partim autem ad honestatem morum unumquemque fidelium provocat per tropologiam, unde sobrium lectorem decet, ut in historia servet Scripturae veritatem. In allegoria vero consideret mysteriorum coelestium rationem, et in tropologia bonarum disciplinarum ediscat utilitatem, quatenus 1132Dtripartita hac distinctione edoctus ad illam coelestem theoriam, hoc est, ad inspectionem secretarum rerum et ad contemplationis divinae provehatur sublimitatem, nec non et illud diligenter inspiciendum est, quod dicitur loqui Moyses verba carminis et ad finem usque complesse, si forte hoc admoneat, ut qui ore Deum laudare studeat, operibus non contradicat: non est enim pulchra laudatio in ore peccatoris. Ideo ipse Dominus in Evangelio ait - « Qui solverit unum de mandatis istis minimis et docuerit sic homines, minimus vocabitur in regno coelorum, qui autem fecerit et docuerit, hic magnus vocabitur in regno coelorum (Matth. XIX). » Principium autem hujus carminis tale est:

1133A« Audite, coeli, quae loquar; audiat terra verba oris mei. » Juxta primordium Isaiae prophetae ita scriptum est: « Audi, coelum, et auribus percipe, terra, quia Dominus locutus est (Isa. I). Supra ergo in eodem libro, quis Propheta, cujus filius, qui contra Judam et Hierusalem, sive per Judam et Hierusalem, et quo tempore viderit titulo demonstratum est, nunc coelum et terram ad audiendum provocat, in coelo supernas significans angelicasque virtutes, in terra mortalium genus, metonymic ω s ab his quae continent ea, quae continentur ostendens. Nam quia per Moysen testes vocaverat Dominus coelum et terram dans populo Israel legem suam, et dixerat: Attende, coelum, et loquar; audiat terra verba oris mei, » post praevaricationem populi eosdem rursus in testimonium vocat, 1133But cuncta elementa cognoscant juste Deum in ultione mandatorum suorum ad iracundiam concitatum.

« Concrescat in pluviam doctrina mea, fluat ut ros eloquium, quasi imber super herbam et quasi stillae super gramina, quia nomen Domini invocabo. » Alia editio: « Exspectetur sicut pluvia eloquium meum, et descendant sicut ros verba mea, sicut imber super germen, et sicut nix super fenum, quia nomen Domini invocavi. » Eloquium legis Dei comparat pluviae ac rori, quia germina spiritualium fructuum a fidelium cordibus expetit, ipsa enim sunt vinea Domini, unde ille fructum sibi amabilem, hoc est, bonorum operum quaerit. Huic similem comparationem in Isaia propheta invenimus: « Quomodo, inquit, descendit imber et nix de coelo, et illuc ultra 1133Cnon revertetur, sed inebriat terram et infundit eam, et germinare eam facit, et dat semen ferenti et panem comedenti: sic erit verbum meum, quod egredietur de ore meo, non revertetur ad me vacuum, sed faciet quaecunque volui et prosperabitur in his ad quae misi illud (Isa. LV). » Verum quia Moyses doctrinam suam in pluviam concrescere dixit, ut patenter, quae haec pluvia esset, demonstraret, subjunxit dicens, « quia nomen Domini invocabo. » Nomen ergo Domini est invocare, Evangelium Christi praedicare, quod qui audit et facit, boni operis fructum in coelis sine dubio possidebit.

« Date magnificentiam Deo nostro, Dei perfecta sunt opera, et omnes viae ejus judicia. » Alia editio: « Date magnitudinem Deo nostro. Deus, vera opera 1133Dejus et omnes viae ejus judicia. » Magnificare Dominum est majestatem Salvatoris corde credulo, ore pio simul cum bonis actibus praedicare. Unde Maria mater Domini in cantico Evangelii ait: « Magnificat anima mea Dominum, et exsultavit Spiritus meus in Deo salutari meo (Luc. I). » « Dei, inquit, perfecta sunt opera. » Quae ergo illa sunt? audi Moysen in Geneseos dicentem: « Igitur perfecti sunt coeli et terra et omnis ornatus eorum, complevitque Deus die septimo opus suum, quod fecerat et requievit die septimo ab universo opere suo, quod patrarat (Gen. II). » Opus ergo Dei est redemptio humani generis, quam quando perfecit, Sabbatum spirituale in sepulcro requiescens nobis ostendit. « Omnes, inquit, 1134Aviae ejus judicia, » quia omnia mandata ejus vera. Unde Psalmista ait: « Lex Domini irreprehensibilis convertens animas, testimonium Domini fidele sapientiam praestans parvulis (Psal. XVIII). » Et item: « Judicia, inquit, Dei vera, justificata in semetipsa, desiderabilia super aurum et lapidem pretiosum multum, et dulciora super mel et favum (Ibid.). » Et alibi: « In labiis, ait, meis pronuntiavi omnia judicia oris tui (Psal. CXVIII). »

« Deus fidelis et absque ulla iniquitate justus et rectus. » Alia editio: « Deus fidelis et non est in eo iniquitas, justus et sanctus Dominus. » Fidelis est, quia quod promisit, procul dubio implevit. Absque ulla iniquitate, quia aequitas summa iniquitatis consortium nullo modo recipit. Justus, quia peccantibus 1134Bet non poenitentibus pro malis suis retribuet poenam. Rectus, quia digne operantibus et bene merentibus vitam reddet perpetuam. Sanctus quia in omnibus judiciis et actibus suis est probatissimus: sanctus enim et pium significat et patientem, et bonorum omnium largitorem, quoniam quidquid ab illo efficitur, virtutum laude completur.

« Peccaverunt ei non filii ejus in sordibus. » Alia editio: « Peccaverunt non ei filii commaculati. » Quomodo non filii Dei, nisi quia non ex fide: « Quotquot autem receperunt eum, dedit eis potestatem filios Dei fieri his qui credunt in nomine ejus (Joan. I). » Unde Dominus Judaeis fidem ejus recipere nolentibus et doctrinam illius spernentibus, ait: « Vos ex patre diabolo estis et desideria patris vestri vultis 1134Cfacere; ille homicida fuit ab initio et latro. » Et Stephanus ad eosdem Judaeos ait: « Patres vestri quem prophetarum non sunt persecuti? et occiderunt eos, qui praenuntiabant de adventu justi, cujus vos nunc proditores, et homicidae fuistis, qui accepistis legem in dispositione angelorum et non custodistis. » Qui ergo incorrigibiliter peccat, filium Dei se non esse probat. Illa autem editio, quam septuaginta interpretes ediderunt, ita habet: « Peccaverunt non ei filii vituperabiles. » Quod quidam ex Graeco interpretati sunt, filii commaculati; quidam, filii otiosi. Unde non magna quaestio, imo nulla est. Sed illud merito ad quaerendum movet, si generaliter dictum est, peccaverunt non ei filii, quoniam qui peccat, non Deo peccat, id est, non Deo nocet, sed sibi. Quomodo intelligendum 1134Dsit quod in psalmo legitur: « Tibi soli peccavi (Psal. L). » Et in Jeremia: « Tibi peccavimus patientia Israel, Domine (Jerem. XIV). » Et iterum in psalmo: « Sana animam meam, quoniam peccavi tibi (Psal. XL). » Et utrum hoc sit peccare Deo, quod peccare in Deum, unde et Heli sacerdos ait: « Si in Deum peccaverit, quis orabit pro eo (I Reg. II). Peccare in Deum est in his peccare, quae ad Dei cultum pertinent, nam hoc Deus legitur dixisse ad Abimelech de Sara: « Propterea peperci tibi, ne peccares in me (Gen. XX). » Peccare autem Domino vel potius peccasse Domino, nisi forte alicubi scriptum occurrat, quod huic sensui resistat, hi mihi videntur non immerito dici, qui condignam poenitentiam 1135Apeccati sui non agunt, ut glorificetur Deus ignoscens, causam quippe reddens David cur dixerit: « Tibi soli peccavi et malum coram te feci (Psal. L). » Subjecit et dicit: « Ut justificeris in sermonibus tuis, et vincas, cum judicaris, » quia cunctis recte sentientibus, liquido apparet, quod Dominus est justus in omnibus viis suis, et sanctus in omnibus operibus suis: « Nec vult mortem peccatoris, sed ut convertatur et vivat (Psal. XL). » Similiter et quod a David dictum est: « Sana animam meam, quoniam peccavi tibi. » Hoc idem agitur, ut Deus ignoscendo glorificetur, quia magna est ejus misericordia super confitentes sibi et redeuntes ad se. In hoc autem Cantico praevidebat Propheta futuros quosdam, qui sic fuerunt peccaturi, Deum offendendo 1135Bmagnis iniquitatibus suis, ut nec poenitentiam agere vellent, et ad Deum redire, ut sanarentur; de quibus etiam dicitur: « Quoniam caro sunt et Spiritus ambulans et non revertens, » ideo dixit non filios esse Dei, quia incorrigibiles exstiterunt.

« Generatio prava atque perversa, haeccine reddis Domino? » Alia editio: « Quia generatio prava et perversa haec Domino retribuisti. »

Simili elogio eorum perfidiam Salvator in Evangelio denotavit, qui tentantibus et signum a se quaerentibus respondit dicens: « Generatio prava et perversa signum quaerit et signum non dabitur ei, nisi signum Jonae (Matth. XII). » Sic et Joannes Baptista turbis Judaeorum ad se venientibus ait: « Genimina viperarum, quis ostendit vobis fugere 1135Cab ira ventura? facite ergo fructus dignos poenitentiae et ne velitis dicere, patrem habemus Abraham. Potens est enim Deus de lapidibus istis suscitare filios Abrahae. » Generatio quippe Judaeorum prava, et perversa semper fuit, quae ingrata beneficiis Dei exstitit, nec ei pro hoc condignas laudes, imo e contrario semper transgressiones varias idolorum sordes atque irritationes rependebat.

« Popule stulte et insipiens, nunquid non ipse est Pater tuus, qui possedit te, et fecit et creavit te? » Alia editio: « Sic plebs fatua et non sapiens, nonne hic ipse est tuus Pater, possedit te, fecit te et creavit te. » Pater universorum et conditor secundum naturae potentiam Deus est unus, quia ab ipso et per ipsum et in ipso sunt omnia, creavit ergo 1135DDeus hominem, quando materiam, unde formari posset, condidit: fecit, quando illum ad imaginem et similitudinem suam formavit, possedit, quando expulsis erroribus per fidem et gratiam, suam sibi vindicavit. Populus ergo stultus et insipiens fuit Judaeorum gens, quae nec creatorem suum et factorem, quem lex sibi data manifestissime ostendit, digno cultu venerabatur; nec ejus beneficii memor, quo illum in Aegypto per miracula atque prodigia de manu inimicorum eruens specialiter possedit, dans illi de coelo manna, et de petra aquam, nec non ducatum ei in mari Rubro, atque in deserto per angelicum ministerium praebuit, hoc est, in columna nubis per diem, et in columna ignis per noctem; 1136Ased haec generatio prava et perversa pro tantis bonis non condignas grates Domino suo rependit: quin potius idola sibi fabricavit, atque illis divinum honorem et sacrificia stultissime impendit dicens: « Hi sunt dii tui, Israel, qui eduxerunt te de terra Aegypti (Exod. XII). »

« Memento dierum antiquorum, cogita generationes singulas, interroga patrem tuum, et annuntiabit tibi; majores tuos, et dicent tibi. » Alia editio: « In mente habete dies saeculi, intelligite annos, nationes nationum, interroga patrem tuum, et annuntiabit tibi; seniores tuos, et dicent tibi. » In memoriam eis reducit facta antiqua, jubens eos interrogare patres et ab eis discere qualiter in praeteritis saeculis et generationibus divina ope sunt liberati, 1136Batque saepius ab inimicis protecti, quatenus vel hoc modo ad poenitentiam peccatorum provocentur, et ab omni errore se exuendo ad veritatem appropinquare otius meditentur: patres enim hic non quilibet hebetes et stulti, sed doctores legis et praedicatores Evangelii mystice intelligendi sunt, quorum doctrinis et exemplis potest homo ad intelligentiam divinam proficere, unde Apostolus ad Corinthios scribens ait: « Nam si decem millia paedagogorum habeatis in Christo, sed non multos patres. Nam in Christo Jesu per Evangelium ego vos genui (I Cor. IV). » Et ad Hebraeos: « Patres, inquit, quidem carnis nostrae habuimus eruditores et reverebamur eos: non multo magis obtemperabimus patri Spirituum et vivemus et illi quidem in tempore paucorum dierum, 1136Csecundum voluntatem suam erudiebant nos: hic autem ad id, quod utile est in recipiendo sanctificationem ejus (Hebr. XII). »

« Quando dividebat Altissimus gentes, quando separabat filios Adam, constituit terminos populorum juxta numerum filiorum Israel. Pars autem Domini populus ejus, Jacob funiculus haereditatis ejus. » Alia editio: « Cum dividerit Excelsus gentes, quemadmodum dispersit filios Adae, statuit terminos gentium secundum numerum angelorum Dei, et facta est pars Domini populus ejus Jacob funiculus haereditatis ejus Israel. » Historiae enim ordo rationem dat de humani generis distributione, quia cum multitudo hominum tanta sit, ut eorum numerositas humana computatione comprehendi non possit, 1136Dsolos filios Israel de tanta multitudine in suam haereditatem Dominus elegit, ubi nomen suum et potentiam suam notam faceret et voluntatis suae secreta per legis judicia et prophetarum scripta manifestaret, sicut superius in praesenti libro Moyses testatus est dicens (Deut. VII): « Te elegit Dominus Deus tuus, ut sis ei populus peculiaris de cunctis populis, qui sunt super terram; non quia cunctas gentes numero vincebatis, vobis junctus est Dominus et elegit vos, cum omnibus sitis populis pauciores, sed quia dilexit vos Dominus et custodivit juramentum quod juravit patribus vestris, eduxitque vos in manu forti et redemit de domo servitutis, de manu Pharaonis regis Aegypti, ut scias quia Dominus Deus 1137Atuus, ipse est Deus fortis et fidelis, custodiens pactum et misericordiam diligentibus se, et his qui custodiunt praecepta ejus in mille generationes, et reddens odientibus se statim, ita ut disperdat eos et ultra non differat protinus eis restituens quod merentur. Custodi ergo praecepta et caeremonias atque judicia, quae ego mando tibi hodie ut facias. » Et haec secundum Hebraicam veritatem ita intelligi possunt. Secundum aliam autem editionem, ubi legitur, statuit terminos gentium secundum numerum Angelorum Dei et in Danielis visione, ubi Angelus de principe Persarum sibi resistenti Prophetae narravit, videtur aliud nobis insinuare: Angelorum enim ministeria super gentes singulas, et super homines singulos ordinata creduntur, quatenus sub eorum 1137Bregimine atque defensione humana natura juxta dispensationem omnipotentis Dei custodiatur atque ab inimicis tueatur. Unde Dominus in Evangelio de parvulis non scandalizandis docens, mox subjunxit dicens: « Angeli eorum in coelis semper vident faciem Patris mei, qui in coelis est (Matth. XII). » Quia enim superna illa civitas ex angelis et hominibus constat, ad quam tantos credimus ex hominum genere conscendere, quantos illic contigit electos angelos remansisse sicut scriptum est, « statuit terminos gentium secundum numerum angelorum Dei: » debemus et nos aliquid ex illis distinctionibus supernorum civium ad usum nostrae conversationis trahere, nosque ipsos ad incrementa virtutum bonis studiis inflammare. Quia enim tanta illic ascensura 1137Ccreditur multitudo hominum, quanta multitudo remansit Angelorum; superest, ut ipsi quoque homines, qui ad coelestem patriam redeunt ex eis agminibus aliquid illuc revertentes imitentur; distinctae namque conversationes hominum, singulorum agminum ordinibus congruunt, et in eorum sortem per conversationis similitudinem deputantur. Pars autem Domini populus est Jacob spiritualis, et funiculus haereditatis ejus Israel verus, Ecclesia videlicet Catholica, de qua per Psalmistam dicitur: « Funes ceciderunt mihi, in praeclaris - etenim haereditas mea praeclara est mihi (Psal. XV). »

« Invenit eum in terra deserta, in loco horroris et vastae solitudinis. Circumduxit eum et docuit et custodivit quasi pupillam oculi sui. » Alia editio: 1137D« Sufficientem eum sibi fecit in eremo in siti caloris, ubi non erat aqua. Circumduxit eum et erudivit eum et custodivit eum sicut pupillam oculi. » Invenisse Dominus Israelem dicitur in loco horroris, et vastae solitudinis: quia in monte Sina legem dans, pactum cum eo inivit, manna de coelo illis distribuit, et aquam de petra produxit, coturnicum carnes dedit, omnem sufficientiam eis praebens in alimentis juxta id quod sibi videbatur eis competere. Et in eadem eremo quadraginta annis eos circumducendo praecepta sua docuit, sed illud merito quaeritur, quomodo dicit eos protexisse, sicut pupillam oculi sui, cum plurimi eorum prostrati sint in solitudine, nisi forte dicamus, tandiu eos protectos ac 1138Acustoditos esse, quandiu mandata Dei servaverunt; cum autem ea transgressi sunt, statim plagis percussi atque ab inimicis devastati sunt. Mystice autem Dominus Israel in deserto invenit, cum Ecclesiam de mundi errore tulit, ubi horror idololatriae et nulla mentio Dei fuit. Circumducit ergo Dominus Ecclesiam, cum in universis mundi partibus eam dilatando in viam mandatorum suorum dirigit, et per Spiritum sanctum voluntatem suam ei indicans, ubique eam plena custodia, quod per pupillam oculi designatur, contra haereticos atque persecutores custodit. Sequitur alia comparatio:

« Sicut aquila provocans ad volandum pullos suos et super eos volitans expandit alas suas et assumpit eos atque portavit in humeris suis. » Alia editio: 1138B« Sicut aquila tegit nidum suum, et super pullos suos consedit, expandit alas suas, et accepit eos et suscepit eos super scapulas suas. » Narrant physici quod aquila, quae ab acumine oculorum est vocata, tam acuti intuitus sit, ut cum super maria immobili penna feratur, nec humanis pateat obtutibus, de tanta sublimitate pisciculos natare in aqua videat, ac tormenti instar descendens raptam praedam pennis ad littus pertrahat; nam et contra radium solis fertur obtutum non flectere, unde et pullos suos ungue suspensos radiis solis objicit, et quos viderit immobilem tenere aciem, ut dignos genere conservat; si quos vero inflectere obtutum videt, quasi degeneres abjicit; dicuntur et aquilae pullos suos cum vident in nidis plumescere, alis percutere, et ad volandum 1138Ceos de nido provocare; nidificant autem in petris et arboribus, in quarum nido lapis echites aedificatur, quem aliqui vocaverunt gaietem, qui ad multa remedia utilis, nihil igne deperdens: narrant autem iidem naturae inquisitores, quod aquilae non tam obeant senio vel aegritudine, quam fame, in tantum superiore crescente rostro, ut aduncitas aperiri non queat; sed cum inde viderit sibi periculum imminere, obtundit rostrum in petra durissima, donec iterum in pristinum usum revertatur. Et inde mystico sermone in psalmo scriptum est, dicente Propheta ad animam fidelem: « Renovabitur sicut aquilae juventus tua (Psal. CII). » Per aquilae similitudinem secundum litteram ostenditur, quod Dominus populum suum provocando ad terram Chanaan 1138Dbellis possidendam juvit, et adjuvando ab inimicis protexit. Secundum mysticum vero sensum aquila haec Dominum Salvatorem significat, qui aquilae similitudinem in eo tenet, quod praedam ab hoste maligno morte sua eripuit, et post resurrectionem suam carnem, quam pro humani generis redemptione suscepit, ad alta coelorum provexit, nos dictis atque exemplis exhortans, quatenus illuc sequamur fide, tendamusque devotione, ubi ipse ascendit corpore, unde et in Evangelio ait: « Ubi fuerit corpus, illic congregabuntur aquilae (Luc. XVII). » Alia etiam exempla, quae de aquila diximus, possunt similiter ad ipsum et ad filios ejus figuraliter transferri. Nam sicut aquila acuti intuitus est, ita ut super maria 1139Aimmobili penna sublata, nec humanis apparens aspectibus pisciculos de tanta sublimitate natantes videns in pelago praedando raptat et ad littus pertrahit, sic Redemptor noster super omnes coelos exaltatus, et ab humanis aspectibus corporaliter sublatus, in pelago tamen istius mundi majestatis suae intuitu videt, quos per praedam salutiferam cruat, atque ad littus aeternae beatitudinis amoris sui fune attrahat. Unde et ipse in Evangelio ait: « Et ego si exaltatus fuero a terra, omnia traham ad me ipsum (Joan. XII). » Pullos suos, hoc est, filios per gratiam baptismatis sibi renatos radiis solis aeterni per contemplationem objicit, et quos viderit constantem in intuendo habere aciem mentis, seu dignos suo genere perpetualiter servat: si quos vero ad ima et ad 1139Bcarnalia desideria obtutum mentis reflectere conspicit, hos, quasi degeneres abjicit: in petris autem et arboribus haec aquila nidificat, quia quos firmos fide et fructificantes virtutibus aspicit, in eis spiritualis germinis fecunditatem parit, ibique gemma illa atque margarita pretiosissima nascitur, quam sacer Evangelii textus narrat negotiatorem spiritualem omnibus pecuniis suis venditis sibimet comparare. Quod autem aquila rostrum superfluum in petra tandit, atque ita priorem modum reducit, significat quod sancti viri quidquid in conversatione sua superfluum, ac sibi noxium conspiciunt allidendum ad petram Christum confringunt, atque semet per gratiam et per studium bonum renovantes ad pristinum officium habiles reddunt.

1139C« Dominus solus dux ejus fuit, et non erat cum eo Deus alicnus. » Alia editio: « Dominus solus docebat eos et non erat cum eis Deus alienus. » Manifestum est quod unus et solus Deus Israel de Aegypto liberavit, et non idola falsa, quae coluerunt Aegyptii, sic et in ereptione nostra de spirituali Aegypto non alius, quam idem Deus qui olim Israelitas eripuit, pater videlicet, et filius, et Spiritus sanctus unus et trinus magnus et omnipotens Deus, qui per incarnationem Jesu Christi Domini nostri salutis nostrae efficit mysterium, unde cognoscant haeretici, quam miserabiliter a vera fide deviantes errent, cum se per schismata ab Ecclesia Catholica sequestrant, qui quot erroribus, tot idolis obnoxii sunt

1139D« Constituit eum super excelsam terram, ut comedat fructus agrorum. » Alia edito: « Adduxit eos in fortitudinem terrae, cibavit eos nascentias agrorum. » Excelsam terram Palestinam vocat, quia ad comparationem terrae Aegypti, quae plana et irrigua est, montuosa et aspera videtur; quae fortitudinem habet, quia non sterilis, sed uberrima est ad proferendos fructus terrae in saturitatem habitatorum illius. Aliter, excelsa terra sancta est Ecclesia, quae virtutibus et scientia sublimis est valde, et bonorum operum germine fertilissima. Ibi fideles quique constituti comedunt fructus agrorum spiritalium, quia ibi germinat bonum semen verbi Dei, aliud tricesimum, aliud sexagesimum, aliud centesimum 1140Afructum, ut unusquisque pro capacitate sua cibum sufficientem sibi inveniat.

« Ut sugeret mel de petra et oleum de saxo durissimo. » Alia editio: « Suxerunt mel de petra, et oleum de firma petra. » Sed nusquam tale aliquid juxta historiam legitur, si tota Testamenti Veteris recenseatur, nusquam de petra mel, nusquam oleum de firma petra, vel saxo durissimo populus ille suxit: sed quia juxta Pauli vocem: « Petra erat Christus (I Cor. X). » Mel de petra suxerunt, qui ejusdem Redemptoris nostri facta et miracula viderunt. Oleum vero de firma petra suxerunt, qui effusione Spiritus sancti post resurrectionem ejus ungi meruerunt. Quasi ergo in firma petra mel dedit, quando adhuc mortalis Dominus miraculorum suorum dulcedinem 1140Bdiscipulis ostendit. Sed firma petra oleum fudit, quia post Resurrectionem suam factus jam impassibilis per afflationem Spiritus donum sanctae unctionis emanavit. De hoc quoque oleo per prophetam dicitur: « Computrescet jugum a facie olei (Isa. X), » sub jugo quippe tenebantur daemoniacae dominationis, sed uncti sumus oleo Spiritus sancti, et quia nos gratia libertatis unxit, dominationis daemoniacae jugum putruit.

« Butyrum de armento et lac de ovibus cum adipe agnorum et arietum filiorum Basan, et hircos cum medulla tritici et sanguine uvae. » Alia editio: « Butyrum boum et lac ovium cum adipe agnorum et arietum filiorum taurorum et hircorum cum adipe renium, tritici et sanguine uvae. » Haec licet juxta 1140Chistoriam fertilitatem terrae Chanaan insinuent, tamen altiori sensu gratiae Christi demonstrant abundantiam, quam sancta Ecclesia munere largitoris sui accepit. Butyrum de armento tulerunt, qui plenitudinem fidei de exemplis patriarcharum didicerunt, et lac de ovibus ceperunt, qui simplicitatem mentis, sive facilitatem doctrinae de innocenti vita Patrum ad aedificationem sui sumpserunt, et haec cum adipe agnorum et arietum, hoc est, cum pinguedine charitatis apostolorum et eorum, quos ipsi ad fidem imbuerant, ad refectionem mentis suae perceperunt; arietum enim nomine apostoli et apostolicae dignitatis viri designantur, quia ipsi duces sunt gregis Dei. Unde dicitur in psalmo: « Afferte Domino, filii Dei, afferte Domino filios arietum (Psal. 1140DXXVIII). » Quod autem subjungitur, « filiorum Basan, » ad eumdem pertinet sensum, quia Basan pinguedo interpretatur, filii enim pinguedinis sunt, qui disciplinis Novi Testamenti instituti sunt, ubi scientiae multitudo et virtutum magnitudo consistit. Potest et illud, quod in alia editione legitur, « filiorum taurorum, » ad personam similiter referri prophetarum et apostolorum, nec non et aliorum sanctorum doctorum, quia illi duces sunt gregis Dei, et armentis spiritualibus praesunt, ex quorum videlicet doctrinis ipsa accipiunt semen verbi, undo foetus bonorum possint proferre operum: nec sufficiebant haec dona, nisi adderentur et hirci. Hirci enim figuram poenitentium habent, quia pro peccato 1141Ain lege offerebantur: non enim satis est dona virtutum habere fidelibus, nisi poenitentiam agant pro suis excessibus, unde ille, qui rex simul et propheta fuit, et cui de incarnatione Christi promissum est, quod ex ejus semine nasceretur, pro suis delictis Dominum deprecatur dicens: « Amplius lava me ab injustitia mea, et a delicto meo munda me, quoniam iniquitatem meam ego agnosco et delictum meum coram me est semper (Psal. L). » Et ut haec omnia beneficia, quae praedicta sunt, eminentiore dono accumularentur, addidit, medullam tritici et sanguinem uvae. Quid enim per medullam tritici, nisi corpus Christi, quid per sanguinem uvae, nisi calix Novi Testamenti in Christi sanguine figuratur? Plurimis enim donis ex pietate Dei ecclesiastico populo 1141Bconcessis hoc inter omnia excellit; quod corporis et sanguinis sui sacramenta in panis et vini confectione ad salutem aeternam illi ministrasit. Sequitur:

« Biberat meracissimum, incrassatus est dilectus et recalcitravit. » Alia editio: « Bibit vinum et manducavit Jacob, et satiatus est, et recalcitravit dilectus. » Nempe universa dona Dei, sine fece alicujus nequitiae seu improperii, credentibus et digne de eis sentientibus conferuntur. Sed quia sicut carnales quondam Israelitae donis Dei abusi sunt, et ex beneficiis largissimus pejores facti, ad idololatriam prolapsi sunt; ita, proh dolor! non pauci de ecclesiastico populo ingrati beneficiis Dei ad vitia relabuntur, et hoc quod sibi ad salutem collatum est, 1141Cin perditionem suam convertunt. Unde subjungitur:

« Incrassatus, impinguatus et dilatatus dereliquit Deum factorem suum, et recessit a Deo salutari suo. Provocaverunt eum in diis alienis, et abominationibus suis ad iracundiam concitaverunt, immolaverunt daemoniis et non Deo, diis, quos ignorabant, novi recentesque venerunt ad eos, quos non coluerunt patres eorum. » Alia editio: « Pinguis factus est et incrassavit, dilatatus est, dereliquit Deum, qui fecit eum, et recessit a Deo salutari suo, exacerbaverunt me in alienis, in abominationibus suis concitaverunt me, sacrificaverunt daemoniis et non Deo, diis quos non noverunt, novi 1141Drecentes venerunt ad eos, quos nesciebant patres eorum. » Hoc veteres Israelitae historialiter fecerunt, sicut in eorum libris sufficienter expressum est, sed et moderni Israelitae, quando dona Dei in scientia et virtutibus sibi ad divinum obsequium concessa ad haeresim sive hypocrisim convertunt, idolis et non Deo servire probantur. Daemoniis utique cultum exhibendo et non Deo, quod patres nostri, hoc est, patriarchae, prophetae et apostoli atque evangelistae nequaquam fecerunt, inde evenit istis idololatris, quod clementissimum Dominum malis suis irritantes, quem propitium habere possent, si bene egissent, propter mala opera severum atque districtum perpessuri sint.

« Deum qui te genuit, dereliquisti, et oblitus es 1142ADomini creatoris tui. » Alia editio: « Deum qui te genuit dereliquisti, et oblitus es Deum alentem te. » Genuit Dominus quodammodo Israelitas per legem et circumcisionem, genuit et ecclesiasticum populum per baptismi gratiam, per sanctificationem Spiritus sancti, et alimentum coelestis cibi, hoc est, evangelicae doctrinae illi sufficienter praebuit; sed qui haec oblivisci non timet, juste sibi iratum Deum per vindictam sentiet. Unde subjungitur:

« Vidit Dominus, et ad iracundiam concitatus est, quia provocaverunt eum filii sui et filiae, et ait: Abscondam faciem meam ab iis et considerabo novissima eorum. » Alia editio: « Vidit Dominus, et zelatus est, et exacerbatus est propter iram filiorum suorum et filiarum, et dixit: Avertam faciem 1142Bmeam ab eis et ostendam quid erit eis in novissimo. » Nihil gravius est in poenis, quam praesentia et visione Dei privari. Inde Psalmista Dominum deprecatur dicens: « Ne projicias me a facie tua (Psal. L). « Et item: » Ne avertas, inquit, faciem tuam a me. » Hinc et Job ad Dominum loquitur dicens: « A facie tua ne abscondar (Job XIII). » Quia ergo filii et filiae, hoc est, utriusque sexus homines irritantes eum pro malis suis, quae faciunt, ejus misericordiam non merentur; sed ab illo ingrati spernuntur in novissimis eorum. Hoc probabitur, quando in poenas gehennae projicientur aeternas, hoc est enim considerare novissima eorum, manifestare tormenta illorum quae ipse eis, non conturbato animo, sed tranquillo, juste impendit.

1142C« Generatio enim perversa est et infideles filii ipsi provocaverunt me in eo, qui non erat Dominus, et irritaverunt in vanitatibus suis, et ego provocabo eos in eo, qui non est populus, et in gente stulta irritabo illos. » Alia editio: « Quia generatio prava et perversa est filii in quibus non est fides in ipsis, ipsi in zelo compulerunt me et non in Deo, in ira concitaverunt me in idolis suis, et ego in zelo expellam eos et non in gentem, in gente insipiente irritabo eos. » Quia ergo Israelitae abjecto cultu Domini sui idola coluerunt, et deserentes verum ac vivum Deum, deservierunt vanitati simulacrorum gentibus, qui populi Dei nomine necdum eo tempore vocari meruerunt, in direptionem traditi sunt. Similiter et ecclesiastici, qui deserta veritate 1142Dad errores declinaverunt, si pro malis suis non poenituerint malignis spiritibus, qui superbiendo perdiderunt angelicam dignitatem ad trudendum inferni carceribus tradentur. Bene ergo daemones gens stulta dici possunt, quia oderunt justitiam et amant iniquitatem.

« Ignis succensus est in furore meo et ardebit usque ad inferni novissima. » Alia editio: « Quia ignis exarsit ab ira mea, et ardebit usque ad inferos deorsum. » De his quos damnant flagella, et non liberant scriptum est: « Percussisti eos, nec doluerunt; attrivisti eos et renuerunt accipere disciplinam (Jer. V). » His flagella ab hac vita inchoant, et in aeterna percussione perdurant. Unde quoque 1143Aper Moysen Dominus dicit: « Ignis exarsit ab ira mea et ardebit usque ad inferos deorsum (Deut. XXXII). » Quantum ad praesentem etenim percussionem spectat, recte dicitur: « Ignis exarsit ab ira mea. » Quantum vero ad aeternam damnationem, apte hoc subditur, et ardebit usque ad inferos deorsum, licet a quibusdam dici soleat illud quod scriptum est: « Non vindicat Deus bis in idipsum. » Qui tamen hoc, quod per Prophetam de iniquis dicitur, non attendunt, et duplici confusione contere eos, et id quod alias scriptum est. Jesus populum de terra Aegypti salvans, secundo eos, qui non crediderunt perdidit, quibus tamen si consensum praebeamus, quamlibet culpam bis ferire non posse, hoc ex peccato percussis atque in peccato suo morientibus 1143Bdebet aestimari, quia eorum percussio hic coepit, illic finitur, ut in incorrectis unum flagellum sit, quod temporaliter incipit, sed in aeternis suppliciis consummatur, quatenus eis, qui omnino corrigi renuunt, jam praesentium flagellorum percussio sequentium sit initium tormentorum. Sequitur:

« Comedit terram et nascentia ejus, et montium fundamenta comburet, congregabo super eos mala, et sagittas meas complebo in eis. Consumentur fame, et devorabunt eos aves morsu amarissimo, dentes bestiarum immittam in eos cum furore trahentium super terram atque serpentium, foris vastabit eos gladius et intus pavor, juvenem simul ac virginem, lactantem cum homine sene. » 1143CAlia editio: « Comedit terra nascentias agrorum, et concremabit fundamenta montium, congeram in ipsos mala, et sagittas meas consummabo in eis, tabescentes fame et esca erunt avium, et extensio dorsi insanabilis, dentes bestiarum immittam in eos, cum furore trahentium super terram, a foris sine filiis privabit eos gladius et in promptuariis eorum timor, juvenes cum virgine, lactans cum stabilito sene. » Comedit, inquit, terram et nascentia ejus: terram tunc comedit et nascentia ejus, cum carnem peccatricem propter opera libidinis flamma aeternae ultionis comburet. Nascentia enim carnis proprie opera sunt concupiscentiae carnalis: « Quae sunt fornicationes, immunditiae, impudicitiae, luxuriae, idolorum servitus, veneficiae, inimicitiae, contentiones, 1143Daemulationes, irae, rixae, dissensiones, sectae, invidiae, homicidia, ebrietates, comessationes et his similia, quae qui agunt, » ut Paulus testatur, » « regnum Dei non consequentur (Gal. V). » Et montium fundamenta comburet, cum superborum iniqua consilia, quibus se impune posse peccare credebant, aeternis cruciatibus consumet, congregabit super eos mala, quando non solum mala opera, sed et cogitationes perversas atque prava pro quibus puniantur in memoriam adducet verba, sagittas suas in eis complebit, quando iram indignationis suae per varia tormenta ostendet. Consumentur fame, hoc est, non cibi nec potus, sed perpetua bonorum operum sterilitate. Et devorabunt eos aves morsu amarissimo, 1144Amaligni videlicet spiritus peccata praeterita improperantes ore lacerant saevissimo. Quod autem additur, post devorationem avium extensio dorsi insanabilis, significat plagas acerrimas, quas patiuntur peccatores in tortura daemonum, qui eos sine ulla miseratione affligunt, et poenas validas in inferno solvere cogunt, bestias atque serpentes iidem maligni spiritus sunt, qui bestiae dicuntur propter ferocitatem, serpentes propter calliditatem, trahunt bestiae atque serpentes reprobos dentibus super terram cum furore, quando hoc, quod prius blandiendo carnalibus persuaserunt, ut seducerent postmodum furibundo animo a peccatoribus expetunt, ut crucient, foris vastabit impios gladius, et intus pavor; quando in extrema ultione undique corpus 1144Bignis aeterni torquet exustio, et intus mentem angustiae opprimit magnitudo, unde scriptum est: « Vermis eorum non morietur, et ignis non exstinguetur (Marc. IX). » « Juvenem, inquit, simul ac virginem, lactantem cum homine sene. » Enumeratis universis aetatibus ostendit nulli aetati in extrema ultione esse parcendum, quos idem idololatriae foedaverat cultus. Allegorice autem in juvene lascivum et libidinosum: in virgine a bonis operibus sterilem, in lactante hebetem, in sene pigrum et inutilem ostendit, hos ergo omnes justa vindicta Dei in extrema ultione consumet, quos nunc commonitio et correptio a peccatis non cohibet.

« Et dixi: Ubinam sunt, cessare faciam ex hominibus memoriam eorum. » Alia editio: « Et 1144Cdixit: Disperdam eos, privabo autem ex hominibus memoriam eorum. » Interrogatio Dei non est ignorantia, sed correptio. Hujus sententiae expletionem facile dignoscere potest, qui Judaeos videt a terra repromissionis ejectos, et per totum orbem dispersos. Generaliter autem omnibus peccatoribus et impoenitentibus hoc futurum imminet, quod de sanctorum terra tollantur, et memoria eorum in coetu electorum non numeretur. Unde per Psalmistam capiti omnium iniquorum simul cum toto corpore poena praedicitur futura, cum supputatis singulis speciebus vitiorum subinfertur: « Propterea destruet te Deus in finem, evellet te et emigrabit te de tabernaculo suo et radicem tuam de terra viventium (Psal. LI). »

1144D« Sed propter iram inimicorum distuli ne forte superbirent hostes eorum, et dicerent, manus nostra excelsa et non Deus fecit haec omnia. » Alia editio: « Nisi propter iram inimicorum, ne longo tempore sint super terram, ne consentiant adversarii et dicant: Manus nostra excelsa, et non Deus fecit haec omnia. » Ideo enim Dominus saepe peccantem populum diu tolerando sustinuit, ne gentes eis confines nomen et potentiam ejus blasphemarent. Unde et Moyses ad Dominum pro peccato Israel indignantem et eas disperdere volentem ait: « Ne, quaeso, Domine, dicant Aegyptii, callide eduxit eos, ut interficeret in montibus et deleret e terra; requiescat ira tua et esto placabilis super nequitiam 1145Apopuli tui, recordare Abraham, Isaac, et Israel servorum tuorum, quibus jurasti per temetipsum dicens: Multiplicabo semen vestrum sicut stellas coeli, et universam terram hanc, de qua locutus sum, dabo semini vestro, et possidebitis eam semper, placatusque est Dominus, ne faceret malum, quod locutus fuerat ad universum populum suum. » Similiter et nos quando peccaverimus propter magnam patientiam suam et longanimitatem sustinet nolens perire quemquam, sed omnes salvos fieri et ad agnitionem veritatis pervenire, juxta illud Pauli apostoli, quod ad Romanos scribens ait: « Existimas autem hoc, o homo, qui judicas eos qui talia agunt et facis ea, quia tu effugies judicium Dei, an divitias bonitatis ejus et patientiae et longanimitatis contemnis? 1145BIgnorans quoniam benignitas Dei ad poenitentiam te adducet, secundum duritiam autem tuam et impoenitens cor thesaurizas tibi iram in die irae et revelationis justi judicii Dei (Rom. II), » etc. Ac ne forte maligni spiritus glorientur in perditione nostra de fortitudine sua, quasi victoriam viribus suis percepissent, cum in hoc semper certent, ut sententiam Altissimi pervertant, qui homines decrevit locum perditorum spirituum in coelis possidere.

« Gens absque consilio est et sine prudentia, utinam saperent et intelligerent, ac novissima providerent. » Alia editio: « Quia gens perdita consilio et non est in eis, disciplinam non sapuerunt intelligere, percipient in futuro tempore. » Gens Judaeorum, nec non et omnium impiorum sine consilio 1145Csalutis sunt, quia in futurum sibi providere nesciunt, quatenus imminentem pro peccatis suis evadant vindictam et infernalium poenarum aeternum effugiant tormentum, et quoniam disciplinabiliter vivere negligunt, tribulationem et angustiam in futuro sustinebunt, juxta illud, quod de eis scriptum est (Prov. I): « Cum irruerit super eos repentina calamitas, et interitus quasi tempestas ingruerit, quando venerit super eos tribulatio et angustia, tunc invocabunt me et non exaudiam, mane consurgent et non invenient me, eo quod exosam habuerint disciplinam et timorem Domini non susceperint. »

« Quomodo persequitur unus mille, et duo fugant decem millia. Nonne ideo, quia Deus suus vendidit eos, et Dominus conclusit illos? » Alia editio: 1145D« Quomodo persequitur unus mille, et duo transmovebunt dena millia, nisi quia Dominus subdidit eos et Deus tradidit illos. » Quicunque magnitudinem Domini veraciter agnoscit, interitum suorum non ex infirmitate potentiae ipsius, sed ex permissione justi judicii ejus evenire manifeste probabit: quia nullo modo fieri posset quod unus persequeretur mille, et duo fugarent decem millia, nisi Dominus eos tradidisset, et exosos projiciendo in manus inimicorum concluderet.

« Non est enim Deus noster ut Deus eorum. Et inimici nostri sunt judices. » Alia editio: « Qui non est Deus noster, sicut dii illorum, inimici autem nostri insensati. » Dominus enim Deus noster verax 1146Aet justus est, idola autem gentium falsa et inutilia: quod etiam non solum nostri, verum etiam nostrorum approbant adversarii. Unde saepius legitur, quod hostibus Judaei traditi pro infidelitate atque injustitia sua merito ab eis arguerentur, sicut Jeremias in lamentatione sua dicit: « Viderunt eam hostes et deriserunt sabbata ejus (Thren. I). » Hinc enim nobis summopere cavendum est, ne hostes nostri, qui per errorem idololatriae merito dicuntur insensati, malis nostris provocati in blasphemiam contra Dominum erumpant, cum viderint nos superatos, et ad nihilum propter peccata nostra redactos: hoc virtuti suae adscribant, quod non sua potentia, sed Domini judicio peractum est.

« De vinea enim Sodomorum vinea eorum, et de 1146Bsuburbanis Gomorrhae, uva eorum, uva fellis, et botrus amarissima. » Alia editio: « Ex vinea enim Sodomorum vitis eorum, et propago eorum ex Gomorrha, uva eorum, uva fellis, botrus amaritudinis ipsis. » Saepe in Scripturis sacris synagoga vel Hierusalem Sodomae et Gomorrhae comparantur, quia sicut illae male abusi sunt donis Dei; sic et istae beneficiis Dei semper exstiterunt ingrati. Unde Isaias de illis ait: « Nisi Dominus exercituum reliquisset nobis semen, quasi Sodoma fuissemus, quasi Gomorrha similes essemus, audite verbum Domini, principes Sodomorum, percipe auribus legem Dei nostri, populus Gomorrhae, etc. (Isa. I). » Vinea enim Domini, sicut in eodem propheta scriptum est, domus 1146CIsrael fuit, quae tunc in fellis amaritudinem conversa est, quando Christi mortem meditabatur. Unde et ipsi in cruce pendenti acetum felle mistum arundine porrexerunt, ut in hoc demonstraretur scientiam legis, quam ipsi rectam acceperunt, felle malitiae suae habere corruptam, cum datorem legis ad se venientem recipere noluerunt, insuper irritaverunt et cruci affixerunt. Aliter hac sententia et haereticorum nequitia percutitur, qui licet gratiae spiritualis a sanctis praedicatoribus purum dogma primitus acceperunt, ipsi tamen erroribus suis illud maculare non pertimescebant et ideo tota doctrina eorum in fellis, et in absinthii amaritudinem conversa est.

« Fel draconum vinum eorum, ut venenum aspidum insanabile. » Alia editio: « Furor draconum 1146Dvinum eorum, et furor aspidum insanabilis. » Apte Judaeorum perfidia et haereticorum versutia draconum felli et aspidum veneno comparantur, quia sicut serpentes amaritudinem et venenum in se occultant, quatenus incautos improvise laedant, sic et Judaei atque haeretici malitiam suam dolo et fraudibus velant, quatenus imprudentes facilius exstinguant. Bene ergo dicitur: « Venenum aspidum insanabile, » quia haereticorum error quemcunque invaserit, nisi se inde liberaverit, sine dubio in aeternum perimit.
