Quod autem juxta aliam editionem legitur: « Furor draconum vinum eorum, et furor aspidum insanabilis, » ostendit non sanae esse mentis, sed magis insanientis, vitam aeternam spernere et mortem perpetuam 1147Aeligere, veritatem Evangelii relinquere, et errorum fallaciam potius sequi velle.

« Nonne haec condita sunt apud me, et signata in thesauris meis? » Alia editio: « Nonne haec omnia congregata sunt apud me et signata in thesauris meis? » Moris est humani generis, ut praeterita cito obliviscatur, et quae aliquando grandia videbantur, spatio temporis labente simul ipsa in recordatione deficiant; sed non ita est apud Deum, ubi praeteritum et futurum non est, sed praesens, semper adest; quae enim nobis praetereunt, ibi reservantur, nec oblivioni tradentur, inde necesse est, ut peccata quae dudum commisimus, instanti poenitentia delere coram oculis Dei studeamus, ne forte in futuro, quando tempus erit vindictae reputet nobis debita 1147Bnostra et dicat: Haec fecisti et tacui, existimasti iniquitatem, quod essem tibi similis: arguam te et statuam illa contra faciem tuam.

« Mea est ultio et ego retribuam eis in tempore, ut labatur pes eorum. » Alia editio: « In die ultionis reddam illis in tempore, quo lapsus fuerit pes eorum. » Certa sunt apud Dominum et tempus et modus vindictae, quo peccatores, si se a peccatis non correxerint, torquendi sunt. Sed quia multae miserationes ejus sunt, « praeoccupemus faciem ejus in confessione, et in psalmis jubilemus ei (Psal. XCIV). » Humiliemus in jejunio animas nostras, vigilemus et oremus, quoniam quis scit si convertatur et ignoscat Deus et relinquat post se benedictionem?

« Juxta est dies perditionis et adesse festinant 1147Ctempora. » Alia editio: « Quia prope est dies perditionis eorum, et haec sunt parata vobis. » Juxta est unicuique dies ultionis, quia certus est, nec diuturnum esse potest, quod aliquando finitur. Quidquid enim temporale est, ut est hominis aetas, cursus praesentis vitae, si ad aeternitatem comparatur, parvum fit in ejus comparatione, ideo dicitur, « et adesse festinant tempora, » quia cito finiuntur temporalia, et parata iniquis adveniunt tormenta, juxta id, quod scriptum est: « Parata sunt derisoribus judicia (Prov. XIX). » Secundum historiam autem Israelitarum vindicta appropiavit, quia ex quo rebellare coeperunt, nunquam perfecte destiterunt, donec regnum, locum et vitae prosperitatem pariter perdiderunt, nam postquam Prophetas occiderunt et Salvatorem 1147Dcrucifixerunt seditionibus antiquis nova superadjicientes, Romanos rebellando in se provocarunt, qui urbe destructa, templo succenso, populo fame et gladio necato, reliquias eorum per totum orbem disperserunt, ubi incertis sedibus vagando spe futurorum manent incerti.

« Judicabit Dominus populum suum et in servis suis miserebitur. » Alia editio: « Quia judicabit Dominus populum suum et in servis suis consolabitur. » Judicabit ergo Dominus populum suum, quando electos suos a reprobis secernit, nam aliquando judicare pro discernere; aliquando vero pro damnare accipitur, unde in psalmo Propheta ad Dominum dixit: « Judica me, Deus, et discerne causam meam 1148Ade gente non sancta; ab homine iniquo et doloso eripe me (Psal. XLII). » Hic judicare pro discretione ponitur; alibi autem idem propheta ad Dominum clamans ait: « Judica, Domine, nocentes me, expugna impugnantes me (Psal. XXXIV). » Sed hic judicium pro damnatione positum est, judicabit enim Dominus sanctos suos, quando eos in die judicii segregans a peccatoribus collocabit in dextera sua: hostes autem eorum et persecutores a sinistris statuet, ut damnationem certam cum diabolo et angelis ejus in inferno recipiant, et ibunt hi in supplicium aeternum: justi autem in vitam aeternam, tuncque perfecte erit, quod in praedicto versu subsequitur: « Et in servis tuis miserebitur. » Quia in coelesti beatitudine fidelibus suis misericordiam praestabit 1148Baeternam. Quod autem, juxta aliam editionem, Dominus legitur in servis suis consolari, metonymice dictum est, ubi per id quod efficitur, id quod efficit demonstratur, nam Dominus juxta naturam semper laetus et tranquillus est, nec unquam in tristitiae moerorem commutatur, de quo scriptum est: « Tu, Domine, qui cum tranquillitate judicas et disponis omnia (Sap. XII). » Bene ergo dicitur Dominus in servis suis consolari, qui electos suos post finem tristitiae praesentis aeterna pace et gaudio consolatur. Potest et iste versus ad Judaeorum conversionem ultimam referri, quando miserante Deo populo suo post plenitudinem gentium ad fidem subintrantem omnis Israel per gratiam divinam ad eamdem fidem conversus salvus fiet.

1148C« Videbit quod infirmata sit manus, et clausi quoque defecerunt, residuique consumpti sunt, et dicet, ubi sunt dii eorum, in quibus habebant fiduciam, ex quorum victimis comedebant adipes et bibebant vinum libaminum, surgant et opitulentur vobis, et in necessitate vos protegant. » Alia editio: « Vidit enim eos fatigatos et defectos in abductione et dissolutos, et dixit: Ubi sunt nunc dii illorum, in quibus confidebatis in ipsis, quorum adipem sacrificiorum edebatis, et bibebatis vinum libationis eorum, exsurgant nunc et juvent vos et fiant vobis protectores. » Videbit, inquit, quod infirmata sit manus cum videre fecerit quod opera malorum, quae fecerunt in idololatria vel caeteris transgressionibus, infirma fuerint, quando ad nihilum redacta, nihil eis qui ea 1148Dfecerant profuerint, et clausi quoque defecerunt, hoc est, sive Judaei ad captivitatem ab inimicis ducti ibidem consumpti sunt, vel omnes impii in potestatem daemonum traditi ultra non praevalebunt, residuique consumpti sunt, quia ab hac vita exeuntes, non habebunt ulterius potestatem atque facultatem aliquid faciendi. Idcirco in Ecclesiaste scriptum est: « Quodcunque potest manus tua facere instanter operare, quia nec opus, nec ratio, nec sapientia, nec scientia erunt apud inferos, quo tu properas (Eccle. IX). » Quod autem subjungitur: « Ubi sunt Dii eorum, in quibus habebant fiduciam, » et reliqua, significat, quod idololatrae nullum adjutorium in die necessitatis habeant ab idolis, quibus antea cultum exhibuerant, 1149Ased nec mundi amatores, qui per avaritiam, quae est idolorum servitus, pecunias in mundo congregaverant, qui deliciis atque luxuriae deservierant, quorum Deus venter erat et gloria in confusionem ipsorum, qui terrena sapiebant, nihil secum conferunt de universo labore suo, quando pro peccatis suis ad tartarum a malignis spiritibus pertrahuntur.

« Videte, quia ego sum solus, et non sit alius Deus praeter me. » Alia editio: « Videte, videte, quoniam ego sum Deus, et non est praeter me. » Haec admonitio generaliter ad omnes homines pertinet, ut, relicta falsitate idolorum, atque deceptione haereticorum, spretisque omnium iniquitatum sordibus, ad unum et solum Deum Patrem, et Filium, et Spiritum sanctum per fidem catholicam colendum 1149Bconvertantur, eumque ex toto corde, tota anima totaque virtute diligant et mandata ejus atque judicia diligenter conservent, quatenus regni coelestis haeredes effici mereantur.

« Ego occidam et vivere faciam, percutiam et ego sanabo, et non est, qui possit de manu mea eruere. » « Alia editio: Ego occidam, vivere faciam, percutiam et ego sanabo, et non est qui eripiat de manibus meis. » Duobus modis omnipotens Deus vulnerat, quos reducere ad salutem curat, aliquando enim carnem percutit, et mentis duritia in suo pavore tabescit, vulnerando ergo ad salutem revocat, cum electos suos affligit exterius, et interius vivificat. Recte ergo per Moysen loquitur dicens: « Ego occidam et vivere faciam, percutiam et ego sanabo. » 1149COccidit enim, ut vivificet; percutit, ut sanet, quia idcirco foras verbera admovet, ut intus vulnera infligat, quia mentis nostrae duritiam suo desiderio percutit, sed percutiendo sanat, quia terroris sui jaculo transfixos ad sensum nos rectitudinis revocat, corda enim nostra male sana sunt, cum nullo Dei amore sauciantur, cum peregrinationis suae aerumnam non sentiunt, cum erga infirmitatem proximi, nec quolibet minimo affectu languescunt, sed vulnerantur, quia amoris sui spiculis mentes Deus insensibiles percutit, moxque has sensibiles per ardorem charitatis reddit.

« Levabo ad coelum manum meam et dicam: Vivo ego in aeternum. » Alia editio: « Quia tollam in coelum manum meam, et jurabo per dexteram meam, 1149Det dicam: Vivo ego in aeternum. » In coelum manum levare, sive attollere est potentiam aeternitatis suae super omnia excellentem ostendere: jurare per dexteram, est, per filium, qui dextera Dei nominatur, promissa ipsius in conspectu hominum confirmare, et aeternitatem suam credentibus sibi per Evangelium revelare, quia potestas ejus potestas est aeterna, et regnum ejus, quod non corrumpetur.

« Si exacuero ut fulgur gladium meum, et arripuerit judicium manus mea, reddam ultionem hostibus meis et qui oderunt me retribuam. » Alia editio: « Quia exacuam velut fulgur gladium et agit judicium manus mea et retribuam judicium inimicis et his qui oderunt me reddam. » Exacuere, ut fulgur 1150Agladium Dei, est, repentinam vindictam in hostibus exercere: et arripere judicium manui divinae est, per justam retributionem singulis secundum propria merita reddere, sicut scriptum est: « Filius enim hominis venturus est in gloria Patris sui cum angelis suis, et tunc reddet unicuique secundum opera sua (Rom. II). » Et Apostolus: « Omnes, inquit, stabimus ante tribunal Christi, ut recipiat unusquisque propria corporis prout gessit sive bonum, sive malum (II Cor. V). »

« Inebriabo sagittas meas sanguine, et gladius meus manducabit carnes. » Sagitta enim hominem, dum non attendit, percutit, et dum illam non praevidet, subito interimit, sagittae Domini sunt celeres vindictae, quae licet modo occultae sint coram oculis 1150Biniquorum, subito tamen manifestabuntur in damnatione perditorum, et Dei gladius carnes comedit, quia in extremo judicio ejus sententia eos, qui carnaliter sapiunt, occidit.

« De cruore occisorum et de captivitate nudati inimicorum capitis. » Alia editio: « A sanguine vulneratorum a captivitate, a capite principum inimicorum. » Ad hoc, quod dicit, de cruore inimicorum subauditur illud, quod praemissum est: « Inebriabo sagittas meas. » Inebriat Dominus sagittas suas in cruore occisorum, quod ultionem exercet in turba inimicorum, tuncque nudabuntur capita hostium, quando vana esse probabuntur consilia iniquarum mentium, quibus se impune posse peccare confidebant, possunt et capita iniquorum magistri accipi 1150Cerrantium, in quos ultio novissima redundat, quia qui fuerunt causa peccatorum, sentient damna in retributione poenarum. Unde in Apocalypsi scriptum est: « Et exiit sanguis de lacu usque ad frenos equorum (Apoc. XIV), » hoc est, processit ultio tormentorum usque ad rectores populorum, usque enim ad diabolum et ejus angelos novissimo certamine exiit ultio sanguinis sanctorum effusi, sicut scriptum est: « In sanguine peccasti, et sanguis te persequetur (Ezech. XXXV). » Notandum vero quod hoc loco in editione LXX interpretum quaedam sententiae interponuntur, quae in Hebraica veritate non inveniuntur hoc modo.

« Laetamini, coeli, simul cum eo, et adorent eum omnes angeli Dei. Laetamini, gentes, simul cum populo 1150Dejus et confirment eum omnes filii Dei. » Hae tamen ex canonica auctoritate rite prolatae esse probantur, quando ex his Paulus apostolus testimonia sumere reperitur. Nam in Epistola, quae ad Hebraeos ab ipso scripta est, nec non et in illa quae ad Romanos similiter ab eo directa est, inde testimonia assumpsisse invenitur. Denique ad Hebraeos scribens sic infert: « Et cum iterum introducit primogenitum in orbem terrarum, dicit, Et adorent eum omnes angeli Dei (Hebr. I). » Ad Romanos vero de gentium convocatione et satietate Judaicae plebis disputans sic ait: « Dico enim Christum Jesum ministrum fuisse circumcisionis propter veritatem Dei ad confirmandas promissiones Patrum (Rom. XV). » Gentes autem 1151Asuper misericordia honorare Deum, sicut scriptum est: « Propter hoc confitebor tibi in gentibus, Domine, et nomini tuo cantabo (II Reg. XXII). » Et iterum dicit: « Laetamini, gentes, cum plebe ejus (Rom. XV). » Unde verum constat, quod apostoli et illam editionem quae ex Hebraica veritate est, nec non et illam quae ex LXX interpretibus, aequaliter probaverunt, cum ex utraque testimonia sumpserunt. Illud vero, quod ad supernam creaturam quasi exhortando loquitur dicens, « Laetamini, coeli, simul cum eo, et adorent eum omnes angeli Dei, » ostendit, quod post adventum Salvatoris in carne et judicium ejus universale, quo justi coronantur et peccatores damnantur, coelestes et terrestres creaturae simul in laudem sui coadunantur Conditoris, et regem Christum super 1151Bomnia regnantem atque dominantem adorant et venerantur in aeternum. Quod autem sequitur: « Laetamini, gentes, simul cum populo ejus et confirmenteum omnes filii Dei, » idem est, quia Ecclesia Christi ex Judaeis et gentibus congregata, postquam in resurrectione carnis immortalitatem et incorruptionem veraciter percipit, hoc confirmat facto, quod ante testificabatur verbo. Sequitur.

« Laudate, gentes, populum ejus, quia sanguinem servorum suorum ulciscetur et vindictam retribuet in hostes eorum. » Alia editio: « Quia sanguis filiorum ejus defenditur, et defendit, et retribuam judicium inimicis, et his qui oderunt eum reddet. » Laudabunt omnes gentes populum Dei, quando viderunt gloriosam apparere in conspectu Conditoris 1151Csui Ecclesiam Dei, et persecutores ejus pro effuso a se sanguine martyrum, et omnibus malis quae in hac vita gesserunt, per malignorum spirituum ministerium retrudi in gehennam ignis aeterni. Tunc enim et boni bonum quod vident laudabunt, quia praemium placet, quod habebunt, et impii in poenis constituti, licet sero, pro malo quod gesserunt poenitebunt, cum viderint gloriam sanctorum clarescere, quam non speraverunt: « Gementes enim in angustiis constituti dicent intra se poenitentiam agentes: Hi sunt, quos aliquando habuimus in derisum et in similitudinem improperii, nos insensati vitam illorum aestimabamus infamiam et finem illorum sine honore. Quomodo ergo computati sunt inter filios Dei et inter sanctos sors illorum est? (Sap. V.)

1151D« Et propitius erit Dominus terrae populi sui. » Alia editio: « Et emundabit terram populi sui. » Emundabit ergo Dominus terram populi sui, sive propitius erit terrae populi sui, quando Ecclesiam suam, quam hactenus regebat in adversis per ministros suos ab omnibus scandalis erutam atque purgatam perpetua pace fruentem vivere facit in prosperis regni coelestis, ubi sine fine gaudebit, et laudes Domino simul cum sanctis in aeternum cantabit.

HERE BEGINS THE CANTICLE OF THE BOOK OF DEUTERONOMY.

It is read in Deuteronomy that Moses, after he had written out the words of the law in a volume and had finished it, 1132Cspoke, in the hearing of the whole assembly of Israel, the words of this song, and completed it even to the end. Whence it is given us to infer that this song, which Moses sang for a testimony to the children of Israel, does not sound a simple history only, but is most full of spiritual understanding. For in part, according to the history, it reproaches the treachery of the Israelites in return for the benefits most richly conferred upon them by the Lord; in part, through allegory, it instructs the holy Church of God concerning the heavenly mysteries; in part, moreover, it calls forth every one of the faithful to uprightness of morals through tropology. Whence it befits a sober reader that in the history he keep the truth of Scripture; but in the allegory let him consider the reason of the heavenly mysteries, and in the tropology let him learn thoroughly the usefulness of good disciplines, so that, 1132Dtaught by this threefold distinction, he may be carried forward to that heavenly theoria — that is, to the inspection of hidden things and to the sublimity of divine contemplation. And this too must be diligently looked into, that Moses is said to speak the words of the song and to have completed it even to the end — whether perhaps this admonishes us that he who is zealous to praise God with his mouth should not contradict it by his works: for praise is not comely in the mouth of a sinner. Therefore the Lord himself says in the Gospel - « He that shall break one of these least commandments and shall so teach men, shall be called least in the kingdom of heaven; but he that shall do and teach, he shall be called great in the kingdom of heaven (Matth. XIX). » Now the beginning of this song is as follows:

1133A« Hear, O heavens, the things that I shall speak; let the earth hear the words of my mouth. » According to the opening of the prophet Isaiah it is thus written: « Hear, O heaven, and give ear, O earth, for the Lord hath spoken (Isa. I). Above, therefore, in the same book, it has been shown by the title who the Prophet is, whose son, who [prophesied] against Juda and Jerusalem, or through Juda and Jerusalem, and at what time he saw; now he calls heaven and earth to hear, signifying by heaven the supernal and angelic powers, by earth the race of mortals, showing metonymic ω s from those things which contain the things which are contained. For since through Moses the Lord had called heaven and earth as witnesses when he gave his law to the people of Israel, and had said: Give heed, O heaven, and I will speak; let the earth hear the words of my mouth, » after the people's transgression he calls the same again to testimony, 1133Bthat all the elements may know that God was justly stirred to wrath in the avenging of his commandments.

« Let my doctrine gather thick into rain, let my speech flow as the dew, as a shower upon the herb and as drops upon the grasses, for I will call upon the name of the Lord. » Another edition: « Let my speech be looked for as the rain, and let my words come down as the dew, as a shower upon the sprout, and as snow upon the hay, for I have called upon the name of the Lord. » He compares the speech of the law of God to rain and to dew, because it seeks from the hearts of the faithful the sprouts of spiritual fruits; for these are the vineyard of the Lord, from which he seeks a fruit lovely to himself, that is, the fruit of good works. A comparison like to this we find in the prophet Isaiah: « As, he says, the shower and the snow come down from heaven and return no more 1133Cthither, but water the earth and drench it, and make it to spring, and give seed to him that beareth [Vulg. serenti, "to him that soweth"] and bread to him that eateth: so shall my word be, which shall go forth out of my mouth; it shall not return to me void, but shall do whatsoever I have willed, and shall prosper in those things for which I sent it (Isa. LV). » But because Moses said that his doctrine gathers thick into rain, in order that he might plainly show what this rain was, he subjoined, saying, « for I will call upon the name of the Lord. » To call upon the name of the Lord, therefore, is to preach the Gospel of Christ; and he who hears it and does it will without doubt possess in the heavens the fruit of a good work.

« Give magnificence to our God; the works of God are perfect, and all his ways are judgments. » Another edition: « Give greatness to our God. God — true are his works, 1133Dand all his ways are judgments. » To magnify the Lord is to preach the majesty of the Saviour with a believing heart and a devout mouth, together with good deeds. Whence Mary the mother of the Lord says in the canticle of the Gospel: « My soul doth magnify the Lord, and my spirit hath rejoiced in God my Saviour (Luc. I). » « The works, he says, of God are perfect. » Which then are these? Hear Moses saying in Genesis: « So then the heavens and the earth were finished, and all the furniture of them; and God completed on the seventh day his work which he had made, and he rested on the seventh day from all his work which he had accomplished (Gen. II). » The work of God, therefore, is the redemption of the human race; and when he had perfected it, resting in the sepulchre he showed us the spiritual Sabbath. « All, he says, 1134Ahis ways are judgments, » because all his commandments are true. Whence the Psalmist says: « The law of the Lord is blameless, converting souls; the testimony of the Lord is faithful, giving wisdom to little ones (Psal. XVIII). » And likewise: « The judgments, he says, of God are true, justified in themselves; more to be desired than gold and much precious stone, and sweeter than honey and the honeycomb (Ibid.). » And elsewhere: « With my lips, he says, I have pronounced all the judgments of thy mouth (Psal. CXVIII). »

« God is faithful and without any iniquity, just and right. » Another edition: « God is faithful and there is no iniquity in him; just and holy is the Lord. » He is faithful, because what he promised he has beyond doubt fulfilled. Without any iniquity, because the highest equity in no way admits fellowship with iniquity. Just, because to those who sin 1134Band do not repent he will repay punishment for their evils. Right, because to those who work worthily and deserve well he will render everlasting life. Holy, because in all his judgments and acts he is most approved: for sanctus signifies both the merciful and the patient, and the bestower of all good things, since whatever is wrought by him is filled full with the praise of virtues.

« They have sinned against him, not his sons, in filth. » Another edition: « They have sinned not against him, sons defiled. » How are they not sons of God, save because they are not of faith: « But as many as received him, he gave them power to be made the sons of God, to them that believe in his name (Joan. I). » Whence the Lord says to the Jews, who would not receive faith in him and who spurned his teaching: « You are of your father the devil, and the desires of your father you will 1134Cdo; he was a murderer from the beginning, and a robber. » And Stephen says to the same Jews: « Which of the prophets have your fathers not persecuted? And they killed them who foretold of the coming of the Just One, of whom you have now been the betrayers and murderers — you who received the law by the disposition of angels and have not kept it. » He therefore who sins incorrigibly proves himself not to be a son of God. But that edition which the seventy interpreters published has it thus: « They have sinned not against him, sons deserving of blame. » Which some have rendered out of the Greek as "sons defiled," some as "sons idle." Whence there is no great question here, nay rather none at all. But this rightly moves one to inquiry: if it has been said generally, they have sinned not against him, the sons — since he who sins does not sin against God, that is, does not harm God, but himself — how then is it to be understood 1134Dthat is read in the psalm: « To thee only have I sinned (Psal. L). » And in Jeremiah: « To thee have we sinned, O patience of Israel, O Lord (Jerem. XIV). » And again in the psalm: « Heal my soul, for I have sinned against thee (Psal. XL). » And whether it is the same thing to sin to God as to sin against God — whence also Heli the priest says: « If a man shall sin against God, who shall pray for him (I Reg. II). To sin against God is to sin in those things which pertain to the worship of God; for God is read to have said this to Abimelech concerning Sara: « Therefore I spared thee, that thou shouldst not sin against me (Gen. XX). » But to sin to the Lord, or rather to have sinned to the Lord — unless perhaps somewhere a written text should occur which resists this sense — those men seem to me to be so called not undeservedly who do not perform worthy penance 1135Afor their sin, so that God may be glorified in pardoning; for David, rendering the reason why he had said: « To thee only have I sinned and done evil before thee (Psal. L), » subjoined and says: « That thou mayest be justified in thy words, and mayest overcome when thou art judged, » because to all who think rightly it plainly appears that the Lord is just in all his ways and holy in all his works: « Nor does he will the death of the sinner, but that he be converted and live (Psal. XL). » Likewise also as to what was said by David: « Heal my soul, for I have sinned against thee. » This same thing is at work, that God may be glorified in pardoning, because great is his mercy toward those who confess to him and return to him. But in this Canticle the Prophet foresaw that there would be certain men who were so to sin, offending God 1135Bby their great iniquities, that they would not even be willing to do penance and to return to God, that they might be healed; of whom also it is said: « Since they are flesh, and a spirit going and not returning. » Therefore he said that they were not sons of God, because they proved incorrigible.

« A depraved and perverse generation, is it these things thou renderest to the Lord? » Another edition: « For, a depraved and perverse generation, these things hast thou repaid to the Lord. »

With a like description did the Saviour brand their treachery in the Gospel, when he answered those who tempted him and sought a sign from him, saying: « A depraved and perverse generation seeketh a sign, and a sign shall not be given it, but the sign of Jonas (Matth. XII). » So too John the Baptist says to the crowds of the Jews who came to him: « Ye brood of vipers, who hath shewed you to flee 1135Cfrom the wrath to come? Bring forth therefore fruits worthy of penance, and do not choose to say, We have Abraham for our father. For God is able of these stones to raise up children to Abraham. » For the generation of the Jews was ever depraved and perverse, which showed itself ungrateful for the benefits of God, and repaid him for this no worthy praises — nay, on the contrary, ever various transgressions, the filth of idols, and provocations.

« O foolish and unwise people, is not he himself thy Father, who possessed thee, and made and created thee? » Another edition: « So, O foolish and not wise people, is not this very one thy Father? He possessed thee, he made thee and created thee. » The Father and founder of all things, according to the power of nature, is one God, since from him and through him and in him are all things. God therefore created 1135Dman when he founded the matter out of which he could be formed; he made him when he formed him to his own image and likeness; he possessed him when, errors being driven out, he claimed him for himself as his own through faith and grace. The foolish and unwise people, therefore, was the nation of the Jews, which neither venerated with worthy worship its Creator and Maker, whom the law given to it showed most plainly; nor was mindful of that benefit whereby, delivering it in Egypt from the hand of its enemies by miracles and prodigies, he specially possessed it, giving it manna from heaven and water from the rock, and furnishing it also guidance in the Red Sea and in the desert by angelic ministry — that is, in a pillar of cloud by day and in a pillar of fire by night; 1136Abut this depraved and perverse generation repaid to its Lord no worthy thanks for so many good things: nay rather it fashioned idols for itself, and most foolishly bestowed upon them divine honour and sacrifices, saying: « These are thy gods, O Israel, that have brought thee out of the land of Egypt (Exod. XII). »

« Remember the days of old, think upon every generation, ask thy father, and he will declare to thee; thy elders, and they will tell thee. » Another edition: « Have in mind the days of the age, understand the years, the nations of nations; ask thy father, and he will declare to thee; thy seniors, and they will tell thee. » He recalls to their memory the deeds of old, bidding them ask their fathers and learn from them in what manner in past ages and generations they were delivered by divine help, 1136Band often protected from their enemies, so that at least in this way they may be roused to penance for their sins, and, stripping themselves of every error, may study to draw near the more speedily to the truth. For by "fathers" here are to be mystically understood not any dull and foolish persons whatever, but the doctors of the law and the preachers of the Gospel, by whose teachings and examples a man can advance to divine understanding; whence the Apostle, writing to the Corinthians, says: « For if you should have ten thousand instructors in Christ, yet not many fathers. For in Christ Jesus, by the Gospel, I have begotten you (I Cor. IV). » And to the Hebrews: « We have had, he says, indeed the fathers of our flesh as instructors, and we reverenced them: we shall not much more obey the Father of spirits, and we shall live; and they indeed for a time of few days 1136Cinstructed us according to their own will; but he unto that which is useful, in the receiving of his sanctification (Hebr. XII). »

« When the Most High divided the nations, when he separated the sons of Adam, he appointed the bounds of the peoples according to the number of the children of Israel. But the portion of the Lord is his people, Jacob the cord of his inheritance. » Another edition: « When the Exalted One shall have divided the nations, even as he dispersed the sons of Adam, he set the bounds of the nations according to the number of the angels of God; and the Lord's portion became his people Jacob, the cord of his inheritance Israel. » For the order of the history gives the reason concerning the distribution of the human race: since the multitude of men is so great that their numerousness cannot be comprehended by human reckoning, 1136Dthe Lord chose out of so great a multitude the children of Israel alone into his own inheritance, where he might make known his name and his power, and manifest the secrets of his will through the judgments of the law and the writings of the prophets, as above in the present book Moses testified, saying (Deut. VII): « The Lord thy God hath chosen thee, that thou shouldst be to him a peculiar people of all the peoples that are upon the earth; not because you surpassed all the nations in number was the Lord joined to you and chose you, whereas you are fewer than all peoples; but because the Lord loved you and kept the oath which he swore to your fathers; and he brought you out with a strong hand, and redeemed you from the house of bondage, from the hand of Pharao king of Egypt; that thou mayest know that the Lord thy 1137AGod, he himself is the strong and faithful God, keeping his covenant and mercy to them that love him, and to them that keep his precepts, unto a thousand generations; and repaying forthwith them that hate him, so that he destroy them and defer no longer, requiting them at once what they deserve. Keep therefore the precepts and the ceremonies and the judgments which I command thee this day to do. » And these things according to the Hebrew verity can be thus understood. But according to the other edition, where it is read, he set the bounds of the nations according to the number of the angels of God — and in the vision of Daniel, where the Angel related to the Prophet concerning the prince of the Persians who withstood him — it seems to hint something else to us: for the ministries of angels are believed to be ordained over each several nation and over each several man, so that under their 1137Brule and defence human nature may be kept according to the dispensation of almighty God, and be protected from its enemies. Whence the Lord in the Gospel, teaching that little ones are not to be scandalized, straightway subjoined, saying: « Their angels in heaven always see the face of my Father, who is in heaven (Matth. XII). » For since that supernal city consists of angels and men, to which we believe that as many ascend from the race of men as it befell that elect angels remained there — as it is written, « he set the bounds of the nations according to the number of the angels of God » — we too ought to draw something from those distinctions of the supernal citizens for the use of our own manner of life, and to kindle ourselves by good endeavours to the increase of virtues. For since so great a multitude of men is believed to be about to ascend 1137Cthither as the multitude of angels that remained, it remains that men themselves also, who return to the heavenly country, should in returning thither imitate something from those companies; for the distinct manners of life of men agree with the orders of the several companies, and are assigned to their lot through the likeness of their manner of life. But the portion of the Lord is the people, the spiritual Jacob; and the cord of his inheritance is the true Israel, namely the Catholic Church, of which it is said through the Psalmist: « The lines are fallen unto me in goodly places - for my inheritance is goodly to me (Psal. XV). »

« He found him in a desert land, in a place of horror and of vast wilderness. He led him about and taught him and kept him as the apple of his eye. » Another edition: 1137D« He made him sufficient unto himself in the wilderness, in the thirst of heat, where there was no water. He led him about and instructed him and kept him as the apple of the eye. » The Lord is said to have found Israel in a place of horror and of vast wilderness, because, giving the law on mount Sina, he entered into a covenant with him, distributed to them manna from heaven, and brought forth water from the rock, gave the flesh of quails, furnishing them all sufficiency in food according to that which seemed to him to be fitting for them. And in that same wilderness, leading them about for forty years, he taught them his precepts. But this is rightly asked: how does he say that he protected them as the apple of his eye, when very many of them were laid low in the wilderness — unless perhaps we say that they were protected and 1138Akept for just so long as they observed the commandments of God; but when they transgressed them, they were straightway struck with plagues and laid waste by their enemies. But mystically the Lord found Israel in the desert when he took the Church out of the error of the world, where there was the horror of idolatry and no mention of God. The Lord therefore leads the Church about, when by spreading her abroad in all parts of the world he directs her into the way of his commandments; and, showing her his will through the Holy Spirit, everywhere guards her with a full guardianship — which is signified by the apple of the eye — against heretics and persecutors. There follows another comparison:

« As an eagle enticing her young to fly, and fluttering over them, she spread her wings and took them up and bore them upon her shoulders. » Another edition: 1138B« As an eagle covers her nest, and has settled over her young, she spread her wings and received them and took them up upon her shoulder-blades. » The natural philosophers relate that the eagle — which is named from the keenness of its eyes — is of so sharp a gaze that, when it is borne over the seas on motionless wing and lies not open to human sight, it sees from so great a height the little fishes swimming in the water, and, descending like a hurled engine, drags the seized prey by its wings to the shore. For it is said also not to turn aside its gaze against the ray of the sun; whence also it holds up its young suspended by its talon and sets them against the rays of the sun, and those which it has seen keep an unmoving eye it preserves as worthy of the stock; but any which it sees turn their gaze aside it casts away as degenerate. Eagles are also said, when they see their young grow feathered in the nests, to strike them with their wings and to entice them out of the nest 1138Cto fly. And they build their nests in rocks and in trees, in whose nest the stone echites is built in, which some have called gaietes, useful for many remedies, losing nothing by fire. And these same searchers into nature relate that eagles die not so much of old age or of sickness as of hunger, the upper beak growing to such a length that its hook cannot be opened; but when it has seen that danger threatens it from thence, it blunts its beak upon a very hard rock, until it returns again to its former use. And thence it is written in the psalm in mystic speech, the Prophet saying to the faithful soul: « Thy youth shall be renewed like the eagle's (Psal. CII). » By the likeness of the eagle it is shown, according to the letter, that the Lord, by rousing his people, helped them to possess the land of Chanaan 1138Dby wars, and by helping them protected them from their enemies. But according to the mystical sense this eagle signifies the Lord Saviour, who holds the likeness of the eagle in this, that by his own death he snatched away the prey from the malignant enemy, and after his resurrection carried up to the heights of the heavens the flesh which he took for the redemption of the human race, exhorting us by words and by examples that we should follow thither by faith, and reach out by devotion, whither he himself ascended in body; whence also he says in the Gospel: « Wheresoever the body shall be, there shall the eagles be gathered together (Luc. XVII). » The other examples also which we have told of the eagle can likewise be figuratively transferred to him and to his sons. For as the eagle is of a sharp gaze, so that, lifted above the seas 1139Aon motionless wing and not appearing to human sight, seeing from so great a height the little fishes swimming in the deep, it seizes them by its preying and drags them to the shore: so our Redeemer, exalted above all the heavens and bodily taken up from human sight, nevertheless sees in the deep of this world, by the gaze of his majesty, those whom he may rescue cruat by the saving prey, and may draw to the shore of eternal blessedness by the cord of his love. Whence he himself also says in the Gospel: « And I, if I be lifted up from the earth, will draw all things to myself (Joan. XII). » His young — that is, the sons reborn to him through the grace of baptism — he sets against the rays of the eternal sun through contemplation; and those whom he has seen to have a steadfast eye of mind in gazing, or worthy of his own stock, he preserves perpetually; but any whom he beholds turning back the gaze of the mind to the lowest things and to 1139Bcarnal desires, these he casts away as degenerate. And this eagle builds her nest in rocks and in trees, because in those whom she beholds firm in faith and bearing fruit in virtues she brings forth the fruitfulness of a spiritual germination; and there is born that gem and most precious pearl which the sacred text of the Gospel relates that the spiritual merchant procures for himself when all his money has been sold. But that the eagle blunts tandit its superfluous beak upon a rock, and thus brings back its former measure, signifies that holy men break in pieces whatever they behold in their manner of life to be superfluous and harmful to themselves, dashing it against the rock which is Christ; and, renewing themselves through grace and through good endeavour, they render themselves fit for their former office.

1139C« The Lord alone was his leader, and there was not with him a alicnus god. » Another edition: « The Lord alone was teaching them, and there was not with them a strange god. » It is manifest that one and only God freed Israel out of Egypt, and not the false idols which the Egyptians worshipped. So also in our own deliverance out of the spiritual Egypt there is no other than that same God who once delivered the Israelites — namely the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, one and threefold, the great and almighty God, who through the incarnation of Jesus Christ our Lord effects the mystery of our salvation. Whence let heretics know how miserably they stray, deviating from the true faith, when they sever themselves through schisms from the Catholic Church — men who are liable to as many idols as they have errors

1139D« He set him up over a high land, that he might eat the fruits of the fields. » Another edito: « He brought them into the strength of the land, he fed them with the produce of the fields. » He calls Palestine a high land, because in comparison with the land of Egypt, which is flat and well watered, it seems mountainous and rough; and it has strength, because it is not barren but most fertile in bringing forth the fruits of the earth unto the fullness of them that dwell in it. Otherwise, the high land is the holy Church, which is exceedingly lofty in virtues and in knowledge, and most fruitful in the germination of good works. There all the faithful, being set up, eat the fruits of the spiritual fields, because there the good seed of the word of God springs up, one bearing thirtyfold, another sixtyfold, another a hundredfold 1140Afruit, so that each one may find for himself food sufficient according to his capacity.

« That he might suck honey out of the rock, and oil out of the hardest stone. » Another edition: « They sucked honey out of the rock, and oil out of the firm rock. » But nowhere is any such thing read according to the history, if the whole of the Old Testament be reviewed: nowhere did that people suck honey out of a rock, nowhere oil out of a firm rock or out of the hardest stone. But because, according to Paul's word, « the rock was Christ (I Cor. X). » They sucked honey out of the rock who saw the deeds and miracles of that same our Redeemer. But they sucked oil out of the firm rock who after his resurrection deserved to be anointed by the outpouring of the Holy Spirit. As it were, therefore, he gave honey in the firm rock, when the Lord, being still mortal, showed to his disciples the sweetness 1140Bof his miracles. But the firm rock poured out oil, because after his Resurrection, being now made impassible, he sent forth the gift of holy unction through the breathing of the Spirit. Of this oil also it is said through the prophet: « The yoke shall rot away at the presence of the oil (Isa. X), » for they were held under the yoke of demoniac domination; but we have been anointed with the oil of the Holy Spirit, and because the grace of liberty has anointed us, the yoke of demoniac domination has rotted away.

« Butter of the herd and milk of the sheep, with the fat of lambs and of rams of the sons of Basan, and goats with the marrow of wheat, and the blood of the grape. » Another edition: « Butter of oxen and milk of sheep, with the fat of lambs and of rams of the sons of bulls and of goats, with the fat of the kidneys, of wheat, and the blood of the grape. » Although these things according 1140Cto the history hint at the fertility of the land of Chanaan, yet in a loftier sense they show the abundance of the grace of Christ which the holy Church received by the gift of her bestower. They took butter of the herd who learned the fullness of faith from the examples of the patriarchs; and they received milk of the sheep who took simplicity of mind, or easiness of teaching, from the innocent life of the Fathers unto their own edification; and these things, with the fat of lambs and of rams — that is, with the fatness of the charity of the apostles and of those whom they themselves had steeped in the faith — they received unto the refreshment of their mind. For by the name of rams are signified the apostles and men of apostolic dignity, because they themselves are the leaders of the flock of God. Whence it is said in the psalm: « Bring to the Lord, O ye sons of God, bring to the Lord the sons of rams (Psal. 1140DXXVIII). » But that which is subjoined, « of the sons of Basan, » belongs to the same sense, because Basan is interpreted fatness; for they are sons of fatness who have been instructed in the disciplines of the New Testament, wherein consists a multitude of knowledge and a greatness of virtues. That also which is read in the other edition, « of the sons of bulls, » can likewise be referred to the person of the prophets and of the apostles, and also of the other holy doctors, because they are the leaders of the flock of God and preside over the spiritual herds, from whose teachings, namely, these receive the seed of the word, whence undo they may be able to bring forth the offspring of good works. Nor would these gifts have sufficed, unless the goats too were added. For goats have the figure of penitents, because in the law they were offered for sin: 1141Afor it is not enough for the faithful to have the gifts of virtues, unless they do penance for their transgressions. Whence he who was at once king and prophet, and to whom it was promised concerning the incarnation of Christ that he should be born of his seed, entreats the Lord for his own offences, saying: « Wash me yet more from my injustice, and cleanse me from my offence; for I acknowledge my iniquity, and my offence is always before me (Psal. L). » And that all these benefits which have been named might be heaped up with a more eminent gift, he added the marrow of wheat and the blood of the grape. For what is figured by the marrow of wheat but the body of Christ; what by the blood of the grape but the chalice of the New Testament in the blood of Christ? For among the very many gifts granted out of the mercy of God to the people of the Church, 1141Bthis excels among them all: that he has ministered ministrasit to it, unto eternal salvation, the sacraments of his body and blood in the confecting of bread and wine. There follows:

« He had drunk the most unmixed; the beloved is grown fat and hath kicked. » Another edition: « Jacob drank wine and ate, and was filled, and the beloved kicked. » Assuredly all the gifts of God are conferred, without the dregs of any wickedness or reproach, upon those who believe and think worthily of them. But since, just as the carnal Israelites of old abused the gifts of God and, made worse out of most bountiful benefits, slid down into idolatry, so — alas for the grief! — not a few of the people of the Church, ungrateful for the benefits of God, slip back into vices, and that which was conferred upon them for their salvation 1141Cthey turn into their own perdition. Whence it is subjoined:

« Grown gross, made fat and swollen wide, he forsook God his maker, and departed from God his salvation. They provoked him with strange gods, and with their abominations they stirred him to wrath; they sacrificed to demons and not to God, to gods whom they knew not; new and recent ones came to them, whom their fathers worshipped not. » Another edition: « He was made fat and grew gross; he was swollen wide; he forsook God, who made him, and departed from God his salvation. They embittered me with strange things, with their abominations they stirred me up; they sacrificed to demons and not to God, to gods whom they knew not; new, 1141Drecent ones came to them, whom their fathers knew not. » This the ancient Israelites did after the manner of history, as is sufficiently set forth in their books; but the modern Israelites too, when they turn the gifts of God granted them in knowledge and in virtues for the divine service into heresy or hypocrisy, are proved to serve idols and not God — rendering worship, that is to say, to demons and not to God, which our fathers, that is, the patriarchs, the prophets and the apostles and the evangelists, by no means did. Hence it comes to pass for these idolaters that, provoking by their evils the most clement Lord, whom they might have had propitious had they done well, they must suffer him severe and strict on account of their evil works.

« Thou hast forsaken the God that begot thee, and hast forgotten 1142Athe Lord thy creator. » Another edition: « Thou hast forsaken the God that begot thee, and hast forgotten the God that nourisheth thee. » The Lord in a certain manner begot the Israelites through the law and circumcision; he begot also the people of the Church through the grace of baptism, through the sanctification of the Holy Spirit, and he furnished it sufficiently with the nourishment of heavenly food, that is, of evangelical doctrine. But he who fears not to forget these things will justly feel God angry with him through vengeance. Whence it is subjoined:

« The Lord saw, and was stirred to wrath, because his sons and his daughters provoked him; and he said: I will hide my face from them, and I will consider their last end. » Another edition: « The Lord saw, and was jealous, and was embittered because of the anger of his sons and his daughters; and he said: I will turn away my 1142Bface from them, and I will show what shall be to them at the last. » Nothing is heavier among punishments than to be deprived of the presence and the vision of God. Thence the Psalmist entreats the Lord, saying: « Cast me not away from thy face (Psal. L). « And likewise: » Turn not away, he says, thy face from me. » Hence Job also speaks to the Lord, saying: « Let me not be hidden from thy face (Job XIII). » Since therefore sons and daughters — that is, men of either sex — provoking him for their evils which they do, do not merit his mercy, but, ungrateful, are spurned by him at their last end. This shall be proved when they shall be cast into the eternal punishments of Gehenna; for this is to consider their last end: to make manifest their torments, which he himself bestows upon them justly, not with a troubled mind, but with a tranquil one.

1142C« For it is a perverse generation, and unfaithful sons. They have provoked me in that which was not the Lord, and have stirred me with their vanities; and I will provoke them in that which is not a people, and with a foolish nation will I stir them. » Another edition: « For it is a depraved and perverse generation, sons in whom there is no faith in them; they in jealousy have compelled me, and not in God; in anger they have stirred me up with their idols; and I in jealousy will drive them out, and not into a nation; with an unwise nation will I stir them. » Since therefore the Israelites, casting away the worship of their Lord, worshipped idols, and, forsaking the true and living God, served the vanity of images, they were handed over for plunder to the nations, who at that time had not yet deserved to be called by the name of the people of God. Likewise also churchmen, who, the truth being forsaken, 1142Dturned aside to errors, if they shall not have repented of their evils, will be handed over to the malignant spirits — who by growing proud lost their angelic dignity — to be thrust into the prisons of hell. Well, therefore, can the demons be called a foolish nation, because they hate justice and love iniquity.

« A fire is kindled in my fury, and it shall burn even to the lowest parts of hell. » Another edition: « For a fire hath blazed out of my anger, and it shall burn even to the lower regions beneath. » Of those whom the scourges condemn and do not deliver it is written: « Thou hast struck them, and they have not grieved; thou hast bruised them, and they have refused to receive correction (Jer. V). » For these, the scourges begin from this life and endure in the eternal striking. Whence also 1143Athe Lord says through Moses: « A fire hath blazed out of my anger, and it shall burn even to the lower regions beneath (Deut. XXXII). » For as far as concerns the present striking, it is rightly said: « A fire hath blazed out of my anger. » But as far as concerns eternal damnation, this is aptly subjoined, and it shall burn even to the lower regions beneath — although by some there is wont to be alleged that which is written: « God does not take vengeance twice for the same thing. » These, however, do not attend to that which is said through the Prophet concerning the wicked, and with a double confusion crush them, and that which is written elsewhere. Jesus, saving the people out of the land of Egypt, in the second place destroyed those who did not believe; to whom, nevertheless, if we lend our assent — that any fault whatever cannot be struck twice — this must be reckoned of those struck for their sin and dying in their sin, 1143Bbecause their striking begins here and is finished there, so that in the uncorrected there is one scourge, which begins temporally but is consummated in eternal punishments; so that for those who altogether refuse to be corrected the striking of present scourges is already the beginning of the torments that follow. There follows:

« It hath devoured the earth and its growth, and shall burn up the foundations of the mountains. I will heap evils upon them, and will spend my arrows among them. They shall be consumed with famine, and birds shall devour them with a most bitter bite; I will send in upon them the teeth of beasts, with the fury of them that drag upon the earth and of serpents. Without, the sword shall lay them waste, and within, dread — the young man together with the virgin, the suckling with the old man. » 1143CAnother edition: « The earth hath eaten the produce of the fields, and shall burn up the foundations of the mountains. I will heap evils upon them, and will consume my arrows among them; wasting away with famine, and they shall be food for birds; and a stretching of the back that cannot be healed. I will send in upon them the teeth of beasts, with the fury of them that drag upon the earth. From without, the sword shall deprive them, leaving them without sons, and in their storehouses, fear — young men with the virgin, the suckling with the settled old man. » It hath devoured, he says, the earth and its growth: then does it devour the earth and its growth, when the flame of eternal vengeance shall burn up the sinful flesh on account of the works of lust. For the growths of the flesh are properly the works of carnal concupiscence: « which are fornications, uncleannesses, immodesties, luxuries, the service of idols, sorceries, enmities, contentions, 1143Demulations, angers, quarrels, dissensions, sects, envies, murders, drunkennesses, revellings, and such like — they who do these things, » as Paul testifies, » « shall not obtain the kingdom of God (Gal. V). » And it shall burn up the foundations of the mountains, when it shall consume with eternal torments the wicked counsels of the proud, by which they believed that they could sin unpunished. He will heap evils upon them, when he shall bring into memory not only their evil works, but also their perverse thoughts and the depraved words for which they are to be punished. He will spend his arrows among them, when he shall show the wrath of his indignation through various torments. They shall be consumed with famine, that is, not of food nor of drink, but with the perpetual barrenness of good works. And birds shall devour them with a most bitter bite: 1144Anamely, the malignant spirits, reproaching them with their past sins, tear them with a most savage mouth. But that which is added after the devouring of the birds, a stretching of the back that cannot be healed, signifies the most bitter wounds which sinners suffer in the torturing of the demons, who afflict them without any pity and force them to pay strong penalties in hell. The beasts and the serpents are these same malignant spirits, who are called beasts on account of their ferocity, serpents on account of their craft. Beasts and serpents drag the reprobate with their teeth upon the earth in fury, when that which they first persuaded carnal men to by flattering, that they might seduce them, they afterwards with a raging mind exact of sinners, that they may torment them. Without, the sword shall lay waste the impious, and within, dread; when in the final vengeance the burning 1144Bof eternal fire tortures the body on every side, and within, the greatness of anguish oppresses the mind; whence it is written: « Their worm shall not die, and the fire shall not be extinguished (Marc. IX). » « The young man, he says, together with the virgin, the suckling with the old man. » Having enumerated all the ages, he shows that in the final vengeance no age is to be spared, whom the same worship of idolatry had defiled. But allegorically, in the young man he shows the wanton and the lustful; in the virgin, her that is barren of good works; in the suckling, the dull; in the old man, the slothful and the useless. All these, therefore, whom now admonition and rebuke do not restrain from sins, the just vengeance of God will consume in the final avenging.

« And I said: Where are they? I will make their memory to cease from among men. » Another edition: « And 1144Che said: I will destroy them; and I will take away their memory from among men. » God's questioning is not ignorance, but rebuke. The fulfilment of this sentence can easily be discerned by him who sees the Jews cast out from the land of promise and dispersed through the whole world. But generally this hangs over all sinners and impenitent men as a thing to come: that they be taken away from the land of the saints, and that their memory be not numbered in the assembly of the elect. Whence through the Psalmist a punishment to come is foretold to the head of all the wicked together with the whole body, when, the several kinds of vices having been reckoned up, it is subjoined: « Therefore God shall destroy thee for ever; he shall pluck thee out and remove thee from his tabernacle, and thy root out of the land of the living (Psal. LI). »

1144D« But for the wrath of the enemies I have deferred it, lest perhaps their foes should be lifted up and should say: Our hand is exalted, and not God hath done all these things. » Another edition: « Except for the wrath of the enemies, that they be not upon the earth for a long time, lest the adversaries agree together and say: Our hand is exalted, and not God hath done all these things. » For this reason the Lord often bore with the sinning people, long enduring them, lest the nations bordering on them should blaspheme his name and his power. Whence Moses also says to the Lord, who was indignant for the sin of Israel and willed to destroy them: « Let not the Egyptians, I beseech thee, O Lord, say: He craftily brought them out, that he might kill them in the mountains and blot them out from the earth. Let thy anger rest, and be appeasable over the wickedness 1145Aof thy people. Remember Abraham, Isaac and Israel thy servants, to whom thou didst swear by thine own self, saying: I will multiply your seed as the stars of heaven, and this whole land whereof I have spoken I will give to your seed, and you shall possess it for ever. And the Lord was appeased, that he should not do the evil which he had spoken against all his people. » Likewise us also, when we have sinned, he bears with, by reason of his great patience and long-suffering, willing that no one perish, but that all be made safe and come to the acknowledging of the truth, according to that word of Paul the apostle which, writing to the Romans, he says: « And thinkest thou this, O man, that judgest them who do such things and dost the same, that thou shalt escape the judgment of God? Or despisest thou the riches of his goodness and of his patience and of his long-suffering? 1145BNot knowing that the benignity of God will lead thee to penance; but according to thy hardness and impenitent heart thou treasurest up to thyself wrath in the day of wrath and of the revelation of the just judgment of God (Rom. II), » etc. And lest perhaps the malignant spirits should glory in our perdition over their own strength, as though they had gained the victory by their own forces — since in this they always strive, that they may overturn the sentence of the Most High, who decreed that men should possess in the heavens the place of the lost spirits.

« It is a nation without counsel and without prudence. Would that they were wise and understood, and would provide for their last end. » Another edition: « For it is a nation that hath lost counsel, and there is none in them; they have not been wise to understand discipline; they shall perceive in the time to come. » The nation of the Jews, and of all the impious as well, are without the counsel 1145Cof salvation, because they know not how to provide for themselves for the time to come, so as to escape the vengeance that hangs over them for their sins and to flee the eternal torment of the infernal punishments; and since they neglect to live under discipline, they shall endure tribulation and anguish in the time to come, according to that which is written of them (Prov. I): « When sudden calamity shall fall upon them, and destruction shall come on as a tempest; when tribulation and anguish shall come upon them, then shall they call upon me, and I will not hear; they shall rise up in the morning, and shall not find me; because they held discipline in hatred and received not the fear of the Lord. »

« How doth one pursue a thousand, and two put ten thousand to flight? Is it not for this, that their God hath sold them, and the Lord hath shut them up? » Another edition: 1145D« How doth one pursue a thousand, and two shall remove ten thousand, unless because the Lord hath subjected them and God hath delivered them up. » Whoever truly acknowledges the greatness of the Lord will plainly prove that the destruction of his own people comes about not from any weakness of his power, but from the permission of his just judgment: because it could in no way come to pass that one should pursue a thousand and two put ten thousand to flight, unless the Lord had delivered them up and, casting them away in hatred, had shut them up in the hands of their enemies.

« For our God is not as their God. And our enemies are judges. » Another edition: « Who is not our God, as their gods are; but our enemies are senseless. » For the Lord our God is truthful 1146Aand just, but the idols of the nations are false and useless: which not only our own people, but even the adversaries of our own, approve. Whence it is often read that the Jews, being delivered to their enemies, were deservedly rebuked by them for their unfaithfulness and injustice, as Jeremiah says in his lamentation: « The enemies saw her, and mocked at her sabbaths (Thren. I). » Hence we must take the utmost heed lest our enemies, who through the error of idolatry are deservedly called senseless, being provoked by our evils, break out into blasphemy against the Lord when they shall have seen us overcome and reduced to nothing on account of our sins, and ascribe to their own strength that which has been accomplished not by their power, but by the judgment of the Lord.

« For their vine is of the vineyard of the Sodomites, and of the 1146Bsuburbs of Gomorrha; their grape is a grape of gall, and a most bitter cluster. » Another edition: « For their vine is out of the vineyard of the Sodomites, and their shoot out of Gomorrha; their grape is a grape of gall, a cluster of bitterness to them. » Often in the sacred Scriptures the synagogue or Jerusalem are compared to Sodom and Gomorrha, because as those abused the gifts of God wickedly, so these too always showed themselves ungrateful for the benefits of God. Whence Isaias says of them: « Unless the Lord of hosts had left us seed, we had been as Sodom, we should have been like to Gomorrha. Hear the word of the Lord, ye princes of Sodom; give ear to the law of our God, ye people of Gomorrha, etc. (Isa. I). » For the vineyard of the Lord, as is written in the same prophet, was the house 1146Cof Israel, which was then turned into the bitterness of gall, when it was plotting the death of Christ. Whence they themselves also offered on a reed vinegar mingled with gall to him as he hung upon the cross, that in this it might be shown that they held the knowledge of the law, which they had received upright, corrupted by the gall of their own malice, since they would not receive the giver of the law when he came to them, but moreover provoked him and fastened him to the cross. Otherwise, by this sentence the wickedness of heretics too is struck, who, although they at first received from holy preachers the pure teaching of spiritual grace, yet did not fear to stain it with their own errors; and therefore their whole doctrine was turned into the bitterness of gall and of wormwood.

« Their wine is the gall of dragons, like the incurable venom of asps. » Another edition: « Their wine is the fury 1146Dof dragons, and the incurable fury of asps. » Aptly are the treachery of the Jews and the cunning of heretics compared to the gall of dragons and the venom of asps, because as serpents hide bitterness and venom within themselves, that they may wound the unwary unawares, so also Jews and heretics veil their malice with guile and frauds, that they may the more easily destroy the imprudent. Well therefore is it said: « The incurable venom of asps, » because whomsoever the error of heretics has invaded, unless he free himself therefrom, it without doubt destroys him for ever. But that which is read according to the other edition, « Their wine is the fury of dragons, and the incurable fury of asps, » shows that it belongs not to a sound mind, but rather to a raving one, to spurn eternal life and to choose perpetual death, 1147Ato abandon the truth of the Gospel and to be willing rather to follow the deceit of errors.

« Are not these things stored up with me, and sealed up in my treasures? » Another edition: « Are not all these things gathered together with me, and sealed up in my treasures? » It is the custom of the human race quickly to forget things past, and that things which once seemed great should themselves likewise fail in remembrance as the space of time slips by. But it is not so with God, where there is no past and no future, but the present is ever at hand; for the things which pass away from us are there kept in reserve, and shall not be handed over to oblivion. Hence it is necessary that we study to blot out, by urgent penance, before the eyes of God, the sins which we long since committed, lest perhaps in the time to come, when the time of vengeance shall be, he reckon to us our debts 1147Band say: These things hast thou done, and I was silent; thou hast thought iniquity, that I should be like to thee: I will reprove thee, and set them before thy face.

« Vengeance is mine, and I will repay them in due time, that their foot may slip. » Another edition: « In the day of vengeance I will render to them, in the time in which their foot shall have slipped. » Both the time and the manner of the vengeance by which sinners, if they do not correct themselves from their sins, are to be tormented, are certain with the Lord. But because his mercies are many, « let us come before his face with confession, and make a joyful noise to him with psalms (Psal. XCIV). » Let us humble our souls in fasting, let us watch and pray, since who knows whether God may turn and forgive, and leave a blessing behind him?

« The day of destruction is at hand, and the times 1147Cmake haste to be present. » Another edition: « For the day of their destruction is near, and these things are prepared for you. » The day of vengeance is at hand for every man, because it is certain; nor can that be long-lasting which at some time comes to an end. For whatever is temporal — as is the age of man, the course of the present life — if it be compared to eternity, becomes small in comparison with it. Therefore it is said, « and the times make haste to be present, » because temporal things are quickly ended, and the torments prepared come upon the wicked, according to that which is written: « Judgments are prepared for scorners (Prov. XIX). » But according to the history, the vengeance of the Israelites drew near, because from the time they began to rebel they never wholly desisted, until they lost alike their kingdom, their place, and the prosperity of their life; for after they had slain the Prophets and had crucified the Saviour, 1147Dadding new seditions to the old, they provoked the Romans against themselves by rebelling; and these, the city being destroyed, the temple burned, the people slain by famine and the sword, dispersed their remnants through the whole world, where, wandering with uncertain dwellings, they remain uncertain of the hope of things to come.

« The Lord will judge his people, and will have mercy on his servants. » Another edition: « For the Lord will judge his people, and will be consoled in his servants. » The Lord therefore will judge his people when he separates his elect from the reprobate; for sometimes "to judge" is taken for "to discern," but sometimes for "to condemn." Whence in the psalm the Prophet said to the Lord: « Judge me, O God, and distinguish my cause 1148Afrom the nation that is not holy; from the unjust and deceitful man deliver me (Psal. XLII). » Here "to judge" is set for discernment; but elsewhere the same prophet, crying to the Lord, says: « Judge thou, O Lord, them that wrong me; overthrow them that fight against me (Psal. XXXIV). » But here "judgment" is put for damnation. For the Lord will judge his saints when, separating them in the day of judgment from sinners, he shall place them at his right hand; but their enemies and persecutors he will set on the left, that they may receive certain damnation with the devil and his angels in hell; and these shall go into everlasting punishment, but the just into life everlasting. And then shall that be perfectly fulfilled which follows in the aforesaid verse: « And he will have mercy on thy servants. » Because in the heavenly blessedness he will grant to his faithful ones eternal mercy. 1148BBut that, according to the other edition, the Lord is read to be consoled in his servants, is said metonymically, where by that which is effected is shown that which effects; for the Lord according to his nature is always glad and tranquil, nor is he ever changed into the sorrow of sadness; of whom it is written: « Thou, O Lord, who with tranquillity judgest and disposest all things (Sap. XII). » Well therefore is the Lord said to be consoled in his servants, who consoles his elect, after the end of the present sadness, with eternal peace and joy. This verse also can be referred to the last conversion of the Jews, when, God having mercy upon his people, after the fullness of the Gentiles entering in to the faith, all Israel, converted by divine grace to that same faith, shall be made safe.

1148C« He shall see that the hand is weakened, and that they also who were shut up have failed, and the remnant have been consumed; and he shall say: Where are their gods, in whom they had confidence, of whose victims they ate the fat and drank the wine of libations? Let them rise up and help you, and protect you in your necessity. » Another edition: « For he saw them wearied and failing in the leading away, and dissolved; and he said: Where now are their gods, in whom you trusted, in them; whose fat of sacrifices you ate, and drank the wine of their libation? Let them now rise up and help you, and become protectors to you. » He shall see, he says, that the hand is weakened, when he shall have made it to be seen that the works of the wicked, which they did in idolatry or in their other transgressions, were weak, when, reduced to nothing, they profited nothing to those who had 1148Ddone them. And they also who were shut up have failed — that is, either the Jews, led away into captivity by their enemies, were consumed there; or all the impious, handed over into the power of the demons, shall no longer prevail. And the remnant have been consumed, because, departing out of this life, they shall no longer have the power and the means of doing anything. Therefore it is written in Ecclesiastes: « Whatsoever thy hand is able to do, do it earnestly, because neither work, nor reason, nor wisdom, nor knowledge shall be in the lower regions, whither thou art hastening (Eccle. IX). » But that which is subjoined: « Where are their gods, in whom they had confidence, » and the rest, signifies that idolaters have no help at all in the day of necessity from the idols to which they had before rendered worship; 1149Anor yet the lovers of the world, who through avarice, which is the service of idols, had gathered moneys in the world, who had served delights and luxury, whose God was the belly and whose glory was unto their own confusion, who relished earthly things: they carry nothing with them out of all their labour, when for their sins they are dragged away to Tartarus by the malignant spirits.

« See ye that I alone am, and that there be no other God besides me. » Another edition: « See, see, that I am God, and there is none besides me. » This admonition pertains generally to all men, that, the falsehood of idols and the deception of heretics being left behind, and the filth of all iniquities being spurned, they may be converted to the worship, through the Catholic faith, of the one and only God, the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit; 1149Band may love him with the whole heart, the whole soul and the whole strength, and diligently keep his commandments and his judgments, so that they may deserve to be made heirs of the heavenly kingdom.

« I will kill and I will make to live; I will strike and I will heal; and there is none that can deliver out of my hand. » « Another edition: I will kill, I will make to live; I will strike and I will heal; and there is none that snatcheth away out of my hands. » In two ways does almighty God wound those whom he takes care to lead back to salvation; for sometimes he strikes the flesh, and the hardness of the mind wastes away in its own dread. By wounding, therefore, he calls back to salvation, when he afflicts his elect outwardly and quickens them inwardly. Rightly therefore does he speak through Moses, saying: « I will kill and I will make to live; I will strike and I will heal. » 1149CFor he kills that he may quicken; he strikes that he may heal, because he therefore applies blows without that he may inflict wounds within, since he strikes the hardness of our mind with desire of himself; but by striking he heals, because, having transfixed us with the javelin of his terror, he calls us back to the sense of rectitude. For our hearts are ill and unsound when they are wounded by no love of God, when they do not feel the hardship of their pilgrimage, when toward the infirmity of a neighbour they languish with not even the least affection; but they are wounded, because God strikes insensible minds with the darts of his love, and straightway renders them sensible through the ardour of charity.

« I will lift up my hand to heaven and will say: I live for ever. » Another edition: « For I will lift up my hand to heaven, and will swear by my right hand, 1149Dand will say: I live for ever. » To lift up, or to raise, the hand to heaven is to show the power of his eternity as excelling above all things; to swear by his right hand is, through the Son, who is named the right hand of God, to confirm his promises in the sight of men, and to reveal his eternity through the Gospel to those who believe in him: because his power is an eternal power, and his kingdom, which shall not be corrupted.

« If I shall sharpen my sword as the lightning, and my hand shall lay hold on judgment, I will render vengeance to my enemies, and to them that hate me I will repay. » Another edition: « For I will sharpen my sword like the lightning, and my hand doeth judgment; and I will repay judgment to my enemies, and to them that hate me I will render. » To sharpen the sword of God as the lightning 1150Ais to exercise sudden vengeance upon his enemies; and for the divine hand to lay hold on judgment is to render to each one by just retribution according to his own merits, as it is written: « For the Son of man shall come in the glory of his Father with his angels, and then will he render to every man according to his works (Rom. II). » And the Apostle: « We shall all, he says, stand before the judgment seat of Christ, that every one may receive the proper things of the body, according as he hath done, whether it be good or evil (II Cor. V). »

« I will make my arrows drunk with blood, and my sword shall devour flesh. » For an arrow strikes a man while he is not attending, and while he does not foresee it, it suddenly slays him. The arrows of the Lord are his swift vengeances, which, although for the present they be hidden before the eyes 1150Bof the wicked, shall nevertheless suddenly be made manifest in the damnation of the lost. And the sword of God devours flesh, because in the last judgment his sentence slays those who relish carnally.

« From the blood of the slain, and from the captivity of the bared head of the enemies. » Another edition: « From the blood of the wounded, from the captivity, from the head of the princes of the enemies. » To this which he says, from the blood of the enemies, there is understood that which went before: « I will make my arrows drunk. » The Lord makes his arrows drunk in the blood of the slain, in that he exercises vengeance upon the throng of his enemies; and then shall the heads of his foes be bared, when the counsels of wicked minds, by which they trusted that they could sin unpunished, shall be proved to be empty. And by the heads of the wicked may also be understood the masters 1150Cof them that err, upon whom the final vengeance overflows, because they who were the cause of sins shall feel the losses in the retribution of punishments. Whence it is written in the Apocalypse: « And blood came out of the lake, even up to the horses' bridles (Apoc. XIV), » that is, the vengeance of torments went forth even to the rulers of the peoples; for the vengeance for the blood of the saints that was shed went forth in the last contest even to the devil and his angels, as it is written: « In blood hast thou sinned, and blood shall pursue thee (Ezech. XXXV). » But it is to be noted that in this place, in the edition of the LXX interpreters, certain sentences are interposed which are not found in the Hebrew verity, in this manner.

« Rejoice, ye heavens, together with him, and let all the angels of God adore him. Rejoice, ye nations, together with his people, 1150Dand let all the sons of God confirm him. » These, however, are proved to have been rightly brought forward out of canonical authority, since Paul the apostle is found to take testimonies from them. For in the Epistle which was written by him to the Hebrews, and also in that which was likewise directed by him to the Romans, he is found to have taken testimonies thence. Accordingly, writing to the Hebrews, he brings it in thus: « And when again he bringeth in the first-begotten into the world, he saith: And let all the angels of God adore him (Hebr. I). » But to the Romans, disputing concerning the calling of the Gentiles and the fullness of the Jewish people, he says thus: « For I say that Christ Jesus was a minister of the circumcision for the truth of God, to confirm the promises of the Fathers (Rom. XV). » But that the Gentiles 1151Aare to honour God for his mercy, as it is written: « Therefore will I confess to thee among the Gentiles, O Lord, and will sing to thy name (II Reg. XXII). » And again he says: « Rejoice, ye Gentiles, with his people (Rom. XV). » Whence it stands true that the apostles equally approved both that edition which is from the Hebrew verity and that which is from the LXX interpreters, since they took testimonies from both.
