But that which he speaks to the supernal creature as though exhorting it, saying, « Rejoice, ye heavens, together with him, and let all the angels of God adore him, » shows that after the coming of the Saviour in the flesh and his universal judgment, in which the just are crowned and sinners are damned, the heavenly and the earthly creatures are gathered together alike into the praise of their Founder, and adore and venerate Christ the king, reigning and ruling over 1151Ball things, for ever. But that which follows: « Rejoice, ye nations, together with his people, and let all the sons of God confirm him confirmenteum, » is the same; because the Church of Christ, gathered out of Jews and Gentiles, after it truly receives immortality and incorruption in the resurrection of the flesh, confirms by deed that which before it was testifying by word. There follows.

« Praise, ye nations, his people, for he will avenge the blood of his servants, and will repay vengeance upon their enemies. » Another edition: « For the blood of his sons is defended, and he defendeth it; and I will repay judgment to my enemies, and to them that hate him he will render. » All the nations shall praise the people of God, when they have seen the Church of God appear glorious in the sight of her Founder, 1151Cand her persecutors thrust back, through the ministry of the malignant spirits, into the Gehenna of eternal fire, for the blood of the martyrs shed by them and for all the evils which they wrought in this life. For then the good also shall praise the good which they see, because the reward which they shall have is pleasing; and the impious, set in punishments, shall repent, though late, of the evil which they wrought, when they shall have seen the glory of the saints grow bright, which they had not hoped for: « For groaning, set in anguish, they shall say within themselves, doing penance: These are they whom we once had in derision and for a likeness of reproach. We senseless ones esteemed their life a disgrace [Vulg. insaniam, "madness"] and their end without honour. How then are they numbered among the sons of God, and is their lot among the saints? (Sap. V.)

1151D« And the Lord will be merciful to the land of his people. » Another edition: « And he will cleanse the land of his people. » The Lord therefore will cleanse the land of his people, or will be merciful to the land of his people, when he makes his Church — which hitherto he was ruling amid adversities through his ministers — plucked out and purged from all scandals, enjoying perpetual peace, to live amid the prosperities of the heavenly kingdom, where she shall rejoice without end, and shall sing praises to the Lord together with the saints for ever.

INCIPIT HYMNUS TRIUM PUERORUM.

Hymnus trium puerorum, quem Danielis prophetae 1152Aliber continet, in Hebraeo non habetur, sicut interpres noster beatus, videlicet Hieronymus, testatur, sed ex LXX, vel aliorum interpretum editione additus est, ubi et ante narratur quod angelus Domini descenderit cum Azaria et sociis ejus in fornacem, et excusserit flammam ignis de fornace, et fecerit medium fornacis quasi ventum roris flantem, et non tetigerit eos omnino ignis, neque contristaverit, nec quidquam molestiae intulerit. Potest et hic angelus Domini, qui descendit cum tribus pueris in caminum ignis ardentis, et eos protexit ab ardore flammarum, typice significare Dominum Salvatorem, quem propheta magni consilii angelum nominavit, eo quod suae et paternae annuntiator est voluntatis. Hic quoque descendit in hunc mundum, ubi rex confusionis, 1152Bid est, diabolus fornacem, hoc est, corda iniquorum igne cupiditatis atque luxuriae inflammat, ut flatu Spiritus sancti per gratiam suam electos suos ab hoc igne servet illaesos, quatenus ejus laudibus intenti pro ereptione sua gratias referant liberatori suo. De quo subditur:

« Tunc hi tres, quasi ex uno ore laudabant et glorificabant et benedicebant Dominum in fornace dicentes. » Isti ergo tres viri, qui quasi ex uno ore laudabant et glorificabant et benedicebant Dominum, significant electos Dei, qui in sanctae Trinitatis fide solidati unum Deum Patrem, et Filium, et Spiritum sanctum adorant, colunt et praedicant, laudant quidem eum recta credulitate: glorificant vera confessione, et benedicunt bona operatione. Unde etiam 1152Cconcorditer dicunt:

« Benedictus es, Domine Deus patrum nostrorum, et laudabilis et gloriosus et superexaltatus in saecula. » Deus ergo nostrorum Dominus est Christus cum Patre et Spiritu sancto unus et omnipotens Deus, quem coluerunt patriarchae, laudaverunt prophetae, praedicaverunt apostoli, omnis Christi Ecclesia confitetur. Hic semper erat, est, et erit laudabilis ac gloriosus et super omnes creaturas exaltatus regnat in saecula saeculorum.

« Benedicite omnia opera Domini Dominum. » Generali laudatione praemissa, quod omnis creatura debeat laudare Dominum, in consequentibus per partes singulas hortatur angelos et coelos, aquas atque, virtutes, solem et lunam, imbrem et rorem, 1152DSpiritum, ignem et aestatem, frigus et aestum et caetera, quae longum est texere, ita ut fontes quoque et maria ac flumina, coetos et volucres bestiasque et pecora ad laudem Domini provocet, et filios hominum, et post omne horum genus Israelem, et de ipso Israel sacerdotes et servos Domini, Spiritus animasque justorum, sanctos et humiles corde, et ad extremum Ananiam, Azariam et Misaelem, qui ad laudes Domini praesenti beneficio provocantur. Omnis autem creatura, non voce, sed opere laudat Dominum, quia ex creaturis consequenter creator intelligitur et in singulis operibus atque effectibus Dei magnificentia demonstratur. Sed quia David in Psalterio pari modo omnia opera Domini, hoc est, omnes creaturas, 1153Aad benedicendum et ad laudandum Deum provocat, restat ut sicut historicus ordo iste benedictionis atque laudabilis exstat, ita et mysterii ejus sensus spiritalis singulis speciebus convenienter aptetur. Dicit enim David juxta finem centesimi et secundi Psalmi, quem ad benedicendum Deum composuit, ita: « Benedicite Dominum omnia opera ejus in omni loco dominationis ejus. » Admonet ergo ipsa opera Domini auctorem suum debere laudare, ut terrena coelestiaque concluderet, nam cum totum opus ejus sit, omnia debent suum benedicere Creatorem, et in omni loco dominationis ejus, quia sicut nulla creatura a laudibus Domini jubetur excipi, ita nec locus quisquam relinquitur, qui tali munere segregetur. Nam cum dicitur: « in omni loco dominationis 1153Bejus, » nihil excipitur, quia ubique dominatur, significans forte Ecclesiam catholicam, quae per totum mundum erat, Domino praestante, creditura; ipsa enim ubique Dominum benedicit, ubique collaudat et hymnidica exsultatione concelebrat. Quod autem in benedictione trium puerorum singulis versibus subjungitur,

« Laudate et superexaltate eum in saecula, » hoc ostendit quod ita laudis ejus praeconium debet pronuntiari, ut ipse super omnia exaltatus in saecula regnans praedicetur.

« Benedicite, coeli, Dominum, benedicite, angeli Domini, Dominum. » Coeli enim secundum historiam benedicunt Dominum, quia in sua positione laudabilem et benedictum ostendunt creatorem. Quamvis ergo 1153Cin Genesi unum coelum fabricatum esse legerimus, tamen eos plurali numero et Paulus apostolus appellavit, qui refert in tertium se coelum raptum, ibique talia cognovisse, quae homini non licebat effari, et hic plurali numero nuncupantur, quod tali forsitan ratione concordat: dicimus unum esse palatium, quod multis membris multisque spatiis ambiatur, ita forsitan et coeli partes dicimus coelos, dum complexio atque sinus ejus unus esse monstretur, coeli vero substantia quamvis pura, lucida atque egregia esse putanda sit, dignaque talibus ac tantis habitatoribus suis, tamen animal eam esse non comperi, et temerarium est ex opinione hominum dicere, quod coelestium litterarum non tradit auctoritas: nam et coelorum nomine in scripturis Apostolos et Prophetas 1153Dinvenimus denotatos, qui de adventu Domini disserendo orbem terrarum sanctis admonitionibus impleverunt, in quibus Deus tanquam in coelis habitat, qui cuncta late complectitur, non ex parte, sed tota eos plenitudine suae majestatis. Pars enim in Deo non est, sed ubique totus et plenus est.

« Benedicite, aquae, quae super coelos sunt, Dominum. » Aquas vero super coelos esse Genesis refert; ait enim: « Divisitque aquas quae erant super firmamentum (Gen. I), » quod utrumque nimiae laudis designat arcanum, ut et coelorum tenuitas firma consistat et aquae supra ipsum positae cum sint graviores, nisi quando fuerint jussae, non defluant. Aquae vero quae super coelos sunt, ut arbitror istas quae 1154Adefluunt esse non dubium est, testante libro Genesis, quod cataractae coeli apertae sint, et facta sit pluvia super terram quadraginta diebus et quadraginta noctibus, etc., quod non adeo quaerere necessarium est, quando aut per se, aut per substantias rationales laudare Dominum omnia commonentur.

« Benedicite, omnes virtutes Domini, Dominum. » De virtutibus Dei dicitur in psalmo: « Benedicite Dominum, omnes virtutes ejus et ministri ejus, qui facitis voluntatem ejus (Psal. CII). » Quoniam illa sublimis spiritualisque substantia beatorum Angelorum, cum Dominum contuetur, ibi veritatem ejus voluntatis agnoscit, nec opus est loqui quibus se divinitas propitiata patefacit; non enim Deus, verberato aere loquitur, sed inspiramine suo voluntates 1154Beorum ad suum velle convertit.

« Benedicite, sol et luna, Dominum. Benedicite, stellae coeli, Dominum. » De quo et Psalmista dicit: « Laudate eum, sol et luna, laudate eum, omnes stellae et lumen (Psal. CXLVIII). » Sol, luna, stellae, ut quidam dicunt, etsi aliquo Spiritu proprio regantur, tamen quia carnalibus oculis patescunt inferiores esse virtutibus, quae non videntur, non absurda aestimatione colligitur, quamvis coeli speciem decoro lumine ornare videantur, nam illa, ad quae pervenit noster aspectus, licet sint lucida atque subtilia, tamen corpora esse manifestum est, et necesse est ut illis cedant quae invisibili virtute subsistunt; quapropter solem, lunam et stellas, sive per propriam rationem, sive per alias sensibiles et judicabiles substantias, 1154Chortatur propheta laudare Dominum, quia creatoris beneficio existere meruerunt. Considerata enim tot lumina ingentes admirationes poterunt commovere, quando mortales oculos videre faciunt et ipsa in tam splendida claritate consistunt. His rebus lumen generaliter adjunctum est, ut caetera lumen in se habentia, quae dinumerata non sunt, tali complexione ad laudes Dominicas incitaret.

« Benedicite, imber et ros, Dominum. » In imbre enim et rore dona coelestia et praecepta ac mandata Domini exprimuntur, quae terram, hoc est, homines irrigant et excitant ad proferendum germen bonorum operum. Dignum est enim, ut quod dono coelesti salubriter hominibus offertur, ad benedictionem et laudem Domini proficiat.

1154D« Benedicite, omnes spiritus, Dominum. » Hoc est quod in Psalterio novissime scriptum: « Omnis spiritu laudet Dominum (Psal. CL), » non utique caro et sanguis, non ambitus, non illecebrae saeculares, sed illud quod est in natura sublimius, hoc laudet Dominum, quod coelestia utique sapit, quando aeterna praemia concupiscit; quapropter universo operi brevem perfectamque sententiam dedit, ut Dominum Salvatorem spiritaliter debeant cuncta laudare.

« Benedicite, ignis et aestas, Dominum. » Ignis aliquando charitatem significat, aliquando tribulationem, sic et aestas aliquando ardorem dilectionis et viriditatem boni operis, aliquando aestum persecutionis 1155Aexprimit. Sed haec benedicunt Dominum, quando homines faciunt benedicere Creatorem suum, quia sancti viri et in prosperis et in adversis semper laudant Dominum. Unde Apostolus exhortatur: « ut in omnibus glorificetur Deus per Jesum Christum. » Similiter quatuor versus sequentes intelligi possunt, ubi dicitur:

« Benedicite, noctes et dies, Dominum. Benedicite, lux et tenebrae, Dominum. Benedicite, frigus et aestas, Dominum. Benedicite, rores et pruina, Dominum. » Per contrarias enim sibimet res laudem Domini fieri hortatur et ad benedicendum provocat. Nam dies et lux et aestas et rores prosperitatem; et econtra nox et tenebrae frigus et pruina tribulationem significant. Non solum enim elementa 1155Bmundi et tempora ad laudandum Deum habilia sunt, sed etiam ipsi homines, qui sub elementis mundi constituti temporaliter vivunt ad benedicendum Deum, debitores fiunt, quia creator omnium, sic temperat electis suis praesentia, ut ipsi perpetualiter praeconia non taceant divina, unde Paulus ait (II Cor. XII): « Libenter igitur gloriabor in infirmitatibus meis, ut inhabitet in me virtus Christi. » Et item: (II Cor. IV): « Id, inquit, quod in praesenti est momentaneum et leve tribulationis nostrae, supra modum in sublimitatem aeternum gloriae pondus operatur in nobis, non contemplantibus nobis quae videntur, » etc.

« Benedicite, gelu et frigus, Dominum. Benedicite, glacies et nives, Dominum. « In gelu et frigore, 1155Cglacie et nive praesentis temporis adversitas, vel duritia mentis humanae significatur, sed saepe haec Dominus in laudem suam convertit, dum homines infidelitatis algore torpentes per fidei calorem et bonorum operum studium ad servitium Christi convertuntur, unde scriptum est in Salomone: Sicut in sereno glacies, ita solventur peccata tua (Eccli. III). » Et in psalmo: « Ignis, inquit, grando, nix, glacies, spiritus procellarum qui faciunt verbus ejus (Psal. CXLVIII), » quibus rebus per allegoriam homines significat, qui in hoc saeculo ex turbulentis et pessimis ad devotionis tranquillissima studia pervenerunt. Quomodo isti faciunt verbum ejus, nisi quia ex tempestuosis et improbis ad confessionis ipsius gratiam venire meruerunt?

1155D« Benedicite, fulgura et nubes, Dominum. » Quid ergo per nubes, nisi Evangelii praedicatores, et quid per fulgura, nisi coruscationes miraculorum nobis demonstrantur? de quibus per Psalmistam dicitur: « Fulgura multiplicabis et conturbabis eos (Psal. XVII). » Per has ergo nubes lumine suo desuper Dominus fulgurat, quia per praedicatores sanctos insensibilitatis nostrae tenebras etiam miraculis illustrat. Cumque nubes istae verbis pluunt, cumque miraculis vim coruscae lucis aperiunt, extremos etiam mundi terminos in divinum amorem convertunt.

« Benedicat terra Dominum, lauda et superexalta eum in saecula. » Hic ergo terra sanctam Ecclesiam significat, de qua in Psalmo scriptum est: « Domini 1156Aest terra, et plenitudo ejus, orbis terrarum et universi qui habitant in ea (Psal. XXIII), » et quamvis terram in bono ac si in malo poni saepe noverimus, hic tamen Ecclesiam debemus advertere, quae Domino specialiter pura mente famulatur. Nam licet omnia abysso sint condita, tamen illud ipsius esse proprie dicimus, quod eum veneratur auctorem; et ideo Ecclesia non immerito fructifera bonorum terra suscipitur, quia nutrit et continet populum Christianum. Haec ergo terra semper laudat Deum et superexaltat eum in saecula, quia non solum in praesenti saeculo, sed etiam in futuro eum semper laudat, et super omnia dominantem praedicat.

« Benedicite, montes et colles, Dominum. Benedicite, universa germinantia in terra, Dominum. 1156BLaudate et superexaltate eum in saecula. » Hinc etiam Psalmista, ubi omnes creaturas ad laudandum Dominum provocat, inter caetera ait: « Montes et omnes colles, ligna fructifera et omnes cedri (Psal. CXLVIII). » Hic montes significat homines prae tumida potestate sublimes, colles mediocres aequalitate tractantes, ligna fructifera, qui ex duritia peccatorum conversi, fructus bonorum operum intulere dulcissimos. Cedri quoque absolute superbos significant, et elatos, sed quia mandat, quod universa terrae germinantia Dominum laudent, ostendit quod ex omnibus Christi gratia sibi electos parat, qui in illa ultima resurrectione Angelorum possint coetibus aggregari, et in laudibus Conditoris sui in aeternum associari.

1156C« Benedicite, fontes, Dominum. » Fons quoque in Scripturis aut Dominum Christum mystice significat, aut gratiam Spiritus sancti, aut baptismi lavacrum, aut originem virtutum. Nam Christum significat, in eo, quod Genesi legitur, fontem esse in medio paradisi. Unde quatuor flumina procedebant, hoc est, quatuor Evangelia de fonte salutaris procedentia ad irrigationem generis humani. Item fons Spiritus sancti gratiam designat in eo, quod Dominus dicit in Evangelio: « Qui biberit aquam quam ego do, fiet in eo fons aquae vivae salientis in vitam aeternam (Joan. IV). » Baptismi vero sacramentum ostendit illud Zachariae, quo ait: « In die illa erit fons patens domui David et habitantibus Hierusalem in ablutionem peccatoris et menstruatae (Zach. XII), 1156Dnam hic fons de domo Dei egrediens fertur ad Ecclesiam, et ad scientiam Scripturarum, ut omnes renascamur in Christo et non aqua baptismatis nostra nobis peccata donentur. Sed hic fontes possumus testimonia sacrarum Scripturarum per ora praedicatorum prolata intelligere. Unde in psalmo scriptum est: « Apparuerunt fontes aquarum (Psal. XVII), » id est, veritas praedicantium, qui fontes aeternae vitae sanctitatis ore fuderunt, ipso enim Domino veniente patuit, quod obscuritas divini tegebat eloquii.

« Benedicite, maria et flumina, Dominum. » Quando dicit maria, generaliter omnes aquarum collectiones, sive salsae sint, sive dulces simul comprehendit juxta illud, quod scriptum est: « Congregationes 1157Aaquarum appellavit maria (Gen. I.), » a quibus flumina procedunt, ut est illud: « Omnia flumina intrant in mare, et mare non redundat, ad locum unde exeunt flumina revertuntur, ut iterum fluant (Eccle. I), » ubi metaphorice aquae nomine limus terrae et in fluminibus homines intelliguntur, qui in terram, unde sumpti sunt revertuntur; sed terra eorum multitudine non impletur. Benedicere ergo jubentur homines Dominum, hoc est, auctorem suum cognoscere, illumque fide et bonis operibus colere, quia ab ipso creati sunt, et ad ejus judicium omnes revertuntur, « quatenus unusquisque propria corporis recipiat, prout gessit, sive bonum, sive malum. » (II Cor. V).

« Benedicite, ceti et omnia quae moventur in aquis, 1157BDominum: laudate et superexaltate eum in saecula. » In cetis ergo, qui pro magnitudine corporis sui montibus comparantur, et caeteris animantibus, quae in mari versantur praeeminent, potentes istius mundi exprimi possunt. Hi cum caeteris hominibus, qui hoc saeculo versantur ad laudandum Creatorem suum provocantur, cujus munere omnes subsistunt, et omnes nutriuntur, quia dignum est ut magni et parvi omnipotentem Conditorem suum laudent et in saeculum benedicant.

« Benedicite, volucres coeli, Dominum. » In volucribus ergo philosophi accipi possunt, qui cogitationum suarum velocitate naturas rerum et cursus siderum discutere censuerunt; sive etiam altiori sensu angeli volucrum nomine designati intelligi possunt, qui 1157Cad custodiam hominum in mundum a Deo mittuntur, ut eorum curam gerant, et contra spiritales nequitias illos defendant, quia aequum est ut omnes praelati simul et subditi omnium dominatorem benedicant, « qui fecit coelum et terram, maria et omnia quae in eis sunt. »

« Benedicite, omnes bestiae et pecora, Dominum: laudate et superexaltate eum in saecula. » In bestiis crudeles et indomiti homines significantur et in pecoribus omne vulgus et infinitae plebes. Nam tales Propheta hortatur, ut conversi a crudelitate et superbia ad humilitatem et innocentiam revertantur, ita ut corde, ore et opere benedicant factorem suum, a quo perfecti sunt coeli et terra et omnis ornatus eorum.

1157D« Benedicite, filii hominum, Dominum: benedicat Israel Dominum. » In filiis quidem hominum rationabiliter viventes, et in Israel divinae contemplationi vacantes designantur. Hos ergo ad benedicendum Deum invitat, quia valde dignum et justum est ut qui super caeteras creaturas terrae, hoc ab opificis sui largitate perceperunt, ut eum veraciter agnoscentes recta fide ac bonis operibus colere et mundo corde atque pura intentione contemplari atque diligere possint, nunquam a laudibus ejus cessent, sed in aeternum benedicentes praedicent.

« Benedicite, sacerdotes Domini, Dominum: benedicite, servi Domini, Dominum. » In sacerdotibus magistros Ecclesiae exprimit, in servis vero subjectorum 1158Aplebem. Omnes ergo adhortatur, ut praelati simul et subditi Dominum Christum, cujus munere sunt sanctificati atque mundati semper benedicant simul cum Patre et Spiritu sancto regnantem in saecula saeculorum.

« Benedicite, spiritus et animae justorum, Dominum. » In spiritus ergo et animae vocabulo totam conditionem interioris hominis comprehendit, qui spiritu sapit et anima vivit. Mandat ergo Propheta ut justorum spiritus et animae benedicant Dominum, quia illi solummodo veraciter benedicunt Dominum, qui justitiae et veritatis regulam servant, horumque benedictiones divinitas gratanter accipit, qui recta fide, vera confessione ac bona operatione juste vivendo illum collaudant.

1158B« Benedicite, sancti et humiles corde, Dominum. » Humilitatem cordis habere et praesens versiculus docet, et illud, quod in Evangelio legitur: « Discite a me quia mitis sum et humilis corde, et invenietis requiem animabus vestris (Matth. XI). » Cordis autem illa est humilitas, quae in alio loco paupertas spiritus appellatur, ut non erigamur superbia, nec ficta humilitate gloriam terrenam quaeramus, sed toto corde inclinemur

« Benedicite, Anania, Azaria et Misael, Dominum: hymmum dicite, laudate et superexaltate eum in saecula. Quia eruit nos de inferno, et salvos fecit de manu mortis et liberavit de medio ardentis flammae, et de medio ignis eruit nos. » Novissime vero tres pueri semetipsos ad benedicendum 1158CDominum exhortantur, et merito ordinem creaturae ob liberationem eorum commutavit, ita ut nec corpora eorum ignis laedere, nec vestimenta flamma comburere posset, cui hymnus est debitus, et laudatio conveniens, quia ipse semper laudabilis et super omnia exaltatus manet in saecula saeculorum. Post has quoque benedictiones additum est a sanctis Patribus id, quod modo canitur in Ecclesia, hoc est sanctae Trinitatis laus et confessio juxta hunc modum.

« Benedicamus Patrem et Filium cum sancto Spiritu, laudemus et superexaltemus eum in saecula. » Quia unus et solus Deus est, Pater et Filius et Spiritus sanctus trinus in personis et unus in substantia, qui creavit coelum et terram, mare et omnia, quae 1158Din eis sunt, cujus laudem praedicare et nomen ejus super omnia fas est confiteri omnibus qui illum credunt, et ab eo vitam aeternam accipere volunt ejusque bonitate frui desiderant in regno coelesti. Hoc autem quod sequitur in hoc Cantico ante praelatum est in libro prophetico, ubi priusquam provocarentur opera Domini ad benedicendum Dominum, ita scriptum legitur: « Benedictus est in firmamento coeli, et laudabilis et gloriosus in saecula. » Firmamentum enim coeli aut angelicas potestates significat, in quibus, Dominus perpetualiter regnat, vel scientiam sanctarum Scripturarum, ubi totius boni et veritatis solidamentum est, et majestas ac bonitas Creatoris assidue laudatur et praedicatur. Hinc quoque 1159Ascriptum est: Coelum plicabitur, sicut liber involutus, et alibi; firmamentum est Dominus timentibus eum et testamentum ipsius, ut manifestetur illis: quicunque enim hic veraciter Deum diligunt et mandata ejus custodire appetunt, laudibusque Dei non solum ore, sed etiam corde assidue vacare student, ex Scripturis sacris consolationem congruam accipientes de die in diem proficient, ac sic sine ulla dubitatione per gratiam Dei post finem hujus vitae hoc, quod diu hic quaesierant in regno coelesti sine fine percipient.

HERE BEGINS THE HYMN OF THE THREE CHILDREN.

The hymn of the three children, which the book of the prophet Daniel 1152Acontains, is not found in the Hebrew, as our blessed translator — Jerome, that is — testifies, but was added out of the edition of the Seventy, or of other translators; and there it is also related beforehand that an angel of the Lord went down with Azarias and his companions into the furnace, and struck the flame of the fire out of the furnace, and made the midst of the furnace like a wind of dew blowing, and that the fire touched them not at all, nor grieved them, nor brought upon them any trouble whatever. And this angel of the Lord, who went down with the three children into the furnace of burning fire and protected them from the burning of the flames, can typically signify the Lord and Saviour, whom the prophet named the angel of great counsel, in that he is the announcer of his own and of his Father's will. He also came down into this world, where the king of confusion — 1152Bthat is, the devil — sets the furnace, that is, the hearts of the wicked, aflame with the fire of covetousness and of wantonness, in order that by the breath of the Holy Spirit, through his own grace, he might keep his elect unharmed from this fire, so that, intent upon his praises, they might render thanks to their deliverer for their rescue. Concerning whom it is added:

« Then these three, as with one mouth, praised and glorified and blessed the Lord in the furnace, saying. » These three men, then, who as with one mouth praised and glorified and blessed the Lord, signify the elect of God, who, made solid in the faith of the holy Trinity, adore, worship and preach one God the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: they praise him indeed by right believing, they glorify him by true confession, and they bless him by good working. Whence also 1152Cthey say with one accord:

« Blessed art thou, O Lord God of our fathers, and praiseworthy and glorious and exalted above all unto the ages. » Therefore the God of our own is Christ the Lord, with the Father and the Holy Spirit one and almighty God, whom the patriarchs worshipped, whom the prophets praised, whom the apostles preached, whom the whole Church of Christ confesses. He always was, is, and will be praiseworthy and glorious, and, exalted above all creatures, he reigns unto the ages of ages.

« Bless ye the Lord, all ye works of the Lord. » A general act of praise having been set first — namely that every creature ought to praise the Lord — in what follows he exhorts, part by part, the angels and the heavens, the waters and, the powers, the sun and the moon, the shower and the dew, 1152Dthe Spirit, fire and summer, cold and heat, and the rest, which it would be long to weave together; so that he calls forth also the fountains and the seas and the rivers, the whales and the birds and the beasts and the cattle to the praise of the Lord, and the sons of men; and after every kind of these, Israel; and out of that same Israel, the priests and the servants of the Lord, the spirits and souls of the just, the holy and humble of heart; and last of all Ananias, Azarias and Misael, who are called forth to the praises of the Lord by the benefit then present. Now every creature praises the Lord not with a voice but by its work, since from the creatures the creator is consequently understood, and in each several work and effect the magnificence of God is displayed. But because David in the Psalter in like manner calls forth all the works of the Lord — that is, all creatures — 1153Ato bless and to praise God, it remains that, just as this order of blessing stands historical and praiseworthy, so also the spiritual sense of its mystery should be fittingly adapted to each several kind. For David says, near the end of the hundred and second Psalm, which he composed for the blessing of God, thus: « Bless ye the Lord, all his works, in every place of his dominion. » He admonishes, therefore, that the works of the Lord themselves ought to praise their author, so as to include things earthly and heavenly; for since the whole is his work, all things ought to bless their Creator, and that in every place of his dominion, because just as no creature is bidden to be excepted from the praises of the Lord, so neither is any place left over that should be set apart from such an office. For when it is said, « in every place of his dominion, » 1153Bnothing is excepted, since he has dominion everywhere — signifying perhaps the catholic Church, which was, by the Lord's granting, to believe throughout the whole world; for she everywhere blesses the Lord, everywhere praises him together, and celebrates him with hymn-singing exultation. And as to what is subjoined to each several verse in the blessing of the three children,

« Praise and exalt him above all unto the ages, » this shows that the proclamation of his praise ought so to be uttered that he himself be preached as reigning, exalted above all things, unto the ages.

« Bless ye the Lord, O heavens, bless ye the Lord, O angels of the Lord. » For the heavens according to the history bless the Lord, because in their own position they show their creator to be praiseworthy and blessed. Although, then, 1153Cwe have read in Genesis that one heaven was fashioned, nevertheless Paul the apostle also called them by a plural number, who relates that he was caught up into the third heaven and there came to know such things as it was not lawful for a man to utter; and here they are named by a plural number, which agrees perhaps by such a reasoning as this: we say that a palace is one, which is compassed about with many chambers and many spaces; so perhaps we also call the parts of heaven "heavens," while its embrace and bosom is shown to be one. But although the substance of heaven is to be thought pure, shining and excellent, and worthy of such and so great inhabitants of its own, yet I have not found it to be a living creature; and it is rash to say, out of the opinion of men, what the authority of the heavenly letters does not hand down. For under the name of the heavens we also find in the Scriptures the Apostles and Prophets 1153Ddenoted, who, discoursing of the Lord's coming, filled the round world with holy admonitions, in whom God dwells as in the heavens — he who embraces all things widely, not in part, but them wholly with the fullness of his majesty. For in God there is no part, but he is everywhere whole and full.

« Bless ye the Lord, O ye waters that are above the heavens. » That there are waters above the heavens Genesis relates; for it says: « And he divided the waters that were above the firmament (Gen. I), » each of which designates a mystery of exceeding praise: that the thinness of the heavens should stand firm, and that the waters set above it, though they are heavier, should not flow down save when they have been commanded. But as for the waters that are above the heavens, it is not doubtful, as I judge, that those which 1154Aflow down are these, the book of Genesis bearing witness that the floodgates of heaven were opened and rain came upon the earth for forty days and forty nights, and so forth — which it is not so necessary to inquire into, when all things are admonished to praise the Lord either of themselves or through rational substances.

« Bless ye the Lord, all ye powers of the Lord. » Of the powers of God it is said in the psalm: « Bless ye the Lord, all ye his powers and his ministers, that do his will (Psal. CII). » For since that lofty and spiritual substance of the blessed Angels, when it beholds the Lord, there recognizes the truth of his will, there is no need of speech for those to whom the divinity, made favourable, lays itself open; for God does not speak by beating the air, but by his own inbreathing turns their wills 1154Bto his own willing.

« Bless ye the Lord, O sun and moon. Bless ye the Lord, O stars of heaven. » Of which the Psalmist also says: « Praise him, O sun and moon; praise him, all ye stars and light (Psal. CXLVIII). » The sun, the moon and the stars, as some say, even if they are governed by some spirit of their own, yet, because they lie open to fleshly eyes, are gathered by no absurd estimation to be inferior to the powers which are not seen — although they seem to adorn the face of heaven with comely light; for those things to which our sight reaches, though they be shining and subtle, are yet manifestly bodies, and it must needs be that they yield to those things which subsist by an invisible power. Wherefore the prophet exhorts the sun, the moon and the stars — whether through a reason of their own or through other substances that have sense and judgment — 1154Cto praise the Lord, because by the creator's benefit they have deserved to exist. For so many lights, when considered, will be able to stir up vast wonderings, since they make mortal eyes to see and themselves stand fast in so splendid a brightness. To these things light has been joined generally, that by such a comprehension he might stir up to the Lord's praises the other things having light in themselves which are not numbered.

« Bless ye the Lord, O shower and dew. » For in the shower and the dew are expressed the heavenly gifts and the precepts and commandments of the Lord, which water the earth — that is, men — and rouse it to bring forth the seed of good works. For it is meet that what is offered to men healthfully by a heavenly gift should go forward to the blessing and praise of the Lord.

1154D« Bless ye the Lord, all ye spirits. » This is what is written last in the Psalter: « Let everyone praise the Lord in spirit (Psal. CL) » [Vulg. omnis spiritus laudet Dominum, "let every spirit praise the Lord"] — not indeed flesh and blood, not ambition, not worldly enticements, but let that which is more sublime in nature praise the Lord, which savours of heavenly things when it desires the eternal rewards. Wherefore to the whole work he gave a brief and perfect sentence, that all things should spiritually praise the Lord and Saviour.

« Bless ye the Lord, O fire and summer. » Fire sometimes signifies charity, sometimes tribulation; so also summer sometimes expresses the ardour of love and the greenness of good work, sometimes the heat of persecution 1155A. [Vulg. aestus, "heat", where Migne prints aestas, "summer"] But these bless the Lord when they make men bless their Creator, because holy men both in prosperity and in adversity always praise the Lord. Whence the Apostle exhorts: « that in all things God may be glorified through Jesus Christ. » In like manner the four following verses can be understood, where it is said:

« Bless ye the Lord, O nights and days. Bless ye the Lord, O light and darkness. Bless ye the Lord, O cold and summer. Bless ye the Lord, O dews and hoar-frost. » [Vulg. frigus et aestus, "cold and heat"] For by things contrary to one another he exhorts that praise of the Lord be made, and calls forth to blessing. For day and light and summer and dews signify prosperity; and on the contrary night and darkness, cold and hoar-frost signify tribulation. For not only the elements 1155Bof the world and the seasons are fit for praising God, but even men themselves, who, set beneath the elements of the world, live temporally, become debtors for the blessing of God; because the creator of all things so tempers things present for his elect that they themselves may never be silent of the divine proclamations. Whence Paul says (II Cor. XII): « Gladly therefore will I glory in my infirmities, that the power of Christ may dwell in me. » And again (II Cor. IV): « That, he says, which is at present momentary and light of our tribulation worketh in us above measure, unto sublimity, an eternal weight of glory, while we look not at the things which are seen, » and so forth.

« Bless ye the Lord, O frost and cold. Bless ye the Lord, O ice and snows. « In frost and cold, 1155Cice and snow is signified the adversity of the present time, or the hardness of the human mind; but often the Lord turns these to his own praise, when men numb with the chill of unbelief are converted to the service of Christ through the warmth of faith and the zeal of good works. Whence it is written in Solomon: As ice in the fair weather, so shall thy sins be dissolved (Eccli. III). » And in the psalm: « Fire, he says, hail, snow, ice, stormy winds which do his verbus (Psal. CXLVIII), » by which things he signifies through allegory the men who in this world have come from turbulent and most wicked ways to the most tranquil pursuits of devotion. How do these do his word, save because from being tempestuous and shameless they have deserved to come to the grace of confessing him?

1155D« Bless ye the Lord, O lightnings and clouds. » What, then, is shown us by the clouds but the preachers of the Gospel, and what by the lightnings but the flashings of miracles? Of which it is said through the Psalmist: « Thou shalt multiply lightnings and shalt trouble them (Psal. XVII). » [Vulg. fulgora multiplicavit et conturbavit eos, "he multiplied lightnings and troubled them"] Through these clouds, then, the Lord lightens from above with his own light, because through his holy preachers he illumines the darkness of our insensibility even with miracles. And when these clouds rain with words, and when by miracles they open the force of flashing light, they turn even the farthest bounds of the world to divine love.

« Let the earth bless the Lord: praise and exalt him above all unto the ages. » [Vulg. laudet et superexaltet, "let it praise and exalt him"] Here, then, the earth signifies the holy Church, of which it is written in the Psalm: « The earth 1156Ais the Lord's, and the fullness thereof, the round world and all they that dwell in it (Psal. XXIII); » and although we have often known "earth" to be set in a good sense as well as in a bad, yet here we ought to perceive the Church, which serves the Lord in a special manner with a pure mind. For though all things have been founded by the abyss, nevertheless we properly say that to be his which venerates him as its author; and therefore the Church is not undeservedly received as the fruitful land of good things, because it nourishes and holds the Christian people. This earth, then, always praises God and exalts him above all unto the ages, because not only in the present world but also in that to come it always praises him, and preaches him as lord over all things.

« Bless ye the Lord, O mountains and hills. Bless ye the Lord, all ye things that spring up in the earth. 1156BPraise and exalt him above all unto the ages. » Hence also the Psalmist, where he calls forth all creatures to praise the Lord, says among the rest: « Mountains and all hills, fruitful trees and all cedars (Psal. CXLVIII). » Here the mountains signify men lifted up by swollen power; the hills, men of middle station dealing with others on equal terms; the fruitful trees, those who, converted from the hardness of their sins, have brought forth the sweetest fruits of good works. The cedars also signify absolutely the proud and the puffed up; but because he commands that all things springing up in the earth should praise the Lord, he shows that out of all men the grace of Christ prepares elect ones for itself, who in that last resurrection may be able to be gathered into the companies of the Angels and to be joined for ever in the praises of their Founder.

1156C« Bless ye the Lord, O fountains. » A fountain too in the Scriptures either mystically signifies the Lord Christ, or the grace of the Holy Spirit, or the laver of baptism, or the origin of the virtues. For it signifies Christ in that which is read in Genesis, that there was a fountain in the midst of paradise. Whence four rivers proceeded — that is, the four Gospels proceeding from the fountain of salvation for the watering of the human race. Again, a fountain designates the grace of the Holy Spirit in that which the Lord says in the Gospel: « He that shall drink the water that I give, there shall be made in him a fountain of living water springing up into life everlasting (Joan. IV). » [Vulg. fons aquae salientis, without vivae, "living"] But the sacrament of baptism is shown by that of Zacharias, where he says: « In that day there shall be a fountain open to the house of David and to the inhabitants of Jerusalem for the washing of the sinner and of the unclean woman (Zach. XII), 1156Dfor this fountain, going forth from the house of God, is borne to the Church and to the knowledge of the Scriptures, that we may all be born again in Christ and that our sins be not forgiven us by the water of baptism. But here we can understand by the fountains the testimonies of the sacred Scriptures brought forth through the mouths of the preachers. Whence it is written in the psalm: « The fountains of waters appeared (Psal. XVII), » that is, the truth of those who preach, who poured out with the mouth of holiness the fountains of eternal life; for when the Lord himself came, that lay open which the obscurity of the divine utterance had covered.

« Bless ye the Lord, O seas and rivers. » When he says "seas," he generally comprehends together all gatherings of waters, whether they be salt or fresh, according to that which is written: « The gatherings 1157Aof the waters he called seas (Gen. I.), » from which the rivers proceed, as is that saying: « All the rivers enter into the sea, and the sea doth not overflow; unto the place whence the rivers come out, thither they return, that they may flow again (Eccle. I), » where metaphorically by the name of water is understood the slime of the earth, and in the rivers, men, who return into the earth from which they were taken; but the earth is not filled with their multitude. Men are bidden, therefore, to bless the Lord — that is, to acknowledge their author, and to worship him by faith and by good works, because they were created by him and all return to his judgment, « so that every one may receive the proper things of the body, according as he hath done, whether good or evil. » (II Cor. V).

« Bless ye the Lord, O whales and all things that move in the waters: 1157Bpraise and exalt him above all unto the ages. » In the whales, then, which for the greatness of their body are compared to mountains, and stand out above the other living creatures that move in the sea, the mighty of this world can be expressed. These, with the other men who move in this world, are called forth to praise their Creator, by whose bounty all subsist and all are nourished; because it is meet that great and small should praise their almighty Founder and bless him unto the age.

« Bless ye the Lord, O birds of heaven. » In the birds, then, the philosophers can be taken, who by the swiftness of their thoughts have judged it right to examine the natures of things and the courses of the stars; or else, in a higher sense, angels can be understood as designated by the name of birds, who 1157Care sent by God into the world for the keeping of men, that they may have care of them and defend them against the spiritual wickednesses; because it is right that all prelates together with their subjects should bless the ruler of all, « who made heaven and earth, the seas and all things that are in them. »

« Bless ye the Lord, all ye beasts and cattle: praise and exalt him above all unto the ages. » In the beasts are signified cruel and untamed men, and in the cattle all the common sort and the countless throngs. For the Prophet exhorts such men that, being converted from cruelty and pride, they may return to humility and innocence, so that with heart, mouth and work they may bless their maker, by whom were perfected the heavens and the earth and all their furniture.

1157D« Bless ye the Lord, O ye sons of men: let Israel bless the Lord. » In the sons of men indeed are designated those who live rationally, and in Israel those who are free for divine contemplation. These, then, he invites to bless God, because it is exceedingly meet and just that they who above the other creatures of the earth have received this from the bounty of their maker — that, truly acknowledging him, they may be able to worship him with right faith and good works, and with a clean heart and pure intention to contemplate and to love him — should never cease from his praises, but blessing him should preach him for ever.

« Bless ye the Lord, O priests of the Lord: bless ye the Lord, O servants of the Lord. » In the priests he expresses the masters of the Church, but in the servants the 1158Apeople of the subject. He exhorts all of them, therefore, that prelates together with their subjects should always bless the Lord Christ, by whose bounty they have been sanctified and cleansed, reigning together with the Father and the Holy Spirit unto the ages of ages.

« Bless ye the Lord, O spirits and souls of the just. » Under the term of spirit and soul, then, he comprehends the whole condition of the inner man, who savours by the spirit and lives by the soul. The Prophet commands, therefore, that the spirits and souls of the just bless the Lord, because those alone truly bless the Lord who keep the rule of justice and truth; and the divinity gladly receives the blessings of those who, living justly by right faith, true confession and good working, praise him together.

1158B« Bless ye the Lord, O holy and humble of heart. » That we should have humility of heart both this present little verse teaches, and that which is read in the Gospel: « Learn of me, because I am meek and humble of heart, and you shall find rest to your souls (Matth. XI). » Now that is humility of heart which in another place is called poverty of spirit, that we be not lifted up with pride, nor with a feigned humility seek earthly glory, but that we be bowed down with the whole heart

« Bless ye the Lord, O Ananias, Azarias and Misael: sing ye a hymmum, praise and exalt him above all unto the ages. For he hath delivered us from hell, and saved us out of the hand of death, and freed us out of the midst of the burning flame, and out of the midst of the fire hath he delivered us. » But last of all the three children exhort themselves to bless 1158Cthe Lord; and deservedly he changed the order of creation for their deliverance, so that neither could the fire hurt their bodies nor the flame burn their garments — to whom a hymn is due and praise befitting, because he himself is always praiseworthy and, exalted above all things, remains unto the ages of ages. After these blessings also there was added by the holy Fathers that which is now sung in the Church, that is, the praise and confession of the holy Trinity after this manner.

« Let us bless the Father and the Son with the Holy Spirit; let us praise and exalt him above all unto the ages. » For there is one and only God, the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, threefold in persons and one in substance, who created heaven and earth, the sea and all things that 1158Dare in them; whose praise it is right for all to proclaim, and whose name above all things it is right to confess, for all who believe in him and wish to receive eternal life from him and desire to enjoy his goodness in the heavenly kingdom. But that which follows in this Canticle was set before in the prophetic book, where, before the works of the Lord were called forth to bless the Lord, it is read written thus: « Blessed is he in the firmament of heaven, and praiseworthy and glorious unto the ages. » [Vulg. benedictus es, "Blessed art thou"] For the firmament of heaven signifies either the angelic powers, in which the Lord reigns perpetually, or the knowledge of the holy Scriptures, where is the solid ground of all good and of truth, and where the majesty and goodness of the Creator is continually praised and preached. Hence also 1159Ait is written: Heaven shall be folded together as a book rolled up; and elsewhere: The Lord is a firmament to them that fear him, and his testament, that it may be made manifest to them. For whosoever here truly love God and seek to keep his commandments, and study to be continually free for the praises of God not only with the mouth but also with the heart, receiving fitting consolation from the sacred Scriptures, will advance from day to day; and so, without any doubt, by the grace of God, after the end of this life they will receive without end in the heavenly kingdom that which they long sought here.

INCIPIT CANTICUM ZACHARIAE Patris B. Joannis Baptistae.

  • 1159B

Igitur Zacharias, pater beati Joannis Baptistae, licet propter dubietatem mentis diu fuisset expers eloquio, munere tamen non est privatus divino: sed edito puero, cujus nomen tacendo conscripserat, gratia sancti Spiritus illustratus, quid futurum esset in Dei populo prophetando pronuntiavit dicens:

« Benedictus Dominus Deus Israel, quia visitavit et fecit redemptionem plebis suae. » Quanta superni muneris est largitas, si prompta ad accipiendum nostrae fidei sit pietas, perpendere possumus. Ecce loquela, quae sola est ablata diffidenti, cum spiritu prophetiae est restituta credenti. Visitavit autem Dominus plebem suam, quasi longa infirmitate tabescentem, et quasi venditam sub peccato, 1159Cunici filii sui sanguine redemit, quod quia beatus Zacharias proxime faciendum cognoverat, prophetico more quasi jam factum narrat. Et notandum, quod visitasse et redemisse plebem suam dicitur. Non quia videlicet ad hanc veniens suam invenit, sed quia visitando suam fecit.

« Et erexit cornu salutis nobis in domo David pueri sui. » Cornu salutis firmam celsitudinem salutis dicit. Ossa siquidem omnia carne involuta sunt. Cornu excedit carnem, et ideo cornu salutis regnum Salvatoris Christi vocatur, quo mundo spiritalis, et quae carnis gaudia superet, altitudo nuntiatur: in cujus figuram David vel Salomon cornu olei sunt in regni gloriam consecrati.

« Sicut locutus est per os sanctorum, qui a saeculo 1159Dsunt prophetarum ejus. » A saeculo, inquit, quia ex tota Veteris Instrumenti Scriptura prophetia de Christo processit, nec soli Jeremias, Daniel, et Isaias caeterique tales, qui et prophetae specialiter appellati, et ejus adventu manifeste sunt locuti; verum ipse pater Adam, Abel et Enoch, et caeteri Patrum factis quique suis ejus dispensationi testimonium reddunt. Unde Dominus ipse Judaeorum duritiam redarguens ait: « Si crederetis Moysi, crederetis forsitan mihi: de enim ille scripsit (Joan. V). »

« Salutem ex inimicis nostris et de manu omnium, qui oderunt nos. » Jungendum est a superiori versiculo, Erexit nobis, id est, erexit nobis salutem ex inimicis nostris, cum enim primo breviter praemisisset: 1160A« et erexit cornu salutis nobis, » continuo quasi apertius quid erexit explanans. « Salutem, inquit, ex inimicis nostris et de manu omnium qui oderunt nos. » Omnes autem qui oderunt nos, vel homines perversos, vel immundos spiritus significat, de quorum manu et interim spe salvi facti sumus, et in futuro reipsa salvandi. Quod autem in quibusdam Ecclesiis canitur, « et liberavit nos ab inimicis nostris, et de manu omnium qui nos oderunt, » idem est, quia tunc plene salutem ex inimicis nostris accipiemus, quando de eorum insidiis et seductionibus perfecte liberati fuerimus, ut misericordiam quam, per gratiam Dei et Patrum merita, hic ex parte accipimus, in futura vita pleniter possideamus. Unde et sequitur:

1160B« Ad faciendam misericordiam cum patribus nostris, et memorari testamenti sui sancti, jusjurandum, quod juravit ad Abraham patrem nostrum daturum se nobis, ut sine timore de manibus inimicorum nostrorum liberati serviamus illi. » Dixerat Dominum juxta eloquia prophetarum in domo David nasciturum, dicit eumdem ad explendum testamentum, quod Abrahae disposuit, nos esse liberaturum, quia videlicet his praecipue patriarchis de suo semine vel congregatio gentium, vel Christi est incarnatio promissa, quod Matthaeus quoque breviter intimare voluit, qui Evangelii sui principium hoc modo caepit: « Liber generationis Jesu Christi filii David, filii Abraham. » Ubi notandum, quod apud utrumque evangelistam David praefertur Abrahae, quia 1160Cet si temporis ordine posterior, promissionis tamen est munere major. Abrahae namque qui adhuc in praeputio positus propria dereliquit, Deum cognovit, fidei testimonium meruit, fides tantum gentium et sacrosanctus Ecclesiae coetus est promissus, dicente ad eum Domino, « atque in te benedicentur universae cognationes terrae; » David autem oraculo sublimiore, quod ex ipso Christus secundum carnem nasciturus esset audivit: « Cumque impleveris, inquit, dies tuos, ut vadas ad patres tuos, suscitabo semen tuum post te, quod erit de filiis tuis et stabiliam regnum ejus. Ipse aedificabit mihi domum, et firmabo solium ejus usque in aeternum. Ego ero ei in patrem, et ipse erit mihi in filium (I Par. XVII). »

« In sanctitate et justitia coram ipso omnibus diebus 1160Dnostris. » Aperte et breviter quomodo sit Domino serviendum designat, videlicet et in sanctitate ac justitia, et coram ipso, et omnibus diebus. Nam qui vel ante mortem ab ejus servitio discedit, immunditia qualibet, sive injustitia, fidei suae sinceritatem commaculat, vel coram homnibus tantum, et non etiam coram Domino sanctus perdurare contendit, et justus, necdum perfecte de manu spiritualium inimicorum liberatus Domino servit, sed exemplo veterum Samaritanorum diis gentium pariter et Domino servire conatur.

« Et tu, puer, Propheta Altissimi vocaberis. Praeibis enim ante faciem Domini, parare vias ejus. » Pulchre cum de Domino loqueretur ad Prophetam 1161Arepente sua verba convertit, ut hoc quoque beneficium esse Domini designaretur. Verum quaeritur quomodo octo dierum alloquatur infantem? sed qua ratione vocem patris non audierit natus, qui salutationem Mariae adhuc utero clausus audivit? nisi forte putandus est Zacharias propter eos potius, qui aderant, instruendos futura sui filii munera, quae dudum per angelum didicerat, mox, ut loqui potuit, praedicare voluisse. Audiant sane Ariani et erubescant, audiant mansueti et laetentur, quod Christum Dominum, quem Joannes prophetando praeibat, altissimum vocat, juxta quod Psalmista Deum hominemque perfectum in una persona collaudans ait: « Mater Sion dicet: homo et homo factus in ea, et ipse fundavit eam Altissimus (Psal. LXXXVI), » eum factum 1161Bqui fundavit eumdem hominem quem altissimum nuncupat.

« Ad dandam scientiam salutis plebi ejus in remissionem peccatorum eorum. » Quasi Jesus idem ac Salvatoris nomen exponere ac diligentius commendare desiderans, salutis mentionem frequentat, cum cornu salutis erigendum, salutem ex inimicis futuram, scientiam salutis plebi dandam commemorat; sed ne temporalem carnalemque promitti salutem putares, « in remissionem, inquit, peccatorum eorum. » Verum Judaei non ideo Jesum Christum suscipere, sed Antichristum malunt exspectare; quia non intus, sed foris salvari, non a peccati dominio, sed ab humanae servitutis cupiunt jugo liberari.

« Per viscera misericordiae Dei nostri, in quibus 1161Cvisitavit nos oriens ex alto. » Et propheta de Domino loquens: « Ecce vir, inquit, oriens nomen ejus, (Zach. VI), » qui ideo recte oriens vocatur, quia nobis ortum verae lucis aperiens filios noctis et tenebrarum lucis effecit filios, juxta quod beatus Zacharias consequenter exponit dicens:

« Illuminare his qui in tenebris et umbra mortis sedent ad dirigendos pedes nostros in viam pacis. » Sedentibus quippe in tenebris et umbra mortis Domino illuminare, est his qui in peccatis et ignorantiae caecitate vixerint agnitionis amorisque sui radios infundere, qualibus dicit Apostolus (Ephes. V): « Fuistis aliquando tenebrae, nunc autem lux in Domino. » Pedes autem nostri in viam pacis diriguntur, cum actionum nostrarum iter per omnia Redemptoris 1161Dilluminatorisque nostri gratiae concordat; congruo sane ordine primo illuminanda corda et post opera dirigenda testatur, quia nemo, quam non ante didicerit pacem valet operari. Unde recte Psalmista. « Inquire, inquit, pacem et sequere eam (Psal. XXXIII), » quod est dicere, illuminare, qui in tenebris sedebas, et viam pacis, a qua diutius exsulabas, ingredere.

HERE BEGINS THE CANTICLE OF ZECHARIAH Father of the Blessed John the Baptist.

  • 1159B

Zechariah, therefore, the father of blessed John the Baptist, although on account of the doubting of his mind he had long been without speech, was nevertheless not deprived of the divine gift; but when the child was brought forth, whose name he had written down while keeping silence, being enlightened by the grace of the Holy Spirit he pronounced by prophesying what was to come to pass among the people of God, saying:

« Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, because he hath visited and wrought the redemption of his people. » How great is the bounty of the heavenly gift, if the devotion of our faith be ready to receive it, we are able to weigh. Behold, speech, which alone was taken away from him when he doubted, was restored to him when he believed, and with the spirit of prophecy besides. And the Lord visited his people, as it were wasting away with a long infirmity, and, as it were sold under sin, 1159Che redeemed them by the blood of his only Son; and because blessed Zechariah knew that this was shortly to be done, after the manner of prophets he relates it as though already done. And it is to be noted that he is said to have visited and redeemed his people. Not, that is to say, because coming to it he found it his own, but because by visiting it he made it his own.

« And he hath raised up a horn of salvation to us, in the house of David his servant. » By the horn of salvation he means the firm loftiness of salvation. For all bones are wrapped about with flesh. The horn exceeds the flesh, and therefore the kingdom of Christ the Saviour is called the horn of salvation, by which there is announced to the world a height that is spiritual and that surpasses the joys of the flesh: in figure of which David and Solomon were consecrated with a horn of oil unto the glory of the kingdom.

« As he spoke by the mouth of his holy prophets, who are 1159Dfrom the beginning. » From the beginning, he says, because out of the whole Scripture of the Old Instrument prophecy concerning Christ went forth; and not Jeremias, Daniel and Isaias alone and the rest of that sort, who were both specially called prophets and spoke openly of his coming; but father Adam himself, and Abel and Enoch, and the rest of the Fathers each by his own deeds give testimony to his dispensation. Whence the Lord himself, reproving the hardness of the Jews, says: « If you believed Moses, you would perhaps believe me: for of, he wrote (Joan. V). »

« Salvation from our enemies, and from the hand of all that hate us. » It is to be joined with the verse above, He hath raised up for us — that is, he hath raised up for us salvation from our enemies; for when he had first briefly premised, 1160A« and he hath raised up a horn of salvation to us, » he immediately explains, as it were more openly, what he raised up: « Salvation, he says, from our enemies, and from the hand of all that hate us. » And by all that hate us he signifies either perverse men or unclean spirits, from whose hand we have both meanwhile been made safe in hope, and are in time to come to be saved in very deed. And as to what is sung in certain Churches, « and he hath delivered us from our enemies, and from the hand of all that hate us, » it is the same thing; because we shall then fully receive salvation from our enemies when we have been perfectly freed from their snares and seductions, so that the mercy which, through the grace of God and the merits of the Fathers, we here receive in part, we may possess more fully in the life to come. Whence there also follows:

1160B« To perform mercy with our fathers, and to remember his holy testament, the oath which he swore to Abraham our father, that he would grant to us, that being delivered from the hands of our enemies we may serve him without fear. » He had said that the Lord, according to the utterances of the prophets, was to be born in the house of David; he says that the same Lord was to deliver us for the fulfilling of the testament which he appointed for Abraham, because, namely, to these patriarchs especially there was promised, of their seed, either the gathering of the nations or the incarnation of Christ; which Matthew too wished briefly to intimate, who began the opening of his Gospel in this manner: « The book of the generation of Jesus Christ, the son of David, the son of Abraham. » Where it is to be noted that with each evangelist David is set before Abraham, because 1160Ceven if he is later in order of time, he is nevertheless greater in the gift of the promise. For to Abraham, who, being still set in uncircumcision, forsook his own goods, knew God, and deserved the testimony of faith, there was promised only the faith of the nations and the most sacred assembly of the Church, the Lord saying to him, « and in thee shall all the kindreds of the earth be blessed; » but David heard by a loftier oracle that Christ was to be born of him according to the flesh: « And when thou shalt have fulfilled thy days, he says, so as to go to thy fathers, I will raise up thy seed after thee, which shall be of thy sons, and I will establish his kingdom. He shall build me a house, and I will make his throne firm for ever. I will be to him a father, and he shall be to me a son (I Par. XVII). »

« In holiness and justice before him all our days 1160D. » Openly and briefly he shows in what way the Lord is to be served: namely both in holiness and justice, and before him, and all our days. For he who either departs from his service before death, and by any uncleanness or injustice stains the sincerity of his faith; or strives to endure holy and just before homnibus only, and not also before the Lord — he serves the Lord not yet perfectly freed from the hand of the spiritual enemies, but after the example of the old Samaritans he attempts to serve the gods of the nations and the Lord alike.

« And thou, child, shalt be called the Prophet of the Most High. For thou shalt go before the face of the Lord, to prepare his ways. » Beautifully, while he was speaking of the Lord, he suddenly turns his words to the Prophet 1161A, so that this too might be shown to be a benefit of the Lord. But it is asked how he addresses an infant of eight days? Yet by what reasoning should he who, still shut in the womb, heard the salutation of Mary, not have heard the voice of his father? — unless perhaps it is to be thought that Zechariah wished rather, for the sake of instructing those who were present, to preach as soon as he could speak the offices to come of his son, which he had learned long before through the angel. Let the Arians indeed hear and blush; let the meek hear and rejoice, that he calls Christ the Lord, whom John by prophesying went before, the Most High, according to that which the Psalmist, praising God and perfect man in one person, says: « The mother Sion shall say: a man, and a man is made in her, and the Most High himself hath founded her (Psal. LXXXVI), » [Vulg. numquid Sion dicet homo et homo natus est in ea, "Shall not Sion say: A man, and a man is born in her"] showing him to be made 1161Bwho founded her, the same man whom he names the Most High.

« To give knowledge of salvation to his people, unto the remission of their sins. » As though desiring to expound and more diligently to commend the name Jesus, which is the same as Saviour, he repeats mention of salvation, when he recalls that a horn of salvation is to be raised up, that salvation from our enemies is to come, that knowledge of salvation is to be given to the people; but lest thou shouldst think a temporal and fleshly salvation to be promised, « unto the remission, he says, of their sins. » But the Jews on this account prefer not to receive Jesus Christ, but rather to await Antichrist; because they desire to be saved not within but without, and to be freed not from the dominion of sin but from the yoke of human servitude.

« Through the bowels of the mercy of our God, in which 1161Cthe Orient from on high hath visited us. » And the prophet, speaking of the Lord: « Behold a man, he says, the Orient is his name (Zach. VI); » who is rightly called the Orient for this reason, that opening to us the rising of the true light he made the sons of night and of darkness sons of the light, according to what blessed Zechariah consequently sets forth, saying:

« To enlighten them that sit in darkness and in the shadow of death, to direct our feet into the way of peace. » For the Lord to enlighten them that sit in darkness and the shadow of death is to pour into those who have lived in sins and in the blindness of ignorance the rays of his own knowledge and love — to such as these the Apostle says (Ephes. V): « You were heretofore darkness, but now light in the Lord. » And our feet are directed into the way of peace when the journey of our actions agrees in all things with the grace of our Redeemer 1161Dand enlightener. And in a fitting order indeed he testifies that hearts are first to be enlightened and afterwards works to be directed, because no one can work the peace which he has not first learned. Whence rightly the Psalmist: « Seek, he says, peace and follow after it (Psal. XXXIII), » which is to say: be enlightened, thou who wast sitting in darkness, and enter the way of peace from which thou wast long an exile.

INCIPIT CANTICUM MARIAE Matris Domini.

Beatissima virgo Maria postquam se coelesti partu gravidam cognovit, nequaquam se de donis coelestibus, 1162Aquasi a se haec essent, extulit; sed ut magis magisque donis esset apta divinis, in custodiam humilitatis mentis suae gressum fixit, memor Scripturae praecipientis: « Quanto magnus es, humilia te in omnibus (Eccli. III), moxque ut angelus qui divinum ei partum nuntiabat ad coelestia rediit, surgit, ad montana conscendit, gestansque in utero Deum, servorum Dei habitacula petiit, ac requirit alloquia, in cujus adventu Elisabeth salutationem matris Domini audiens, mox de ea prophetavit et matrem Domini ad se venisse testata est, quam benedictam inter mulieres esse et benedictum fructum ventris ejus pronuntiavit, insuper et ad vocem ejus narravit infantem in utero suo exsultasse Spiritu sancto repletum. Haec quoque audiens Maria, non se jactanter 1162Binani gloria extulit, sed magis per humilitatem tota intentione animi gratias Deo retulit dicens:

« Magnificat anima mea Dominum, et exsultavit spiritus meus in Deo salutari meo. » Tanto, inquit, me Dominus, tamque inaudito munere sublimavit, quod non ullo linguae officio explicari, sed ipso vix intimo pectoris affectu valeat comprehendi, et ideo totas animae vires in agendis gratiarum laudibus offero, totum in contemplanda magnitudine ejus cui non est finis quidquid vivo, sentio, discerno, gratulanter impendo, quia et ejusdem Jesu, id est, salutaris, spiritus meus aeterna divinitate laetatur, cujus me caro temporali conceptione laetatur; cui simile est illud Psalmistae. « Anima autem mea exsultabit in Domino, et delectabitur super salutare ejus (Psal. 1162CXXXIV), » et ipse enim Patrem Filiumque pari venerabatur amore.

« Quia respexit humilitatem ancillae suae, ecce enim ex hoc beatam me dicent omnes generationes. » Cujus humilitas respicitur recte beata ab omnibus cognominanda gratulatur, sicut econtrario cujus superbia despecta condemnatur, Aevae, id est, vae sive calamitatis, nomine multata tabescit. Decebat enim ut sicut per superbiam primae nostrae parentis mors in mundum intravit, ita per humilitatem Mariae vitae introitus panderetur.

« Quia fecit mihi magna qui potens est, et sanctum nomen ejus. » Ad initium carminis respicit, ubi dictum est: « Magnificat anima mea Dominum: » sola quippe anima illa, cui Dominus magna facere 1162Ddignatur, dignis eum praeconiis magnificare, et ad consortes ejusdem voti ac propositi potest cohortando dicere: « Magnificate Dominum mecum, et exaltemus nomen ejus in invicem; » nam quicunque nostrum quem cognovit, quantum in se est, magnificare et nomen ejus sanctificare contempserit, minimus vocabitur in regno coelorum. Sanctum nomen ejus vocatur, qui singularis culmine potentiae transcendit omnem creaturam atque ab universis quae fecit longe segregatur, quod Graeca locutione melius intelligitur, in qua ipsum verbum, quod dicitur ἅγιον, quasi extra terram esse significat, cujus etiam nos imitatione pro modulo nostro segregari praecipimur ab omnibus qui non sunt sancti nec Deo dedicati, 1163Adicente Domino: » Sancti estote, quia et ego sanctus sum (I Pet. I). Quicunque enim se consecraverit, merito extra terram et extra mundum videbitur. Potest enim et ipse dicere, super terram ambulantes conversationem in coelis habemus.

« Et misericordia ejus a progenie in progenies timentibus eum. » A specialibus se donis ad generalia Dei judicia convertens, totius humani generis statum describit, et quid superbi, quid humiles mereantur, quid filii Adae per liberum arbitrium, quid filii Dei sint per gratiam, alternis versibus explicat. Non ergo, inquit, soli mihi fecit magna, qui potens est, sed et in omni gente et progenie, qui timet eum, et operatur justitiam, acceptus est illi.

« Fecit potentiam in brachio suo, dispersit superbos 1163Bmente cordis sui. » In brachio suo, in ipso Dei Filio significat, non quod Deus Pater figura determinetur carnis humanae, eique Filius tanquam membrum corporis haereat; sed quia omnia per ipsum facta sunt, ideo brachium Domini dictus est. Sicut enim tuum brachium per quod operaris, sic Dei brachium dictum est ejus Verbum, quia per Verbum operatus est mundum; cur enim homo brachium, ut aliquid operetur extendit? quia non continuo fit quod dixerit; si autem tanta potestate praevaleret, ut sine ullo motu corporis sui quod diceret fieret, brachium ejus verbum ejus esset. Dum ergo audivimus brachium Dei Patris esse Deum Filium, non nobis obstrepat consuetudo carnalis, sed quantum illo donante possumus, Dei virtutem et sapientiam 1163Ccogitemus, per quam facta sunt omnia.

« Deposuit potentes de sede, et exaltavit humiles, esurientes implevit bonis, et divites dimisit inanes. » In brachio ergo suo Dominus per omnes saeculi generationes potentes, hoc est, superbos de sede jactantiae suae deponit, et exaltat humiles, qui non sua potentia, sed in Dei bonitate confidunt, « quia omnis qui se exaltat, humiliabitur: et qui se humiliat, exaltabitur (Matth. XXIII). » Similiter et esurientes qui esuriunt et sitiunt justitiam aeternis bonis implet, et divites avaros et parcos inanes bonis operibus perire sinit.

« Suscepit Israel puerum suum memorari misericordiae suae. » Pulchre Israel puerum Domini appellat, qui ab eo sit ad salvandum susceptus, obedientem 1163Dvidelicet et humilem, juxta Oseae dictum: « Quia puer Israel et dilexi eum (Ose. XI); » nam qui contemnit humiliari, non potest utique salvari, nec dicere cum Propheta: « Ecce enim Deus adjuvat me, et Dominus susceptor est animae meae (Psal. LIII). Quicunque autem humiliaverit se, sicut parvulus, hic est major in regno coelorum (Matth. XVIII). »

« Sicut locutus est ad patres nostros Abraham et semini ejus in saecula. » Semen Abrahae non carnale, sed spiritale significat, id est, non ejus tantum carne progenitos, sed sive in circumcisione seu in praeputio fidei illius vestigia secutos: nam et ipse in praeputio positus credidit, reputatumque est ei ad justitiam, atque ejusdem fidei signaculum circumcisionem 1164Aaccepit, ut sic utriusque populi pater fieret per fidem, juxta quod Apostolus ad Romanos plenissime disseruit. Adventus ergo Salvatoris, Abrahae et semini ejus in saecula promissus est filiis promissionis, quibus dicitur: « Si autem vos Christi, ergo Abrahae semen estis, secundum promissionem haeredes (Galat. III). » Bene item vel Domini, vel Joannis exortum matres prophetando praeveniunt, ut sicut peccatum a mulieribus coepit, ita etiam bona a mulieribus incipiant, et quae per unius deceptionem perit, duabus certatim praeconantibus mundo vita reddatur.

HERE BEGINS THE CANTICLE OF MARY Mother of the Lord.

The most blessed virgin Mary, after she knew herself to be great with a heavenly birth, in no way lifted herself up over the heavenly gifts 1162Aas though these were from herself; but that she might be more and more fitted for the divine gifts, she fixed the step of her mind in the keeping of humility, mindful of the Scripture which enjoins: « The greater thou art, humble thyself in all things (Eccli. III), and as soon as the angel who announced to her the divine birth had returned to the heavenly places, she arises, goes up into the hill country, and, bearing God in her womb, she seeks the dwellings of the servants of God and asks for their converse. At her coming Elizabeth, hearing the salutation of the mother of the Lord, at once prophesied concerning her and bore witness that the mother of the Lord had come to her, and pronounced her blessed among women and the fruit of her womb blessed; and moreover she related that at her voice the infant in her own womb had leaped for joy, being filled with the Holy Spirit. Mary too, hearing these things, did not boastfully lift herself up 1162Bwith vain glory, but rather through humility, with the whole intention of her mind, rendered thanks to God, saying:

« My soul doth magnify the Lord, and my spirit hath rejoiced in God my saviour. » With so great a gift, she says, and one so unheard of, hath the Lord exalted me, that it cannot be unfolded by any service of the tongue, but can scarcely be grasped by the very inmost affection of the breast; and therefore I offer all the powers of my soul in the praises of thanksgiving; whatever I live, feel, discern, I spend rejoicingly and wholly upon the contemplating of his greatness, of whom there is no end, because my spirit is glad also in the eternal divinity of the same Jesus, that is, of the saviour, whose flesh rejoices me by a temporal conception; to which is like that saying of the Psalmist: « But my soul shall rejoice in the Lord, and shall be delighted over his salvation (Psal. 1162CXXXIV), » for he too venerated the Father and the Son with an equal love.

« Because he hath regarded the humility of his handmaid; for behold from henceforth all generations shall call me blessed. » She whose humility is regarded rightly rejoices to be named blessed by all, just as, on the contrary, she whose pride is despised is condemned, and, fined with the name of Eve (Aeva) — that is, of vae, woe, or of calamity — wastes away. For it was fitting that, just as through the pride of our first parent death entered into the world, so through the humility of Mary the entrance of life should be laid open.

« Because he that is mighty hath done great things to me, and holy is his name. » He looks back to the beginning of the song, where it was said, « My soul doth magnify the Lord »: for that soul alone, to which the Lord vouchsafes 1162Dto do great things, can magnify him with worthy proclamations, and can say by way of exhortation to the sharers of the same vow and purpose: « Magnify the Lord with me, and let us exalt his name one with another; » [Vulg. in id ipsum, "together, unto the same"] for whosoever of us shall have despised, so far as in him lies, to magnify him whom he has known and to hallow his name, shall be called least in the kingdom of heaven. His name is called holy, who by the height of a singular power transcends every creature and is set far apart from all the things he has made — which is better understood by the Greek expression, in which the very word which is said, ἅγιον, signifies as it were to be outside the earth; in imitation of whom we too, according to our measure, are commanded to be set apart from all who are not holy nor dedicated to God, 1163Athe Lord saying: » Be ye holy, because I also am holy (I Pet. I). For whosoever shall have consecrated himself will deservedly be seen to be outside the earth and outside the world. For he too can say: walking upon the earth, we have our conversation in the heavens.

« And his mercy from generation unto generations, to them that fear him. » [Vulg. in progenies et progenies, "unto generations and generations"] Turning himself from the special gifts to the general judgments of God, he describes the state of the whole human race, and sets forth in alternating verses what the proud and what the humble deserve, what the sons of Adam are through free will, what the sons of God are through grace. Not to me alone, then, he says, hath he that is mighty done great things, but in every nation and generation he that feareth him and worketh justice is acceptable to him.

« He hath shewed might in his arm; he hath scattered the proud 1163Bin the conceit of their heart. » By "in his arm" he signifies in the very Son of God — not that God the Father is bounded by the figure of human flesh, and that the Son cleaves to him as a member of the body; but because all things were made through him, therefore he is called the arm of the Lord. For just as thy arm is that through which thou workest, so the Word of God has been called the arm of God, because through the Word he wrought the world. For why does a man stretch out his arm in order to work anything? Because what he has said is not straightway done; but if he prevailed with so great a power that without any motion of his body what he said were done, his arm would be his word. When therefore we have heard that the arm of God the Father is God the Son, let not fleshly custom clamour against us, but, so far as we can by his gift, let us think upon the power and wisdom of God, 1163Cthrough which all things were made.

« He hath put down the mighty from their seat, and hath exalted the humble; he hath filled the hungry with good things, and the rich he hath sent empty away. » In his arm, then, the Lord throughout all the generations of the world puts down the mighty — that is, the proud — from the seat of their boasting, and exalts the humble, who trust not in their own power but in the goodness of God, « because every one that exalteth himself shall be humbled, and he that humbleth himself shall be exalted (Matth. XXIII). » In like manner he fills with eternal goods the hungry who hunger and thirst after justice, and suffers the rich, the greedy and the sparing, to perish empty of good works.

« He hath received Israel his servant, being mindful of his mercy. » Beautifully he calls Israel the servant of the Lord, as one taken up by him to be saved — obedient, 1163Dthat is, and humble, according to the saying of Osee: « Because Israel is a child, and I loved him (Ose. XI); » for he who scorns to be humbled cannot at all be saved, nor say with the Prophet: « For behold God helpeth me, and the Lord is the protector of my soul (Psal. LIII). And whosoever shall humble himself as a little child, he is the greater in the kingdom of heaven (Matth. XVIII). »

« As he spoke to our fathers, to Abraham and to his seed for ever. » He signifies the seed of Abraham not carnal but spiritual — that is, not only those begotten of his flesh, but those who, whether in circumcision or in uncircumcision, have followed the footsteps of his faith; for he himself also, being set in uncircumcision, believed, and it was reputed to him unto justice, and he received circumcision as the seal of that same faith, 1164Athat so he might become the father of both peoples through faith, according to what the Apostle most fully set forth to the Romans. The coming, therefore, of the Saviour, promised to Abraham and to his seed for ever, is promised to the sons of the promise, to whom it is said: « And if you be Christ's, then are you the seed of Abraham, heirs according to the promise (Galat. III). » Well too do the mothers by prophesying go before the rising either of the Lord or of John, so that, as sin began from women, so also good things should begin from women; and that life, which perished through the deceiving of one, should be given back to the world by two vying with each other in proclaiming it.

INCIPIT BENEDICTIO SIMEONIS PROPHETAE.
