1164BNarrat evangelista Lucas quod, cum parentes Jesu Domini nostri ipsum puerum inducerent in Hierusalem ad templum Domini, ut facerent secundum legis pro eo, et offerrent hostias secundum quod scriptum est in lege Domini, acceperit eum justus Simeon in ulnas suas, et benedixerit illum, prophetizans de ipso. Magna quidem Domini potentia, sed non minor claret humilitas, ut qui coelo terraque non capitur grandaevi hominis gestetur totus in ulnis; sed et tropice accipit Simeon Christum, veteranus infantem, ut doceat nos exuere veterem hominem, qui corrumpitur cum actibus ejus, et renovato spiritu mentis nostrae induere eum qui secundum Deum creatus est in justitia, et sanctitate, 1164Cet veritate, hoc est, deponentes mendacium, loqui veritatem, et caetera quae ad novi hominis statum pertinent ore, corde et opere, perficere. Accipit senior justus secundum legem et timoratus puerum Jesum in ulnas suas, ut significet justitiam operum, quae ex lege erant, quis enim nesciat opera per manus et brachia solere figurari. Humili quidem salutaris fidei evangelicae gratia mutanda accipit senior infantem Christum, ut insinuet hoc saeculum quasi senio jam et longaeva aetate defussum ad innocentiam, et, ut ita dixerim, infantiam Christianae conversationis rediturum, et sicut aquilae juventutem illius esse renovandam.

« Et benedixit Deum et dixit: Nunc dimittis servum tuum, Domine, secundum verbum tuum in pace. » 1164DVides non solum Novi, sed et Veteris Testamenti, justos spe futurae vitae desiderium habuisse dissolvi a corpore, imo pacis viam deputasse, sarcinam deponere terrestrem, ut pote qui se in sinu Abrahae requiem non dubitarent habituros esse perpetuam Denique et Idithun, hoc est, transiliator cupidita tum saecularium, postquam multa diu tacitus mundi mala contemplatus interna secum cordis meditatione concaluit, tandem locutus in lingua sua, et quae intus egerit promens. « Notum, inquit, fac, Domine, finem meum, et numerum dierum meorum, qui est, ut sciam quid desit mihi; ecce veteres posuisti dies meos (Psal. XXXVIII). » Quibus absque dubio verbis aperit, quam maximum se praesentium calamitatum 1165Anacturum speret in fine solatium, quem tantisper advenire desiderat.

« Quia viderunt oculi mei salutare tuum, quod parasti ante faciem omnium populorum. » Beati oculi qui vident quae Simeon vidit. « Beati qui non viderunt et crediderunt. » Illud ipsum, inquit, quod omnibus postmodum gentibus, populis, et linguis, mente ac fide conspiciendum parasti, spe ac dilectione quaerendum praevidisti, ipse desideratum nunc et carnis et cordis oculis tuum salutare contemplor.

« Lumen ad revelationem gentium, et gloriam plebis tuae Israel. » Lumen quidem utrique populo salutare Dei, id est, Christus a Deo Patre paratus, 1166Aqui tamen gloria magis Israel, cui diu speratur, et ex quo praenuntiatus advenit. Gentium vero dicitur esse revelatio, quarum mentis oculos profunda jam caecitate demersos, neque ulla spe adventus Dominici erectos ipse visitare pariter revelare atque illustrare dignatus est. Et bene revelatio gentium Israelis gloriae praefertur, qui « cum plenitudo gentium introierit, tunc omnis Israel salvus fiet, » quomodo et Psalmista cum diceret: « Notum fecit Dominus salutare suum ante conspectum gentium revelavit justitiam suam (Psal. XCVII), » subjunxit, atque ait, « Memor fuit misericordiae suae Jacob et veritatis suae domui Israel. »

HERE BEGINS THE BLESSING OF SIMEON THE PROPHET.

1164BThe evangelist Luke relates that, when the parents of Jesus our Lord brought the child himself into Jerusalem to the temple of the Lord, to do for him according to the [custom] of the law, and to offer sacrifices according to what is written in the law of the Lord, the just Simeon received him into his arms and blessed him, prophesying concerning him. Great indeed is the power of the Lord, but no less does his humility shine forth, that he who is not contained by heaven and earth should be borne whole in the arms of an aged man. But Simeon also receives Christ figuratively, an old man an infant, that he may teach us to put off the old man, who is corrupted with his deeds, and, our mind's spirit being renewed, to put on him who according to God is created in justice and holiness 1164Cand truth — that is, putting away lying, to speak the truth, and to fulfil with mouth, heart and work the other things that pertain to the state of the new man. The elder, just according to the law and God-fearing, receives the child Jesus into his arms, that he may signify the justice of the works which were of the law; for who does not know that works are wont to be figured by the hands and the arms. The elder receives the infant Christ, this world being to be changed by the humble grace of the saving faith of the Gospel, that he may intimate that this age, as it were now wearied defussum with old age and length of years, will return to innocence and — if I may so speak — to the infancy of the Christian manner of life, and that its youth is to be renewed like the eagle's.

« And he blessed God and said: Now thou dost dismiss thy servant, O Lord, according to thy word in peace. » 1164DThou seest that the just, not only of the New but also of the Old Testament, had in the hope of the life to come a desire to be dissolved from the body — nay rather, that they reckoned it the way of peace to lay down the earthly burden, inasmuch as they doubted not that they would have perpetual rest in the bosom of Abraham Finally Idithun also — that is, the overleaper of worldly cupidita tum — after he had long in silence contemplated the many evils of the world and had grown warm within himself by the inward meditation of his heart, spoke at last in his own tongue and brought forth what he had done within: « Make known to me, he says, O Lord, my end, and the number of my days, which is, that I may know what is wanting to me; behold thou hast made my days old (Psal. XXXVIII). » [Vulg. mensurabiles, "measurable"] By which words he opens without doubt how great a solace of present calamities 1165Ahe hopes to obtain at the end, which meanwhile he desires to come.

« Because my eyes have seen thy salvation, which thou hast prepared before the face of all peoples. » Blessed are the eyes that see what Simeon saw. « Blessed are they that have not seen and have believed. » That very thing, he says, which thou hast prepared to be beheld by mind and by faith by all nations, peoples and tongues hereafter, which thou hast foreseen to be sought by hope and by love — that same salvation of thine, now desired, I behold with the eyes both of flesh and of heart.

« A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, and the glory of thy people Israel. » A light indeed to each people is the salvation of God — that is, Christ, prepared by God the Father — 1166Awho nevertheless is rather the glory of Israel, by whom he is long hoped for and out of whom, being foretold, he comes. But he is said to be the revelation of the Gentiles, whose eyes of mind, already sunk in deep blindness and raised up by no hope of the Lord's coming, he himself vouchsafed to visit and likewise to reveal himself to and to enlighten. And rightly is the revelation of the Gentiles set before the glory of Israel, since « when the fullness of the Gentiles shall have come in, then all Israel shall be saved; » just as the Psalmist also, when he had said, « The Lord hath made known his salvation; before the sight of the Gentiles he hath revealed his justice (Psal. XCVII), » subjoined and said, « He hath been mindful of his mercy to Jacob and of his truth toward the house of Israel. » [Vulg. recordatus est misericordiae suae et veritatis suae domui Israel, without Jacob]

Cruces for this work — where the plate is defective or the reading uncertain →

The Latin is Migne's printing of Commentaria in Cantica (PL 112, coll. 1089–1166A), by Rabanus Maurus, from the Corpus Corporum transcription. Parenthetical references in small type stand on Migne's page — editorial identifications inherited from the edition he reprinted or supplied by his shop, not necessarily the author's own; the author's own citations run in the prose itself. Every gilt column mark is an address: migne.app/pl/112/1089 resolves to the first.
